commit d2bab6122009cc0946697a8658d09a6554ff43c4
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-06T15:28:22+02:00
Refs:   

perf(engine): cache the biome/feature library scan, drop it on commit

Preflight reads biome records once per painted cell (task-716):
cell_kind, merge_rule and is_building are called from the per-cell
identity and merge passes, and each call re-scanned all 106 biome JSON
files from disk. A 160x100 grid took ~7 minutes for a grid read that
should take a second -- 2.3 million file opens for one GET /grid.

Cache the parsed directory for the life of the process (the library
dirs are static), and drop the cache in the WorldPainter commit path:
every mutation funnels through _commit, so it is the right place to
notice that a hand edit to a biome JSON should be visible without a
server restart. The taxonomy file itself has no write path (task-589).

Verified: second biomes() read returns the same cached object
(106 entries); clear_cache() drops it and re-reads fresh.

commit 9bf0b44a976900fcb8b3384b85fb7615437cea0d
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-06T15:27:52+02:00
Refs:   

fix(worldpainter): brush hover outline renders; checklist below the map

The brush hover preview was drawn at the end of _drawRoute, below the
route early-return, so with no route in progress (every moment except
a route drag) the outline was dead code: the mousemove handler set
state.brushHover and redrew the decor layer, but the draw never
reached the canvas context. Move the preview above the return
(task-719).

The what-next checklist also moves from above the map to below it:
the canvas is the thing being looked at, and a collapsed progress
bar wedged between the toolbar and the art was the only thing
standing between an author and their paint.

Verified live: 0 outline pixels at rest, ~2900 dashed pixels on
hover; the outline box tracks the pointer across the grid and a 3x3
brush at the top row correctly draws 3x2 (edge-clipped); contrast
4.4-6.6 against map content. Paint and erase share the path.

commit 35df052f510493868ccc88f88b8e5bb2ed8bb443
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-05T21:38:01+02:00
Refs:   

fix(editor): preserve chat transcript across Apply commits

Applying staged mutations used to call resetSession(), wiping the
message history and chat transcript on every commit. Replace that
with AgentLoop.afterApply, which rebuilds the system prompt against
the post-Apply world and appends a commit note recording what landed
(and which ops remain staged), so the model stays truthful without
losing the conversation. The UI draws a divider chip instead of
resetting, and apply/applySelected route through the new hook.

Cover the regression with unit tests asserting the conversation
survives, pending ops are reported, and the system prompt refreshes.

Also refresh character library records (newline normalization,
inventory indentation, james.json recast as the fisherman), extend
kraktooth camp loot tables, and file tasks 714-716 for the mind
panel, live-update transport, and fisherman vertical slice.

commit d3189648bee0dcce57860c47ac64eca3821de278
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-05T01:08:58+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): scale node size and apply separation tuning live

Adds a graph_node_scale multiplier on the drawn size of item/way/character nodes
(areas size to their name and are unaffected), persisted to storage. The
separation pass reads the same value so larger nodes get correspondingly more
room.

The guard's knobs - strength, range, pull - and node size only took effect when
the pass ran, and it was reachable solely through apply(), i.e. via a full layout
re-derivation on a stabilization event. Tuning them live did nothing until the
next rebuild. resolveSeparation() re-runs the overlap guard in place with no
ring re-seed and no teleport, so a slider tick shows its effect immediately.

A displaced stray is now reeled toward its holder's *current* position, which is
what the knob's hint promises, rather than onto a fresh ring slot.

commit 755d2db876d3f3c1cbccc0febb5987744c87b210
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-05T01:08:26+02:00
Refs:   

fix(world-sync): implement refresh(), which four call sites already called

Four call sites (inspector/area-view, graph-manager, structures, worldpainter)
call worldSync.refresh() after a world edit. The method never existed: one caller
was unguarded, which is how a live run produced "Scope change failed:
window.worldSync.refresh is not a function" after a scope change that had already
succeeded, and the other three were silently doing nothing.

Re-reads the library and re-renders without opening the modal. bug-522.

commit 43a48fa6915f24d1142dfa0e7e435c4c05405fb5
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-05T01:08:20+02:00
Refs:   

feat(plan): ground the typed plan contract and salvage truncated steps

task-700. The plan phase now emits typed steps validated against world facts,
with one bounded re-composition: rejected steps return their failed preconditions
(to the LLM once) and whatever still does not ground is dropped, never executed.

Adds plan-grounding.ts, a pure fact-side validator kept separate from the prompt
builder so the contract can be tested without an LLM.

Also fixes a truncation failure found in a live run (kraktooth, Rikka): the
response was cut off mid-object at max_tokens and the whole plan was lost, so the
character acted with no plan at all. _salvageTypedSteps now pulls the complete
step objects out of a half-written {"steps":[...]} instead of discarding
everything, and the prompt budget accounts for the typed re-composition being
larger than the original prose request.

commit ba2a397cc515494692906322ea61edd5b3bbf3be
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-05T01:08:07+02:00
Refs:   

fix(expression-pack): make the 30-expression vocabulary the single source of truth

The "generate expression pack" prompt emitted a stale 11-name list padded out to
a 40-item `slot13..slot40` suggestion, and the sprite-sheet splitter labelled
tiles with the same drifted vocabulary - position 5 read `afraid`, positions 8-12
were wrong outright. Both now read one shared list.

Root cause was four separate copies of the expression order that had drifted
apart:

- inspector/helpers.ts EXPRESSION_ORDER - the real one, now `neutral` + the 30
  authored expressions (31 tiles), with an icon per key
- inspector/agent-view.ts - carried a stale literal AND a hardcoded
  `slot13..slot40` array; both deleted, it reads EXPRESSION_ORDER now
- inspector/sprite-sheet.ts EMOTION_ORDER and character-art.ts CANONICAL - module
  fallbacks for when InspectorHelpers has not loaded yet, realigned

Splitter overflow past the 31st tile is now blank rather than a `slotN`
placeholder: the Names textarea's own contract is "blank = skip", so a sheet
wider than the authored vocabulary does not upload the extra tiles instead of
inventing names for art nobody specified. Verified at widths 12/31/33/40/60 -
zero `slotN` at any of them.

The authored art vocabulary and the engine's face vocabulary are two different
sets and conflating them was the trap. engine/emotion.py:AXIS_TO_EXPRESSION picks
a face from the 11 affect axes and emits keys of its own (afraid, ashamed, ...),
and character-art.ts:emotionKeyFor returns that key unconditionally - so swapping
the list alone would have left a frightened character with no portrait, silently
fallback to neutral. EXPRESSION_ART_ALIASES joins the two (afraid -> fearful,
ashamed -> embarrassed), applied only after a direct slot lookup misses so packs
already storing the engine's names render unchanged. Engine keys with no authored
counterpart (aroused, affectionate, envious, calm) deliberately fall back to
neutral rather than borrowing another expression's face.

Tests: the two sprite-sheet tests are re-anchored to the 31-name order and assert
overflow is blank; a new one pins the splitter's copy to EXPRESSION_ORDER so it
cannot drift again; three new character-art tests cover the alias mechanism
(engine key resolves to art, a direct slot still wins, an unmapped key goes
neutral).

Verified live against an authored 31-tile sheet: the prompt emits exactly the 30
in order ending `28. serious, 29. tearful, 30. thoughtful`, and the splitter
labels land on the matching captioned panels (neutral on NEUTRAL, fearful on
FEARFUL, embarrassed on EMBARRASSED, thoughtful on THOUGHTFUL).

commit eed00a72f9190b9b40a240fa284d88c168a56499
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-04T16:08:24+02:00
Refs:   

feat(memory): add dynamic reinforcement, decay, and reflection

Add activation-based forgetting with per-memory resistance and
configurable decay. Reinforce memories on recall and re-encounter while
preserving authored importance.

Track categories, confidence, consolidation, and contradiction links.
Apply these signals in multi-channel retrieval.

Generate structured, provenance-aware reflections with recursion guards
and optional belief, emotional, behavioral, and relationship effects.

fix(ui): stop the behavior graph silently destroying action params (task-388 Phase 2+3)

Two silent data-loss defects in the behavior graph editor, both reproduced in the

the form editor's catalog: 110 of 121 action types (124 of 149 params) were

followed and any other branch vanished. That one is an editor bug, not a model
limit: engine/triggers/behaviors.py:45 is 'for action in actions:', a flat
action list, so the engine runs fan-out natively.

commit f11d71cde63b488be19b88a47c2ee6d36d24b425
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T19:15:00+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(ts): clear the type-collision cascade (245 -> 19 errors)

The 245 errors were not 245 problems. They traced to four structural faults,
each fixed at the cause rather than site by site.

1. File-local types sharing global names. Every top-level `interface` in a
   classic script is GLOBAL, and 35 names were declared in 2+ files with
   DIFFERENT shapes per file. Two same-named types with different shapes are not
   the same type, so sharing a name is simply wrong. Renamed per file; the file
   using a name most keeps the original so readers still resolve. This alone
   cleared 162 errors (TS2687 / TS2717 / TS2374), which were the least
   diagnosable of the lot: they report a PROPERTY name - "all declarations of
   'id' must have identical modifiers" - and never hint that six files each
   declared their own GraphNode.

2. A second duplicate in the SAME file (ApiClientWithSpeak appeared twice in
   room-context.ts), which a cross-file pass cannot see because one of the two
   declarations is nested inside an IIFE. Removed the redundant copy.

3. Ambient shapes hand-written from the wrong file. GraphNetwork's declaration
   listed four members belonging to a different module while the real API has
   64, so every caller of the real API failed on a type describing something
   else. Regenerated from network-manager.js by tools/ambient_from_js.py. Same
   for ItemLibraryPlacement, where a local `const` alias in library-browser.ts
   both redeclared the global (TS2451) and gave item-library.ts a narrower type
   than the real API - deleting the alias fixed 9 errors at once.

4. tools/hoist_declares.py treating a Window member as equivalent to a bare
   `declare const`. It is not: `window.X` and bare `X` are different lookups, so
   deleting a bare declaration because "a declaration exists" on the Window
   interface leaves the bare name undeclared.

19 errors remain, in 6 files, all genuine annotation work. Zero emitted .js
changed throughout - every one of these is type-level.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

refactor(ts): give file-local types unique names (245 -> 83 errors)

Every top-level `interface` in a classic script is GLOBAL. 35 names were
declared across the converted files, so they all merged - and they do not merge
cleanly, because the shapes differ per file. That produced 94 errors of

name still resolves. 28 files renamed, 35 collisions resolved.

commit 1e44c3b875934dbb6f868d028e3987879693bf24
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T19:04:34+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(ts): derive cross-module ambient shapes from the .js definitions

372 -> 245 project errors across 39 files.

GraphNetwork alone was 82 errors and 34 missing members. The declaration in
globals.d.ts had been hoisted from the WRONG file and listed four members that
belong to a different module (revealItemsForNode, hideRevealedAreas are
visibility calls, not network-manager API), so every caller of the real API
failed on a type that described something else.

tools/ambient_from_js.py reads \window.X = { ... }\ out of the .js owner and
emits a declaration with that object's real top-level members - 64 for
GraphNetwork, derived rather than guessed. Argument and return types stay any:
getting the NAMES right is what callers need, and narrowing them is real work
that belongs with converting the module.

An important gotcha this had to work around: an ambient \declare const X: any\
in globals.d.ts does NOT help once the owner is converted, because a real
declaration always beats an ambient one. Callers see the real, narrow inferred
type, which lists only the members that file itself uses. That is why the fix
has to live in the owner, not the hub.

Two of my own bugs while building the generator, both caught by the dry run
before any write: the member-depth test ran after counting the line's own brace
so every method was dropped, and \sync init() {\ failed the member regex on the
async prefix. Both fixed; the dry run now reports 64 methods.

commit 9892c0344274722c8104fdf4f99fac21ee54baab
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T18:46:28+02:00
Refs:   

fix(js): clear the redeclaration SyntaxErrors that killed two whole files

item-library.js died at parse time with 'redeclaration of const TriggerTypes',
taking the entire file with it - a top-level const in a classic script is a
global lexical binding, so two files claiming a name is a parse-time error, not
a scoped one. library-browser.js had the same collision on itemLib.

Both offenders were lane-introduced aliases that shadowed a real global:
item-library read window.TriggerTypes into a local const named TriggerTypes,
library-browser read window.itemLib into one named itemLib. Renamed the local
bindings to TriggerTypesNs and itemLibRef.

Renaming had to skip the property access, or the window lookup itself breaks, so
it was done with word-boundary matching that excludes .TriggerTypes and the
object-literal key - a plain replaceAll would have silently rewritten the lookup
to read the wrong global.

Verified in a browser rather than by regex alone: 0 console errors,
TriggerTypes present with 3 keys, event stream rendering. Duplicate top-level
bindings across all 158 emitted files: 0.

commit e7c54eba9812f97bfb751e82958c9ff83751e3e1
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T18:43:59+02:00

refactor(js): convert 94 more front-end modules to TypeScript (WIP)

Six parallel conversion lanes took static/js from 42 to 136 TypeScript files.
This commit lands their output so it is not lost. It is deliberately labelled
WIP: the per-file builds all pass and every .ts has its .js, so the front end
loads, but `npm run build:ts` reports 375 cross-file errors that the next
commit reconciles.

Those 375 are all project-wide, not per-file:

- TS2451 redeclarations - lanes declared a top-level `const` locally because
  globals.d.ts was off-limits to them, so two files now claim the same name
- TS2687/TS2717/TS2374 - interface-merge conflicts in contextual-actions.ts
- TS2304/TS2339 - globals the hub does not declare yet (TriggerTypes,
  TriggerEditor, and ~90 real ApiClient methods against the 8 in the shim)

The per-file isolation in tools/ts_convert.py is why none of this reached a
browser: `build-one` type-checks a file against itself plus globals.d.ts, which
is sound for classic scripts but cannot see cross-file collisions by
construction. That was the right trade to stop six lanes 404-ing each other,
and the wrong shape for integration. A whole-project check belongs alongside it,
run after each lane reports rather than once at the end.

Two behavioural regressions were found and fixed during integration, both
caused by making a type error disappear at the cost of runtime behaviour:

- agent/action-normalizer.js rewrote `window.config` to bare `config`, turning
  an undefined-safe guard into a ReferenceError. Proven by A/B: clean HEAD ran
  `522 passed, 0 failed`, the worktree ran `521 passed, 1 failed`. Fixed.
- A top-level `const X = (() => {...})(); window.X = X` pattern is a GLOBAL
  LEXICAL BINDING, so two files using the same name is a parse-time SyntaxError
  that kills the whole file. Three hit the browser. Fixed, and
  ts_convert.py now checks emitted .js for it.

Also fixed: three files were left mid-conversion with no .js, so the front end
was 404ing on main.js, item-library.js and inspector.js. main and item-library
were finished (1 and 16 errors); inspector.ts was reverted to .js because its 36
errors depend on hub declarations owed here.

screenshots, and their task files.

build(js): pin lit-html 3.2.1 for types and convert lit-bootstrap

shared/lit-bootstrap.js was the one structurally-blocked file: it is the only ES
module in static/js, it imports from eight `lit-html` specifiers, the vendored

blocks, because globals.d.ts already carried a hand-written `LitApi` asserting
what lit's API is. That assertion is now checkable against the real thing
instead of being maintained from memory, which is the difference between a
derived type and a fiction.

Version skew, caught and corrected: `npm i -D lit@3.2.1` resolved to
lit-html 3.3.3, because the `lit` metapackage takes a caret range on its
lit-html dependency. Types from 3.3.3 would have described a different lit than
the one the vendored bundle runs - confirmed by reading 3.2.1 out of
static/js/vendor/lit-html/lit-html.js. Dropped the `lit` metapackage entirely
(lit-bootstrap imports only from `lit-html`) and pinned `lit-html: "3.2.1"`
exact, so a future install cannot drift. AGENTS.md's package-lock `name`-field
rewrite trap did not trigger - verified absent in the diff.

No runtime change: the browser still loads the vendored bundle; node_modules is
dev-only.

All eight specifiers resolve, so the file converts. The two repo-local helpers
(renderPanel, renderInto) take `TemplateResult` imported from lit-html itself.
Verified the emitted ES module keeps all 8 imports and both exports and the
`window.Lit` assignment - a stripped import would break the app at runtime.

This was the last file blocked on types. 137 of 158 converted.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 02d9e7ed3993c4d98b69ac88b59452d4303e5976
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T18:30:33+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(js): remove the temptation that broke test_fear_verbs

A converter silenced `Property 'config' does not exist on type 'Window'` by
rewriting `window.config` to bare `config`. That made the type check pass and
the runtime fail: `window.X` is undefined-safe, bare `X` throws ReferenceError
when the module has not loaded. Proven ours by A/B - clean HEAD runs
`522 passed, 0 failed`, the worktree ran `521 passed, 1 failed`.

Fixing 80 sites with local casts would have left the trap in place for the next
converter, so this removes the cause instead.

- tools/window_members.py generates `interface Window` from evidence: 117 names
  that modules actually assign via `window.X =`. With those members present
  `window.config.maxTokens` compiles, so the correct spelling is also the easy
  one. `any` rather than `unknown`, deliberately: `unknown` would make every
  property access through a window member error and recreate the temptation.
  Names that are only lexical top-level `const` are excluded, because
  `window.X` would typecheck for them while being undefined at runtime.

- ts_convert.py gains `baseline` and `audit-window`, and `check` now runs the
  audit as a step. It records each converted file's pre-conversion
  `window.NAME` usage counts at conversion time, because once the conversion is
  committed the original .js is gone from HEAD and the baseline could no longer
  be re-derived. Current state: 136 files baselined, 52 dropped prefixes
  flagged for review. Reported rather than blanket-failed - a drop is benign
  when the name is a binding the file itself declares, and adding defensive
  casts where none are needed is its own damage.

globals.d.ts is a hub and was edited while lanes are mid-flight, deliberately:
every added member can only remove type errors, never add one, and leaving it
incomplete is what invited the regression.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 3e7f8dbe9067dc710f8997b2bab31748af12e0aa
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T17:14:31+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(js): make per-file conversion safe for parallel lanes

tsc -p tsconfig.json compiles and emits every .ts in the project with
noEmitOnError, so one lane's mid-conversion file fails the build for every
other lane and concurrent runs race on the same emit. That is the blocker for
converting the remaining 133 files with more than one agent.

Converted files share no imports - they are classic scripts whose cross-file
symbols are ambient in globals.d.ts - so a single file's typecheck depends only
on itself plus that declaration file. Two new subcommands exploit that:

- build-one PATH   type-check and emit ONE file, independent of the project
- verify-emit      prove per-file emit reproduces the project emit

verify-emit deletes the .js first and requires it to reappear byte-identical.
Comparing a rebuilt file against itself proves nothing: the first version of
this check did that and reported 25/25 while tsc was actually writing to
static/js/shared/shared/, leaving the real .js stale. That also explains the
stray nested agent/ graph/ inspector/ shared/ soak/ stream/ ui/ directories,
which held duplicate .js copies that the typecheck then picked up - producing
a bogus "Cannot redeclare block-scoped variable 'ScenarioStatus'". Both fixed;
no stray directories remain.

convert --apply now emits per-file instead of running the project build, so it
is safe to run concurrently. The invariant this preserves: a file is only ever
either an untouched .js, or a .ts whose .js has already been regenerated - so
templates/index.html never 404s on a script.

globals.d.ts gains provisional declarations for 28 singletons owned by modules
that are still .js (InspectorHelpers, GraphToolbar, DiffModal, tippy, vis and
the rest), so parallel lanes never need to edit this hub file - concurrent
edits to one file lose declarations. Each records its owning module so the real
shape can replace it when that module converts.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

refactor(js): convert 20 front-end modules to TypeScript

Converts the cheapest tier of static/js (20 files, 0-15 type errors each) from
.js to .ts. Each conversion renames, adds real annotations, and keeps
npm run build:ts green so the emitted .js stays on the path
templates/index.html loads.

Also adds tools/ts_convert.py, which automates the mechanical half of the
migration documented in docs/design/typescript-migration.md: report / list /
plan / convert / check / sweep. It measures readiness against tsconfig.json's
strict settings, because tsconfig.check.json is lenient and a lenient probe
reports files as ready that then fail noEmitOnError on rename.

Three defects surfaced and are fixed here:

- tsc drops a file's leading JSDoc when the first statement is type-only, so
  the emitted .js loses the @module header js_module_index.py reads. Five files
  hit it; their type declarations now sit below the first value declaration,
  and ts_convert.py fails the build if a header does not survive emit.
- config.js declares `const config = new ConfigManager()` at top level, so in
  tsconfig.check.json it shadows the globals.d.ts declaration that
  tsconfig.json depends on. controllingPlayer is declared on both sides.
- Window lacked worldState/VW/Lit/SoakUI/SoakState, so every `window.X` access
  failed. window.Lit is now declared as LitApi (all 14 members, per W0 of the
  migration plan), which unblocks the 46 templated files.

Gate: build:ts, typecheck, lint, js_module_index --check, unit, header survival
all green. Verified live in a browser on / and /soak with 0 console errors.

This also repairs HEAD: 6fd774b8 swept two of these files in mid-conversion,
leaving threat-detector.js and vital-color.js absent from the tree and the
build failing with 14 errors.

commit 3025a5fc775b59cd06f5afc3a745a52d349469fe
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T11:44:37+02:00
Refs:   

feat(vitals): one resolver for the ceiling, hit dice, and one death path (task-538)

The engine had no health model; it had a 100. Max_HP was the only vital with a
maximum, and that maximum's DEFAULT was the literal 100 written out in about ten
places. The literal was harmless only because Max_HP was itself always 100, and
that is what hid two live bugs:

  - heal clamped to 100, so healing a character by 5 could take it above its own
    maximum. data/library/characters/fluffy.json - a sheep - has shipped with
    Max_HP: 8 for months, so this was a bug in shipped data, not a
    hypothetical: one heal spell produced 13 HP on an 8-HP animal.
  - the regen gate read 'HP < 100', i.e. 'HP < maximum', so a character whose
    maximum is 7 regenerated every turn and could never be finished off.

engine.vitals.ceiling() is now the only place the rule lives, and every site
asks it: player.py, engine/effects.py, both deserialisers, both vital effect
handlers, vital_rates.change, engine/combat.py, engine/traits.py (the 'scarred'
threshold was unreachable for small stat blocks for the same reason),
engine/tick_manager.py and routes/player_ops.py. It never silently returns 100
for HP, follows the Max_{vital} convention generally, and returns infinity for
Temperature, which is anatomical and band-driven rather than a percentage.

Also:

  - hit_dice: '2d6' -> 7, '7d8+14' -> 46, resolved at load time by
    engine.vitals.hit_dice_max. Average (half-up) by default because a bestiary
    number must not change every time a save loads; 'roll' for a character that
    rolls up. An unreadable expression resolves to nothing rather than to 0 HP.
    An explicit Max_HP WINS over the formula, which is what keeps all 70 library
    characters untouched.
  - set_vital and modify_vital_max effects (task-537 section M), registered in
    EFFECT_TYPES and SAFE_EFFECT_TYPES, with library templates. modify_vital_max
    moves the ceiling and leaves the current value alone by default, so a spell
    that lifts your maximum does not silently heal you.
  - ONE death path. world.kill_player(name, cause) zeroes HP, applies the dead
    condition, writes the lived-log death record, drops held items and spawns
    the body; it returns False for an already-dead character so a second lethal
    check cannot spawn a second corpse. Four sites each set state='dead' and did
    a different subset of that, so a corpse's inventory survived a killing blow
    but not a script. The authoritative death state is the 'dead' CONDITION -
    Player.state is already derived from it, so state and condition were never
    two stores.

Two bugs found while closing this: the shipped 8-HP sheep above, and a
swallowed AttributeError in the new kill path (player_manager has no
time_ticks; the clock is on the world), which meant the death record the
function existed to write was silently never written.

Tests: tests/test_health_model.py - 43 passed. Health/traits/combat/skill/
condition sweep - 236 passed. Full suite compared BY FAILURE NAME against a
clean master worktree: 15 failed / 6666 passed on both, Compare-Object empty.
docs/virtualWorld/Characters/Vitals System.md documents the model, the three
scales, the authoring surface and the death decision.

commit 4d22ffb00c02747936fdcfdb492cc80896fa02ef
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T11:41:29+02:00
Refs:   

fix(review): stop fabricating damage_type, and guard the data tools

Addresses the review of the items-library worktree.

WARNING (combat regression): the shared materializer defaulted a missing
`damage_type` to "bludgeoning". Combat gates typed resistance on a truthy
`damage_type` (engine/combat.py:302), so every previously-untyped weapon became
subject to bludgeoning resistance. The default is now "" (combat's own
"no type" sentinel) in engine/library_nodes.py and _refresh_item; declared
types are still carried.

Tooling guards:
- tools/gen_library_items.py anchors ITEM_DIR to the repo root, stamps emitted
  templates with `_generated_by`, refuses to overwrite a file that lacks it
  (pass --force to override), and fails --check when the item dir is missing.
- tools/backfill_item_affinity.py anchors ITEM_DIR and fails --check/--report
  when the directory is missing or empty (previously a vacuous "OK" from the
  wrong cwd).
- Drops the no-op RESOURCE_TAGS union (the three extras were already keys).

Test coverage: test_character_loadout_check now exercises the template-aware
branch with a dict of templates (library-id equipped into an undeclared slot is
flagged; a library-id entry without inline properties is not a
missing-properties warning).

commit 400f330d86aa0918921cb88ff53b9361bbec407f
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T10:43:17+02:00
Refs:   

fix(equipment): prune equipped ids whose item node was deleted (task-654)

The inverse divergence, found while closing task-654: DELETE
/api/graph/node/<id> removes the node and its edges, but player.equipped is not
part of the graph, so the id survived the node.

    equipped: { feet: [heavy_black_boots, heavy_black_boots_061fbea7], ... }

Nothing reconciles the structures in either direction. The stale id is then
worn in the equipment readout, and body_parts.is_exposed degrades a missing
node to 'this region is uncovered' - so it silently changes coverage maths
instead of raising.

EquipmentSystem.prune_dangling_equipped / prune_all_dangling_equipped drop
those ids (and their edges) and leave markers and live items alone; the node
delete route calls it for item nodes. The task file also records the decisions,
the library-loader import bug found on the way, and why the declared-slot vs
used-slot disagreement is three library items rather than the engine.

tests/test_equipment_api_edges.py: 12 passed.

feat(items): item provenance and acquisition history (task-514)

Carried gear can now say where it came from, so the matrix's "how they got it"
column is real: Mikka's wrench stolen from a human wagon, Gribba's apron taken
from an abandoned farm, Vekka's mirror traded from a scout.

- engine/items/provenance.py: one shape on the INSTANCE node's properties,
  {"text", "source", "area", "tick"}, normalized from a plain string or a dict,
  rendered as one bounded line (160 chars).
- A library template may declare a default `provenance`; the shared
  materializer copies it onto the instance (library_nodes), and it survives
  place/refresh/graph authoring.
- Set on acquisition: the `give_item` effect (params.provenance / params.source)
  and `steal` (rewrites the story to record the theft). Taking from a container
  records the source only when the item has no richer existing story.
- Surfaced on examine (engine/items/examine_actions._render_node_desc) and in
  the agent prompt line (engine/room_perception.describe_item), each adding at
  most one line.
- Decision documented and pinned: provenance does NOT participate in stack
  identity (engine/items/stacking.py), so two otherwise-identical items with
  different stories still combine; the merged stack keeps the survivor's story.
- Three task-513 templates author defaults (Mikka's wrench, Gribba's apron,
  Vekka's mirror). Tests cover normalize/render, template->instance copy, the
  prompt line, stack neutrality, and steal rewriting.

commit 96b533941e0d05f1029e5a4411e1359483ec01f9
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>

fix(equipment): an equipped payload writes the edges too, not just the dict (task-654)

POST /api/players/<name>, /api/players/import and the library character
loader all assigned player.equipped and nothing else, while every reader of
equipment reads the graph: combat._best_weapon_node (weapon selection) and
equipment_bonuses.get_equipment_nodes (defense, insulation, resistances) both
read EDGE_CARRYING/EDGE_EQUIPPED. So an API write returned 200, showed the item
in the inspector's Equipment section, and did nothing at all in combat. The
in-world verb take writes both, which is why one character could show
hand: [item_spear] (inert) beside hand_right: [item_rusty_hatchet] (live).

EquipmentSystem.set_equipped_payload is now the single writer: it creates the
equipped edges, drops the carrying edge, returns items the payload no longer
claims to carrying rather than orphaning them, keeps the __multi_slot_<id>
markers (dict-side only, never edges) across the sync, normalises the payload
against the canonical slot set, and reports entries that resolve to no item
node instead of dropping them silently.

Also removes the library loader's earlier raw assignment, which the resolver a
few lines below overwrote anyway - and because that resolver ran on
cdata.get('equipped') or {}, a library character with no equipped block had its
equipment cleared on import.

tests/test_equipment_api_edges.py (10 tests): the writer creates the edges, a
slot emptied by a later payload returns the item to carrying, markers survive,
unresolvable entries are reported, and both readers - weapon selection and
equipment bonuses - agree with the inspector. One test drives both writers, an
API write and a real take, and asserts they leave the same shape.

feat(characters): Kraktooth goblin starting loadouts (task-519)

The six goblins now start with their gear from the task-513 templates, authored
as library-id references rather than duplicated inline property blobs:

- inventory is a list of item library ids;
- equipped maps each slot to the library ids worn there.

Import and refresh-to-world materialize the inventory through the shared
property mapping and resolve the references to node-id strings, so each piece
keeps its equip_slots/damage/damage_type/defense and is carried exactly once.

- Zikka: patchwork vest, leg wraps, shoulder plate, boots, cleaver, hooked
  and backup knives, whetstone, bone necklace, trophy tags.
- Mikka: work vest/trousers/gloves, scrap plates, pry-bar dagger, hammer,
  pliers, stolen wrench, lockpicks, copper earrings, loose gears.
- Gribba: butcher's apron over leather clothes, head wrap, forearm guards,
  butcher's knife, meat hook, spoon, flint, drying herbs, the Good Knife,
  food charms.
- Rikka: asymmetric wrap, light leather, throwing knives, performance stick,
  juggling stones, string puppet, little drum, bells, beads, cheap jewelry.
- Vekka: layered leather, hooded cloak, thin guards, hunting bow, curved
  knife, small mirror, folded map, charcoal, found stones.
- Krikka: fitted leather, reinforced palms, climbing guards, hooked blade,
  backup knife, climbing rope, harness, resin, hidden pouch, shiny stones.

Tests: every goblin loads with no duplicate/orphan carried nodes, every
equipped entry is a node-id string pointing at a carried item, and a
materialized weapon keeps its template damage/damage_type.

feat(items): Kraktooth goblin gear spec and templates (task-513)

compact, re-runnable spec rather than hand-written JSON.

- data/library/items/_specs/kraktooth_gear.json: 72 items across clothing,
  armor, weapons, tools, accessories and shared materials, keyed by id and
  grouped by the category that supplies their defaults. Deferred entries
  (partial-armor sub-slots, the shared wire/nail/spring material kit, generic

  recorded in the spec.

  actions, base tags, default equip slot and weapon damage; the entry
  overrides. Validates categories, equip slots, damage types and tag casing,
  and emits sorted ids in a fixed field order so `--check` is byte-stable.
- 72 templates emitted into data/library/items/.
- Tests pin the category invariants, the emit/check round trip, and the six
  goblins' gear.

feat(items): item biome affinity vocabulary (task-571)

No library item declared which biomes it belongs in, so the compiled
wilderness area tags (forest, woods, dense, rocky, farmland, shore, ...)
matched no item and every wilderness cell planned empty.

- New optional `affinity` field on the item schema: the area/biome tags an
  item belongs in, kept separate from general `tags`. Threaded through the
  shared materializer, place/refresh, and the graph item builder so a node
  carries it too.
- engine/population.py unions `tags` and `affinity` when indexing domains,
  so `affinity: ["forest", "woods"]` makes an item a candidate for a forest
  cell without diluting its general tags.
- Backfill: tools/backfill_item_affinity.py derives affinity for the raw
  natural resources from the tags they already carry (herbs, mushrooms,
  berries, roots, plants, ore/stone, fish, scrap) and writes 361 item files;
  `--check` proves it is stable. Prepared food, furniture and buildings are
  excluded.
- Tests: affinity resolves a wilderness plan, comma-string affinity parses.

commit 87bfc5a5acd41277824b018a5e35ae551a95c252
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>

fix(characters): one equipped shape for import and refresh (bug-516, task-519)

Import and refresh-to-world disagreed about what a library character's
`equipped` field means. Import rewrote references to node-id strings;
refresh wrote the raw template value through, so a dict entry landed on the
live player and `is_exposed` -> `graph.get_node(dict)` raised TypeError and
took down GET /api/state.

- One shared materializer for `inventory` (string library-id refs and dict
  node_id/library_id/properties entries) that preserves equip_slots,
  damage/damage_type, defense, insulation, light_level, triggers and
  contents. Refresh now materializes idempotently instead of ignoring
  inventory.
- One shared resolver for `equipped`: a reference may be a node id, library
  id or name and is resolved against the loaded inventory to node-id
  strings. Refresh no longer writes a non-string.
- `library_item_properties` carries `damage_type`; it was silently dropped
  on every materialization.
- `is_exposed` skips non-string equipped entries the way it skips
  `__multi_slot_` markers, so a foreign-shaped slot can never 500 state.
- character_loadout_check learns string inventory refs and library-id
  equipped refs, and exempts library-id-backed entries from the
  missing-properties warning.
- Regression tests: refresh a dict-equipped template -> /api/state 200;
  string refs preserve full stats; library-id equipped resolves.

commit f3b7b42a4c4c626dcc44f540dd8f2acd29facaec
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-02T01:16:24+02:00
Refs:   

feat(auto-dress): LLM picks the outfit, and you see why before it lands

The button said "from Interests" and did a tag intersection, which is not the
same thing as choosing what someone would wear. Measured: a blacksmith whose
interests are metal/tools/iron/temper matched nothing wearable, fell through to
the generic clothing branch, and was handed a Guiding Cane -- matched via the
`tool` tag, which a hammer also carries.

The engine cannot call a model (keys live in the browser, see
routes/action_handlers.py:195), so the inspector asks the engine for a wearable
pool, calls the model, validates the ids, and posts them back. The pool is
deliberately WIDER than the tag filter accepts, because the filter is the reason
the smith's wardrobe came out arbitrary.

The first live run then showed the real problem was visibility, not selection:
the model was handed the MERCHANT's personality and dressed the blacksmith like
a trader, and every layer reported success. So the proposal is now confirmed in
a modal that shows the personality and base_description the model read, with the
picks as per-slot checkboxes. `description` is deliberately excluded from the
prompt -- it is regenerated FROM the equipped items on every wear/remove, so
feeding it to a prompt that chooses equipment is circular.

Three bugs found and fixed along the way:

- A repeated library id minted a second item node. _hydrate_item(always_fresh)
  creates a NEW node per call, so one id twice put two pairs of boots in one
  slot. De-duplicated in the engine, where re-validation belongs; the browser
  already did it, but a boundary that trusts its caller is not a boundary.
- `node` was unbound in the equip loop's except branch whenever _hydrate_item
  raised, so the failure was swallowed and the candidate vanished unreported.
- tools/character_loadout_check.py, added this session, found 14 dict entries in
  `equipped` across three character files -- each of which 500s GET /api/state
  once that character is refreshed from the library. All 14 collapsed to
  node-id strings textually, so the fix is 3 files and no formatting churn.

All five Eldenford nodes now carry an explicit library_id, pinned via
refresh-to-world with sections: [] (applies nothing, still links). Sync no longer
depends on any name matching. That pins today's filename-shaped keys, which does
not survive a rename -- task-667 records why the library needs a surrogate key.

Gates: 485 JS unit tests, 32 Python tests, lint, typecheck, module index,
feature index, loadout check, task tree -- all clean.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 7c1757cbca8f5420f47c48ceda4e6b4a39b028e2
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>

feat(graph): central gravity follows layout, contents simulated, follow pass removed

Central gravity is now per-layout: 0.05 in the free Graph layout (barnesHut 0.3), 0 in Map and Levels. The rule lives in `GraphNetwork.centralGravityFor()` and all physics switches route through `GraphNetwork.applyModePhysics()` to prevent silent re-enablers.

With `centralGravity: 0` in both solvers, the global field that dragged children to the canvas origin is gone. Items, characters, and logic triggers are now in the vis solver (`physics: true`, unfixed) - verified on kraktooth_goblin_camp: 29/29 items, 23/23 characters, 29/29 triggers simulated. The 120 ms follow pass, parent-relative offset map, `rememberDrop`, and live separation easer are deleted (~270 lines from `relative-layout.js`). A drag no longer re-derives on `dragEnd`; the edge spring carries contents.

Separation module is now a seed pass only: it de-overlaps the derived ring once per layout. Measured cold on kraktooth_goblin_camp (638 nodes): contents median 134 px from their room, 0 overlapping pairs.

Physics defaults rebalanced for `centralGravity: 0`: repulsion `-8`, spring constant `0.10`, spring length `120` (was `-40 / 0.02 / 100`). Map spacing default raised to 240 px/cell. Auto-span increased to 10000 px with tidy stepper snapping so mid-size worlds show place names by default.

Added Jake Halloway character and authoring tools (`author_kraktooth_library.py`, `names_jake_halloway.py`). Updated task-485 (superseded) and task-511 (narrowed) documentation. Unit tests updated for new behavior.

BREAKING CHANGE: Nodes previously pinned with `physics: false` and a follow pass are now simulated. Scenarios where a content's parent is excluded from physics will see contents drift (known gap - fix requires "content belongs in solver only when its parent is").

Eight way properties were read by the engine but missing from the library spawn

(barriers.py:169), climb_dc/jump_dc (movement.py:543), refusal_message and

four places and had already diverged, which is how insulation ended up
settable by a trigger yet unsyncable from a library.

tools/way_property_index.py to generate the reference page from it and fail
when a hand-maintained list drifts from it. Each property declares which
lists it belongs in, so view-layer and edge-layer keys stop registering as
drift - a gate that always cries wolf is one nobody runs.

commit 129ca96d71153d29903b1a6eb8a645147a5ecbf0
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-01T11:15:56+02:00
Refs:   

feat(library): one template-link contract, and a way to break it (task-289, task-317)

Two cards described the same remaining work from two directions, so they were
decided and implemented as one unit. Splitting them is how library_id ended up
with three different id-resolution behaviours.

Re-measured the old Status claims first rather than trusting them:
refresh-to-world did already dispatch all four types, and break-template-link
and engine/sync.py did genuinely not exist anywhere.

What was actually broken was not the missing whitelist. It was that the link
itself had no single definition:

  item       never guesses - explicit id only
  way        slugifies the node's name
  area       strips an `area_` node-id prefix, then falls back to the name
  character  falls back to its display name

So one operation on four node types could resolve to three different templates,
or to none. And nothing could unbind any of them, so "this copy is mine now"
was unexpressible: an author's hand-fix was one stray Refresh away from being
undone, with nothing to say otherwise.

engine/sync.py is the contract - TemplateSpec per type (registry, mutable-field
whitelist, how it resolves an id, whether it syncs onto the node or its
Player), resolve_template_id, link, break_template_link, locked_fields,
changed_fields, NEVER_SYNCED. The four _refresh_* handlers now call it instead
of each carrying its own fallback.

The per-type apply bodies stayed put on purpose. A character's fields land on its
Player through a coercion table and a way's are coerced booleans; folding them
into one loop would have been a refactor for its own sake. What got unified is
the part that had drifted.

Two decisions taken rather than assumed:

- A guessed sync now records the link. A character with no link would guess a
  template from its own name, sync from it, and not write library_id - so the
  node read as unlinked while plainly having a template, and Break Link had
  nothing to break.
- Breaking a link does NOT lock the node's fields. Locking them would alter the
  node (the card says a break changes no data) and would make a later deliberate
  re-link + refresh silently apply nothing, which reads as a broken button.

current_state is a real disagreement, recorded rather than resolved: the card
says never sync it, the code syncs it for items and ways. For a way the card is
right (open/closed is runtime, so a sync re-opens a door an NPC closed); for an
item it carries authored content ("hidden" vs "normal") and has shipped that way
since the item sync was written. It is noted in NEVER_SYNCED and pinned by a
test so it cannot drift unnoticed.

Verified in a browser, not by unit test: refreshed way_bathroom_door from the
bathroom_door template (linked: true, description updated), opened the way
inspector, and the footer read Save to Library / Refresh from Library / Break
Link. Clicked Break Link - the confirm says the data is kept. Afterwards the
button is gone (nothing left to break), the panel reads "was linked to
bathroom_door - this copy no longer syncs", library_id is null,
template_broken_from is set, the description is unchanged, and the event stream
logged: Unlinked "bathroom door" from library template "bathroom_door". Data
kept.

26 new tests in tests/test_template_links.py. Gates: lint clean, 465 JS unit
tests, full pytest 12 failed / 6570 passed - the identical 12 names a clean
master worktree fails.

Still open, deliberately: task-290 (variants and override tracking - a field
whitelist is not a variant system), the current_state ruling, and the library
browser's per-entry instance counts. Filed separately as task-658:
js_module_index.py --check fails on room-context.js despite a complete header,
verified pre-existing on a clean master worktree.

commit 8abad0dbc09d75fb0fcf972138973d13f625c305
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-01T01:55:41+02:00
Refs:   

fix(weather): one open-sky predicate, and the compiler finally writes it (bug-54)

Cause 1 shipped on 2026-09-27. Cause 2 was still open, and by the time it was
picked up the card's own picture had gone stale: the concurrent biome work
landed, the taxonomy grew 51 -> 105, and three biomes (porch, courtyard,
balcony) now declare `outdoor`. That fixed half the problem and is exactly why
the other half was still broken.

The real shape was one fact with four readers, and the spellings disagreeing:

  lighting.is_outdoor_area              "outdoor" in tags
  area_description._is_open_sky         either spelling
  environment_propagation (x2)          "exterior" in tags
  tick_manager                          "exterior" in tags
  virtual_world_engine forecast         "exterior" in tags

A painted area was tagged `outdoor` by its biome and the forecast was looking
for the literal `exterior`, so it was skipped every turn, silently. That is the
thing this bug is named for, and no amount of tagging the areas would have
fixed it.

Both spellings are now READ through one predicate (engine/area_tags.py) and
only one is WRITTEN. Neither can be retired cheaply on disk - biomes.json
authors write `outdoor`, a few hundred hand-authored library areas carry
`exterior` by hand - so is_open_sky accepts either while compile_grid emits
exactly one, asserted by a test, because emitting both is the cheap fix that
re-creates the trap.

The compiler takes the fact from the scope's MODE, not the biome: of 105 biomes
only 3 claim open sky and an outdoor `beach` is not one of them, so delegating
would have left the most obvious outdoor biome in the game on a static light
value. compile_grid already computed `outdoor = record.mode == "world"` and
threw it away.

Verified in a browser, not by unit test: painted a 3x3 sparse_forest scope in
world mode and generated it through the app's own
POST /api/world/scopes/verify_wild/grid/generate. Header went 36 -> 45 areas,
the nine new areas carry ["forest", "woods", "outdoor"], and after one
POST /api/turn/apply all nine had weather "snowy", humidity "humid" and
temperature 19.6. Before this change a compiled painted world received none of
it, ever.

tests/test_open_sky.py (35 tests) covers the predicate, every reader agreeing on
either spelling, a real town/interior compile staying untagged, a real world
compile being tagged, one spelling only, and the forecast reaching a painted
area while staying out of interiors. One of them is a deliberate grep guard: a
new `"outdoor" in tags` in a known reader fails the suite, which is how this bug
comes back.

Full suite 12 failed / 6544 passed - the same 12 names a clean master worktree
fails, so none of them are mine.

commit 27de541a2fbbe6c70015e896a4c53d26933daf56
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-10-01T01:48:16+02:00
Refs:   

feat(spatial): relative facing - left, right, forward, back (task-313)

A character had no orientation, so "to your left" resolved to nothing: the four
words were already accepted by the parser and then failed at resolve_exit, and
back/forward were handled only by a transit branch no area could ever reach.

Facing is the heading of the last crossing, stamped on the Player when it moves
and changed only by moving - there is no turn-in-place verb, which keeps the
feature to one piece of state. The resolved cardinal goes through ORDINARY exit
resolution, so the words alias an area's directions and never replace them:
"go left" becomes "go west" and the room still calls the door what the author
called it.

Three gaps the card's scoping notes did not name, each of which would have
shipped a dead feature:

- The authored "cardinal" never reached the graph. serialization_legacy read
  the exit KEY (in labs, the label "Shaft 1", not a heading) and ignored
  exit_info["cardinal"], so cardinals authors had already filled in went
  nowhere.
- Only world_compile wrote "cardinal" onto an edge; connect_areas and the
  create_way effect wrote "direction" only. facing.edge_cardinal() reads
  cardinal then falls back to direction, and is the single reader both the
  stamper and the resolver use - otherwise facing would work in compiled zones
  and fail silently everywhere else.
- labs.json holds connectivity twice (a legacy "areas" block AND a pre-built
  "graph") and the loader uses the graph, so filling in the areas cardinals
  changed nothing observable. The four Vent 2 graph edges needed them too.

The transit machinery is deleted: is_transit_area, get_transit_roles,
resolve_transit_movement, data/library/tags/transit.json, and BOTH
handle-renaming branches - the server description and the agent prompt, which
had the same defect. The tag was declared applies_to:["ways"] while its only
consumer read it off the AREA, so it had never run in any shipped content; it
also replaced handles rather than aliasing, hardcoded two ways, and derived
"back" from the way a character stood AT rather than came through. task-224
gets a superseded-by note so nobody re-implements it.

The agent prompt now advertises the words when - and only when - the character
has a facing, because a prompt that offers a word the engine refuses is worse
than silence. /api/state carries facing so the prompt can know.

Verified live, not by unit test: crawled the labs vent from Room 3, exits
listing "[Vent 2]" and "[Shaft 1]" with no back/forward substitution; "go left"
resolved to Door 3 while "go back" took Shaft 1 from the same position; "go
left" in the shaft named the resolved cardinal and the current facing instead
of refusing generically; the event stream logged the resolution outright as
"matched 'back' as exit 'Shaft 1' (north of facing south)".

52 new tests in tests/test_facing.py. The transit tests were ported, not
deleted - they stood a character AT a way, which is a different situation. Full
suite 12 failed / 6509 passed; a clean master worktree fails with the IDENTICAL
12 names at 6446 passed, so the 12 are the floor and not mine. AGENTS.md's
known-failure table said 1 failed and was stale; it now carries the measured 12.

The Connect Rooms modal already created the way node and its 4 edges, so counting its 22 controls found exactly the three gaps the audit listed: no display name, no cardinal suggestions, no one-way.

- Way Name field; handle_build_connect_legacy now uses an authored name and falls back to the historical '{room}-{dir}'.
- A 10-entry cardinal datalist on both direction inputs, matching graph/layout-engine's dirOffsets set. Directions stay free text.
- One-way checkbox. one_way was already honoured by the engine (movement.py:541, npc_behaviors.py) and by the Way inspector, so this wires existing machinery rather than adding a mechanic. It disables and clears the B -> A direction, since that guard makes the field meaningless.

Also fixes a real bug found on the way: both area selects defaulted to the first area, so submitting the form untouched produced a self-referential way. Area B now defaults to the second area, and both callers reject the same-area case with a toast.

Verified in a browser on a second instance (port 4445, leaving the running session alone): ways 20 -> 21, node named 'Rope Ladder' with one_way true, Way inspector shows ONE-WAY checked, stream logs 'Connected rooms'. lint, typecheck, 465 JS unit tests and 107 movement/way pytest tests pass.

commit ac35ac0ab2cf3b1e318da87454703a66b0ea161d
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-30T23:04:49+02:00
Refs:   

fix: live-audit corrections across combat, items, UI and docs

Work from a live audit of the running app. Thirty-two defects fixed and
verified in the browser, ten findings retracted for judging a surface from a
partial view, and the rest filed or narrowed with the measurement recorded.

Combat
- attack and defence use ability_mod, not raw ability scores. STR 9 was landing
  as +9 and DEX 14 as +14, so the defender won the contest more often than the
  attacker. engine/checks.py already defined the modifier; combat never called it.
- "defense" is a damage expression read by the existing weapon-damage parser,
  so an author types 20 or d8. The old pattern required a leading count, so "d8"
  evaluated to zero -- armour that silently reduced nothing.
- flee / disengage / withdraw are real verbs. All four fell to the emote
  catch-all and answered "Belne flee."; retreat only appeared to work because
  the waiting state string-matched the word to cancel itself.

Items and characters
- equipped items are findable, examinable and droppable. Three defects, one
  cause: the matcher and drop both omitted them, and drop compared names
  case-sensitively ("Knife" against "knife").
- 326 painted ways store an area ID in the area_from name slot, so the one_way
  guard compared a name to an id and never matched -- every one-way passage in a
  painted world was traversable both ways.
- size is a first-class character property rather than a size_* trait, and the
  way endpoint resolver prefers the id.
- player.inventory was hardcoded [] in to_dict, so every export reported an
  empty inventory.

UI
- the scope picker had never rendered a scope: the endpoint answers a nested
  tree, and the caller read a key that does not exist on it.
- the WorldPainter offered 1 of 6 scopes, which is what blocked reaching
  Eldenford at all.
- the cell palette was 130 flat rows because a comment claimed a select cannot
  nest. It can; there are now 23 real optgroups plus a filter.
- the scope tree called a scope holding 21 areas and 25 items "not built".
- the turn modal orphaned its context menu and left a full-page scrim.
- aimed attacks silently ignored the region when the phrase carried an article.
- narration substituted the literal word "someone" for the player's own pronoun.

Docs
- AGENTS.md gains a numbered gate for UI inspection, replacing advice that was
  in effect skipped seven times.
- the live-audit README records the retractions and what they cost, so the
  mistakes are auditable rather than quietly reverted.

Also includes two authored library fixtures (a padded gambeson with a dice
defence, a straw practice dummy) and the audit screenshots, plus in-flight
podcast and emotion work that was already in the tree and is not described here.

commit 9e968c5d9a1575ab8fc058adba2f4b81a48f8184
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-30T17:48:26+02:00
Refs:   

docs(emotion): a whole-system survey of mood and emotion

Every mood- and emotion-related thing in the repository, gathered from four
read-only passes (engine Python, engine JS, data, docs and task tree) and then
measured rather than read off comments.

The finding: there are two emotion models, they write different fields, and
nothing synchronises them. A legacy single-label string on Player.emotion and a
36-dimension affect map on Player._emotions. Five of the six production write
paths still write the legacy one - and the legacy one is not what selects a
character's face, so the most common way to change a character's emotion does not
change their expression.

One concept carries six vocabularies of five sizes: 7 (behaviour-editor
dropdowns) / 11 (engine art keys) / 12 (client art keys, +neutral) / 36 (affect
dimensions) / 70 (keyword aliases, duplicated Python+JS) / ~50 (memory picker).
The 11-vs-12 pair is fine and verified equivalent; the 7 is the seam an author
actually touches and it reaches 7 of 36.

Also recorded: felt_from_llm is written, tested and called by nothing but two
tests; there is no stored 'mood' anywhere (the word means a derived narrative
state); and the label map duplicated across the wire is in sync today (verified
70/70, zero divergence) with no test that would notice if it stopped being so -
which corrects the expectation that these two copies had already drifted.

Every file:line citation is checked: 35 resolve, none past end of file, 13 task
ids exist. Contradictions already written down are collected with locations
rather than re-litigated, and the closing recommendation is to decide which model
is the one before fixing anything else, because four of the five items below that
decision depend on it.

docs(tasks): task-593 shipped, and task-600 traced to its four causes

task-593 moves to review with the labelling work: the acceptance, the reason the
constant 'LLM' fallback mattered more than the missing labels, and the note that
there is no server-side provider call to capture.

task-600 was filed in another lane with acceptance "TODO" and is rewritten with
what the Generate button actually does. It works - it wrote a persona - and the
persona is bad, which is this task rather than a separate one. Four causes, each
traced:

- `json-schemas.js:154` declares a *strict* schema of two nullable strings, so the
  model is required to compress a character into one prose blob. The shape
  `character_appearance.py` reads - fears, likes, dislikes, kinks, turn_offs, each
  a list of tag ids - has no slot at all.
- the prompt names fears and motivations and provides nowhere to put either;
- `temperature: 0.9` for output that is saved permanently and feeds every prompt;
- and `parsed.personality || 'A mysterious character.'` silently overwrites a
  hand-authored character with a cliché and reports success, which is why the
  failure was ambiguous.

Three decisions are recorded as decisions rather than left as chat: whether prose
stays alongside the ids (recommend yes - different code reads each, and the ids
are derived from the prose in the same call so they cannot disagree), which id
vocabulary applies, and whether task-600 absorbs bug-514. It does not: the
personality button toasts when no key is set, but three *other* generators return
silently on the same condition, so bug-514's premise is wrong for the button and
right for its siblings.

The id validation is recorded as blocked on task-601, which has not settled
whether a tag id may contain ':' and spaces. Validating against a charset that is
about to change builds the validator twice.

task-601 and the rest of the 2026-09-30 task burst belong to another lane and are
left unstaged.

commit 98ecd5c62de074f68755ebc154177ddb73a21003
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-30T14:59:06+02:00
Refs:   

fix(inspector): label every LLM call site, and never fall back to a bare 'LLM'

Eleven of seventeen provider call sites passed no `label`, and the collector's
fallback was the constant `'LLM'` - so eleven unrelated calls were
indistinguishable in the inspector and in the tick log (`LLM -> inclusionai/...`
over and over). The four inspector *Generate* buttons were all among them, which
is why the tool looked broken precisely where it was used.

Every call site now names itself: `inspector/generate-personality`,
`/generate-interest-tags`, `/generate-fear-tags` (told apart by `opts.field`,
since the two share one implementation), `/generate-appearance`,
`/appearance-repair`, `/edit-area|item|way` via a new `spec.id` on the shared
Improve flow, `ai-generator/generate` and `ai-generator/repair` (the second call
of a repair pair), `object-responder`, `settings/test-connection`.

The fallback is the real fix. `captureRaw`'s `'LLM'` is a name carrying no
information, and it is indistinguishable from a capture that failed - which is
exactly the confusion it created. `_deriveLabel` now uses the caller's label, then
the model, then a shape summary of the request (tools / streamed / structured /
chat), so a future call site that forgets to label itself is still traceable and
visibly *unlabelled* rather than impersonating the whole system.

Nothing else changed: a label is capture metadata, `captureRaw` is already
fire-and-forget, and `chatWithTools` routes through the same `chat()` that
already captured on all three exits (error, streamed, normal).

There is no server-side provider call to capture: the backend queues
(`queue_llm_respond`), the browser calls the provider and posts back to
`/api/llm_respond`, so every request passes through `llmClient`. Labelling the
call sites therefore covers all of them - worth stating, because the alternative
architecture would have been a much larger piece of work.

commit d7a5021bb7cceb624143b48511fd45db16a85677
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-30T14:56:16+02:00
Refs:   

fix(world): a promoted scope compiles, and a way knows what it connects

Two authoring paths, both older than the scope model, produced nodes the scope
projection could not place - and both failed *silently*, which is the worst way.

**A way with edges but no properties.** `connect_areas` (the NL editor, and the
graph canvas behind the same route) creates the way node and its four connection
edges and nothing else, so `area_from`/`area_to`/`world_scope_id` were empty.
`world_scopes.way_endpoints` read only those properties, so it resolved to
`(None, None)`, the membership test in `project_subgraph` never passed, and the
way existed only in the unfiltered graph - visible under "Whole world", absent
from the scope it plainly belonged to.

The properties were a *cache* of the edges; movement walks the edges and never
reads the cache, and the one reader that believed the cache was deciding
membership. Two fixes, in that order of importance:

- `way_endpoints` falls back to the connection edges. Connectivity is the edges;
  a way that has them is not scopeless, merely described badly. This covers every
  authoring path, present and future.
- `_infer_way_facts` fills the cache on write from both graph write doors
  (`/api/graph/nodes` and the batch `connect_areas`), stamping the endpoints as
  ids, `world_scope_id` when both ends share a scope - deliberately unset when
  they do not, which is a real state - plus the midpoint and `current_state`.
  Existing properties are never overwritten.

**A `baked` scope refused to compile.** Promotion sets `paint_policy: "baked"`
and writes no grid, so the record read as authored-not-painted and the refusal at
`compile_grid` was written on the belief that such a scope never carries paint. It
does: the paint route creates a grid on demand, so promoting a selection and then
painting the rest of the room landed exactly there, and the error told the author
to delete the scope and start again with 70 painted cells discarded. Nothing
overlaps - promotion clears the promoted area's painted cell - so the fix is to
delete the refusal and let it compile; hand-authored nodes carry no provenance, so
`apply_patch` cannot replace them and ungenerate skips them. The three tests that
asserted the old contract are rewritten to the new one, including the case the
refusal was written for (a baked scope with *nothing* to compile now says so).

**And the mirror stops being a second opinion.** A scope record's `area_ids` list
is a denormalised cache of what the areas' own `world_scope_id` stamp already
says, but it was written by two paths and read by a third - and not by the compile
path, which is the one that mints the most areas. Eldenford interior reached 71
stamped areas and 1 listed. `area_ids_in_scope` now derives from the stamps like
`own_area_ids` already did, so the subtree counts, the scope tree and "Whole
world" stop disagreeing with the single-scope projection. The list is still
written; it is simply no longer a decision input.

feat(library): 1,383 authored items, and a generator that is in the repo

The library goes from 532 to 1,915: vegetables, herbs, medicinals, dyeing plants
and incense, orchard and hedgerow fruit, pulses and oilseeds, mushrooms (including
fly agaric and deathcap, tagged as hazards rather than food), meat, fish, dairy,

trades, clothing by class, textiles, kitchenware, lighting, manors and market
squares.

The content lives in the repository and the shipped files are held against the
authored tables by a test. That is not ceremony. On 2026-09-30, 545 authored items
were silently absent from the library and the pass was still being reported as
written, because the count came from what the generator *wrote* rather than what
was *there*; the tables were in a temp directory, so nothing in the repo noticed.
The same comparison then found 194 items whose weight had been truncated to an
integer by a scratch helper and then defaulted to 1.0 - an ash scoop weighing a
stone instead of three quarters of one. A presence-only test would have shipped
that forever, so the test compares the record, not just the id.

item_shapes.py carries the lessons as enforced rules rather than as a note to
self: a row's *shape* decides what it is (four transposed-column bugs in one
session, each failing differently), a claim that cannot be backed is dropped
rather than half-written, and a typo'd wearables id raises instead of skipping
silently - which is how seven items shipped with no equip slot.

Two forage tests asserted a pool that no longer exists (one draw of exactly three
ids; one literal relief of 45). They now assert the property: whatever is drawn is
tagged food and authors a real on_eat that relieves Hunger downward, and the
movement matches an effect the library holds.

feat(graph): one hierarchy, and a map that names its places

The scope tree lived at the bottom of the graph view while the Outline tab - the
panel that already held a hierarchy - held only the bottom of one, and a region
could be picked from two further dropdowns. It is now the top of the Outline tab:
world, then scope, then the areas inside it. Clicking a scope row loads it, and an
unmade scope offers the honest jump to the WorldPainter, which is where ⚙ Generate
actually lives. The breadcrumb stays in the graph, because "where am I" is a
question about the canvas and "what else is there" is a question about the world.

The clipboard copy of the outline now carries the whole hierarchy; copying areas
alone silently dropped the two levels above them.

AUTO_SPAN_PX 1600 -> 4200, so a 20x30 painted extent derives 140px/cell - the card
threshold - and the Map layout draws named cards instead of anonymous dots. The
ladder still holds at both ends: a small zone clamps to 300 and gets roomy cards, a
200-cell world clamps to 25 and takes the whole map in view as dots. The test
asserts the property at each end rather than a number, because a single span
constant cannot give both a 6x8 camp at 200 and a 30-cell world at 140.

vis-network pinned to 9.1.9, with a comment saying to bump it deliberately and
check the graph view when doing so. Edge labels wrap, and the edge length is
computed from the wrapped line count, so a wrapped label cannot overlap the nodes
at either end. An `unlocks` edge carries a whole paragraph, so it is cut to whole
sentences and at most three lines, with the full text still on the hover tooltip.

bug-48's acceptance is written here: the fix was already in the code, and the
invariant that stops it recurring is the pure mapTabDisabled(state) the unit
suite now covers.

commit fcb43ab5867f72dec162a5fd2885ab5402dad47c
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-30T11:41:13+02:00
Refs:   

fix(config): a string-valued setting could not be set

The coercion ladder had no str arm, so a string-defaulted key fell into the
`else: float` one. `float("exterior")` raised, so `forecast.apply_scope` was
unsettable: warned about on every load by the file path, and dropped *in silence*
by the API path the Settings menu uses. One key, one warning, one setting that did
nothing.

Both paths now share one coercion, keyed on the **default's** type rather than the
value's. That also fixes `bool("false")` reading as True, and a bool being silently
accepted where a number is declared. A key may declare `choices`, and
forecast.apply_scope does - without it "exteriorr" quietly applies the weather to
every area.
