commit fbe36fa15332617f68c1d77dcd4e6b52b2da1ca5
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:35:37+02:00
Refs:   

feat(world): the apartment.v1 scoped generation recipe (task-398)

The contract in engine/generation.py was already implemented, but nothing
consumed it. This adds the first real recipe: an apartment of three areas
with a front door in from a hallway, and tag-aware furniture chosen by
task-9's planner.

The task's one hard requirement on the materialisation path was "the engine
must not call route-private helpers such as _spawn_library_item_node;
extract a public library/graph materialization service usable by both the
route and generator". The existing materialize_library_item was not enough,
and the reason is worth recording: it takes a Flask app, mutates a graph,
and mints ids from time.time() plus random.randint. Fine for a player
picking something up, unusable for a generator, which must be able to name
the node it wants, build it without a graph, and produce the same patch for
the same seed.

So engine/library_nodes.py holds the property mapping -- one definition of
what a library item node is -- plus build_item_subtree, whose child and
trigger ids are derived from the parent id and position
(<id>_content_0, trigger_<id>_<type>_0) rather than minted from a clock.
routes/library_ops.py now delegates its mapping to it and keeps its own
id scheme and graph mutation, so route behaviour is unchanged while the
duplicate RELATION_EDGE_TYPES there is gone.

missing_content_ids is returned rather than logged, because a recipe has to
be able to say "this entry references something that does not exist" in its
report instead of shipping a box with nothing in it.

LibraryIndex.candidates walked the whole library for every placement; it
now walks the by_tag postings for the requested domains. The old filter kept
an entry only when it carried at least one domain tag, so that union is
exactly the old candidate set -- same answer, no full scan per placement.

The apartment generates no resident. The task allows "vacant or one
explicitly requested resident seed" and forbids silently manufacturing an
LLM character, so the recipe takes no resident argument at all.

One bug my own tests caught: build_item_subtree had two overlapping
parameters, and the top-level item node received neither while its children
and triggers received the provenance one. A generated child missing the
`generated` fragment reads as hand-authored, which is exactly the state a
later regenerate is allowed to overwrite, so a generated subtree would have
been half-protected.

Verified: same seed gives a byte-identical patch, different seed gives a
different population, a second apply is refused and leaves the graph
untouched, no duplicate nodes, a manual edit to a generated item survives a
refused second generate, every generated node carries provenance, and items
hang off an area or container by a normal spatial relation so examine/take
find them by the existing lookup.

Not done: the editor preview surface. The report already carries the
unresolved tag pools the task requires to be visible, but the
preview -> confirm -> apply UI is not built, and it is a Generate button --
exactly what task-397's scope tree deliberately does not offer yet.

commit 3d50b5c622cbd4ed2ab9bb4998bb238bf1ac00a7
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:20:31+02:00
Refs:   

feat(spatial): one `at` per character, and a distance that is walked (task-419)

Position is a relation, not a coordinate, and a character holds exactly one
`at` edge in the world at a time. That is what keeps the edge set at
O(population) rather than O(n^2).

set_character_position already cleared before setting, so the invariant held
on the path everything used. What was missing was the other path: an effect
handler, an NL edit, or a load file that wrote an `at` edge directly. So
enforce_single_at reduces a character to at most one -- deterministically,
lexicographically first, unless keep_target names one so a move is not undone
by an older edge -- and check_spatial_invariants is the validator the task
asks for, reporting multiple_at_edges, dangling_at_edge and
at_non_anchor_target, because these are properties a save file can break with
no code running.

clear_at is demotion and is deliberately a separate function. Reducing to at
most one must keep the last edge: a character standing at nothing is still
standing somewhere. Only losing attendance takes the position away. My first
version folded the two together and the demotion test caught it.

apply_positional_fidelity gives exactly the attended characters one `at`
edge and nobody else one: 40 characters produce 40 `in` edges and 8 `at`
edges, not 40 x 40 pairs. An attended character with no anchor stays "in the
area" -- attending someone does not invent a place for them.

Proximity is walked, never stored: proximity_hops returns 1 / 2 / None along
`at` then beside/on/under/behind, and proximity_phrase renders at / by /
across the room. The test proves why it cannot be cached: adding a `beside`
edge changes the answer immediately and removing it un-applies immediately.
None means unreachable by this model, not absent.

The anchor vocabulary is a pooled resource and a landmark cluster.
spawn_from_pool is bounded three ways, all of which must hold or "gravel"
becomes a way to manufacture items: at most max_spawn per call, at most
remaining ever, never more than asked. A pool outside any area spawns
nothing rather than orphans. select_area_anchors is the 3-8 budget -- pools
first, then landmarks, then the rest, ties by id, clamped, and a ceiling
rather than a quota.

engine/serialization.py needed no change: the `at` edge, a pool's remaining
count and the anchor selection all round-trip through the existing generic
edge path. The new tests assert that, including that a reloaded pool does not
refill and a reloaded budget does not change, since an area that read
differently depending on how it was loaded would be worse than no budget.

check_spatial_invariants is a callable, tested against a broken graph, but
is not yet wired to a validation surface; there is no graph-validator route
to hang it on.

commit 4ee518e2023de0c835766af110abd909de04e988
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:20:13+02:00
Refs:   

feat(world): awareness channels, so audibility replaces a hop radius (task-418)

A text/graph world has no Euclidean space, so "within two rooms" is a spatial
fiction: hop radius was only ever a cheap proxy for co-present at one remove.
Sound is not a proxy. It already exists in engine/sound.py with a per-way
acoustic barrier, so a closed door demotes a room for a physical reason
instead of an arbitrary enter: 2.

AwarenessChannel is the seam -- propagate(graph, origin_area_id, context)
returning area_id -> strength 0..1, origin included -- with one implemented
channel (SoundChannel) and one test double proving a second channel needs
nothing but propagate. The seam is duck-typed on purpose; reading
channel.threshold directly and letting a try/except swallow the resulting
AttributeError is what made my first second-channel test produce nothing.

bug-30 turned out to be already fixed: propagate_sound is a Dijkstra on
cumulative barrier, documented as taking every path and letting the
least-damped route set the perceived loudness. That is the shockwave
semantics this task asks to choose deliberately, and the threshold is defined
against the returned amplitude rather than the walk's algorithm, so nothing
here silently depends on that staying true.

A locked door is a latch, not a soundproof wall, and the tests now say so
out loud: engine/sound.py gives locked and closed the same barrier, so a
shout crosses one locked door (0.5 + 1 = 1.5, shout 2 leaves 0.5) and
exclusion comes from a chain (0.5 + 1 + 1 stops it). Every barrier test names
its penetration explicitly because the arithmetic is the test.

Caching keys on the graph revision *and* a door fingerprint, because
way.properties["current_state"] = "locked" mutates in place and never bumps
the revision. Caching on the revision alone would keep a door's pre-lock
awareness after the door was locked, which is the case the channel exists
for.

Recency is the within-tier sort key, not a tier of its own: anyone in the
aware set is already tier *aware*, so a recency tier could only restate
co-presence lower down. Tiers are anchor, co-present, audible, hook, and a
hook is the only way to attend someone you cannot perceive.

select_attended calls characters_by_area once per *audible* area and never
for an area nothing perceives, so the cost is bounded by how much of the
world is audible from the anchors rather than by the population -- which is
the point of retiring the radius. Through the real entry point, 200
characters in one room gives an attended set of 8 and iterates the roster
dict zero times, same as with 8 characters. Candidates come from task-416's
presence buckets, so the two compose with no new index.

Nothing here writes fidelity state. task-411 keeps the feed into the tick
loop, and this task is authoritative for selection only, so the two cannot
disagree about who is in the set.

commit a080a3752881448900cae656e0048d7cbb4c75d0
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:19:32+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): the world scope hierarchy as a tree (task-397 step 4)

The graph workspace could already load one world scope at a time, and
task-531 had already added a breadcrumb of where you are. What was missing
was the tree: a world with a county over two towns read as three unrelated
entries in a flat <select> with the names indented by depth. Nothing said
how big a scope was, and nothing said whether anything had been built in it.

static/js/graph/scope-tree.js nests the one payload the server already
sends -- GET /api/world/scopes?flat=1, which already carries parent_id and
depth per scope -- into a collapsible tree. No new endpoint, no per-scope
request. The rules are pure functions (buildTree, visibleRows,
toggleCollapsed, rowLabel) with 16 unit tests, so the panel's behaviour is
checked rather than only eyeballed; the DOM half is a thin render over them.

A card states what the manifest says. An unmade scope reads "Apartment 3B
-- not built" rather than "0 areas", because a materialized-but-empty scope
is a different fact and showing a count of nothing would read as an empty
room.

Deliberately no Generate button. Generating a scope needs a recipe, and
recipes are task-398; a card offering one before a recipe exists would be
the exact "fabricate nodes merely by being rendered" this task forbids.

The flat picker stays -- it is the one control that works without scrolling,
and the tree is a second reading of the same list rather than a
replacement. Both repaint from GraphToolbar.syncScope(), so the loaded scope
is marked in the chip, the breadcrumb and the tree together or not at all,
and the panel loads a scope through GraphToolbar.loadScope() so the picker
cannot drift out of step.

The new unit tests caught four bugs in my own first pass, the substantive
one being that buildTree reset node.children inside the placement loop, so a
child listed before its parent was dropped and the tree depended on the
order the manifest happened to serialise in. visibleRows now also carries a
cycle guard: a card is rendered at most once, because hanging the panel is
the one failure an author cannot work around.

Browser-verified against a live dev server with a four-deep hierarchy:
twisties collapse and reopen, clicking a card loads that scope and marks it
aria-current, and _scopeFilter, the picker value and the tree's selection
stay in agreement. Camera and filter behaviour are untouched.

commit b8b7664c2bd5df7ad5ab893d43bc0ec9b8ebe916
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:18:46+02:00
Refs:   

feat(ticks): area-major iteration, and a co-presence index (task-416)

The per-tick sweep grouped nothing. Co-presence was reconstructed inline
wherever it was needed, by scanning the whole roster once per character,
which is quadratic in the population; and an area's own on_tick items were
ticked in two separate passes over the whole graph.

Co-presence is now a reverse index (area -> character) built once per turn
and kept live: a character that changes area is re-filed in constant time,
so a lookup returns exactly what the old scan would have returned, in the
same roster order. Both ad-hoc scans are gone.

The standing-item sweep is now one area-major pass, sorted by area id and
then node id, so it is a pure function of the graph rather than of graph
insertion order. That fixed two pre-existing determinism bugs: the old
passes ran in insertion order, then trigger-index *set* order. Areas with
no characters, no lit object and no on_tick item are never visited, and an
on_tick item the world has not placed anywhere still fires, id-sorted,
after the sweep -- a first version of mine dropped those and the existing
test_trigger_system case caught it.

Placement is cached on the graph revision plus node/edge counts, because a
door's current_state mutates in place and never bumps the revision.

The acting queue is deliberately untouched: the character loop still walks
the roster in registry order. Area grouping decides the order of scoped
evaluation only, never who acts.

engine/tick_manager.py also carries the task-418 awareness() and
attended_set() entry points, because they are built on this presence index
and were written in the same pass.

Co-present read, best-of-200 per call: flat from 10 to 160 characters
(35.6 -> 32.5 us) where the old scan grows (38.7 -> 45.0). The win is
removing the quadratic term; end-to-end tick throughput sits inside
run-to-run noise at fixture scale.

commit cea610d9432047bb43295a2fb0ccd2106a39da25
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T16:14:35+02:00
Refs:   

docs: a checklist for whether a mechanic is actually wired

Three inert mechanics turned up in one pass over the character/social code, and
all three presented identically to a telemetry table or a soak. The pattern is
specific enough to be worth writing down where the next person will hit it.

A mechanic that exists, works, and is never called looks exactly like a mechanic
with a low event rate. Before concluding anything from a "0 events" reading:

1. Is anything calling it? A working fear_sources with no caller is
   indistinguishable from no fears arising. rg the name outside its own module.

2. Is it reading the right object? A character graph node is created bare and
   carries no tags, traits or any other definition. Anything looking a character
   up by its NODE sees nothing - the data lives on the Player. engine/fear.py
   matched against the node for a long time before this was caught, and it is
   why task-552 was inert twice over.

3. Has anyone authored any? A field can be serialized, round-trip a save and
   look entirely functional while every value in every scenario is []. Nothing
   in a soak moves until data exists, and no code change fixes that.

Plus a fourth, quieter failure: a guard whose lookup can never match.
is_undead_ghost takes a NAME; a call site passing the Player object compiles,
runs, and always returns False. When two similar guards disagree, one of them is
a no-op - check the signature rather than assuming both are right.

And the testing corollary, which cost two of the three findings: a test that
passes for the wrong reason is worse than no test. Prefer asserting the
mechanism - which value was read, which branch ran - over asserting a substring
some other layer could also have produced.

Each example here is a real defect with a commit behind it, not a hypothetical,
so the advice can be checked rather than taken on trust.

commit 0f6852c7cd55e7ea96ff49155d4029e89c12d56d
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T16:13:20+02:00
Refs:   

fix(npc): the undead-ghost exclusion in process_npc_reaction never fired

Found while working task-552 and reported twice as out-of-scope, then left
unfixed because it was pre-existing. It is a one-word bug in a file this lane
owns and it is provably dead code, so leaving it would be the same failure this
commit series has been unpicking all along.

is_undead_ghost(player_name: str) looks the character up BY NAME in
player_manager.players. process_npc_reaction was handing it the Player OBJECT,
so the dict lookup could never match and the guard has never once fired. A ghost
was therefore free to gawk at, and comment on, whatever was happening - which is
the opposite of what the guard was written for.

The mismatch between the two guards is what made it visible: the sibling
_can_observe, added by task-547, always passed npc.name and was always correct.
One of the two had to be wrong, and the signature said which.

Verified before fixing rather than assumed: is_undead_ghost("Wraith") is True
while is_undead_ghost(the_object) is False.

tests/test_undead_ghost_exclusion.py pins the bug at the level it was made, the
behaviour before and after, and a guard that asserts both call sites use
is_undead_ghost(npc.name) so the two cannot drift apart again. That last test is
the point: a test that passes for the wrong reason is worse than no test, which
is exactly what happened once already in task-490, where a combat test appeared
to prove the ghost resistance worked while actually reading the visibility
gate's output.

One surprise worth recording rather than quietly accommodating. This guard calls
is_undead_ghost, the BROAD alias - is_undead or is_incorporeal - so it also
excludes a plain zombie. That is the alias's documented job and it predates this
fix; the fix makes the guard fire for the first time, it does not change who the
guard covers. Pinned as such, with a note that a zombie being allowed to react
is a different question and belongs in a call site using is_undead /
is_incorporeal directly, as task-490's profile does.

-k "npc or ghost or undead or perception or background or pack": 375 pass, the
single failure being the pre-existing test_mcp_misc::test_ghost_mode FastMCP
wrapper mismatch.

commit 7aa46999b81fd07a27015e9ba0374962c66d489e
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:10:29+02:00
Refs:   

feat(items): carried recreation and performance items (task-517)

Entertainment had no recurring source except a standing fixture. `_recreate`
looked for a spot tagged `recreation` and found nothing else, so a goblin
carrying Rikka's kit in their pack had no way to spend a bored hour unless the
camp happened to contain a drum as well.

A carried item is now a **fallback**, and the area is still checked first -
deliberately, because a camp with a drum must behave exactly as it did, and its
fixture is free while the fallback costs a little Energy. That split is the
whole design: the new path is additive and the old one is untouched, so no
existing soak moves.

- `RECREATION_ENERGY_COST = 5`, applied only on the carried path.
- `_carried_recreation` skips a hidden item and a spent one. `uses: -1` is "no
  charge model" - pebbles and cups do not wear out; a drum has a skin that does,
  and at zero charges the fallback reports False rather than topping a
  character up from an empty drum for the rest of the run.
- A spent item is marked `used_up`, not deleted: a used-up drum is still a drum,
  and `_consume_here` deleting things is a hazard task-425 already guards
  against for fixtures.
- Foreground `use` needed no engine change - an `on_use → adjust_vital
  Entertainment` trigger already reaches the vital. It just needed authoring.

Rikka's kit: juggling stones, string puppet, nested cups (`uses: -1`) and a
little drum (`uses: 40`).

Worth recording: the anti-spam is a *threshold*, not once-per-tick, so a small
kit takes a few rounds to lift a character off zero and then stops. The test
says so explicitly rather than pretending one use was enough.

Gate: `pytest -q` 61 failed / 4320 passed, the same 61 as before this change.
The 61 background-simulation and 66 soak/determinism tests pass unchanged -
checked separately, since "the fixture path is unchanged" is the acceptance
criterion and the full-suite count alone would not show it.
`node tools/unit/run.cjs` 397 passed / 13 failed (the same pre-existing ones).
lint, typecheck, js_module_index, tasks validate clean.

No hub file touched.

commit 202d1e237826db5ad17322702119bdef59e53bee

docs: correct the test baseline and record the worktree gotchas

The baseline was "roughly 60 failed / 3239 passed". Measured on a clean master
worktree on 2026-09-28 it is 61 failed / 4202 passed. Both numbers were wrong
and the passed count was off by about a thousand, so anyone using it as a
comparison point was comparing against fiction.

Also switches the guidance from comparing counts to comparing failure NAMES.
The counts drift every time a test is added, so a matching total proves nothing
while a matching set does. The powershell snippet is the one used to produce
the number above, and it was run against two real outputs to confirm it reports
"IDENTICAL" rather than being plausible-looking untested advice.

Adds the two test_character_identity.py failures to the known-failures list.
They are not incidental: test_collapse_is_idempotent and
test_kraktooth_loads_as_one_node_per_character fail on exactly the
canonical-node problem task-457 describes - a character node is created bare and
carries no definition - so going green there is that task's natural acceptance.

New "Gotchas in a managed worktree" section:
- Never git stash. Stashes are shared across worktrees, so a stash is visible
  to every other lane; rebase or merge master in instead.
- Running npm in a worktree rewrites package-lock.json's "name" field to the
  worktree directory name. Check git status after the JS gates and revert it.

.gitignore gains .kilo-local-port, a runtime artefact the test run writes in
every worktree and which was showing up as untracked noise.

commit 35dbdaba96a48bb8b31137822571608016a41f87
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:06:19+02:00
Refs:   

task-457: audit what a character node actually holds (no behaviour change)

The task reads like a migration about moving fields onto the character node.
It is the first step of one. A character node carries no definition at all: it
is created with a name and a type and nothing else, at engine/player_manager.py
:202 in add_player and again as a bare Node(...) at engine/serialization.py:552
on the load path. Nothing anywhere writes definition properties onto it.

So every field in the task's scope - stats, skills, vitals, traits, tags,
interest_tags, personality, descriptions, emotion, conditions, the
simple_npc/autonomy/behaviour fields - lives only on the Player, and the node is
canonical for exactly one thing: its own name.

The clearest evidence is task-552's. Every shipped character carries
"goblin", "teen", "female" in player.tags, and nothing on the node could ever
see them, so fear_sources matched nothing however it was called.

Two tests in tests/test_character_identity.py are already red on master -
test_collapse_is_idempotent and test_kraktooth_loads_as_one_node_per_character
- and they fail on exactly this, character_arix alias nodes and one node per
character. They are the closest thing this task already has to an acceptance
criterion, and they were red before any of this work. Verified against a clean
master worktree, so they are part of the baseline.

This commit is read-only on purpose. engine/character_record.py gives the two
halves that do not need a hub file: DEFINITION_FIELDS, every defining field
grouped and tagged with where it lives today and whether the node must carry
it, and definition_drift(player, node), which reports the fields the Player
holds alone, split into missing and differs. audit(world) answers "how far
along is 457" against a running world rather than a plan.

Deliberately no projector. A function that writes a definition onto a node,
called from nowhere, does not make the node canonical - it makes a second source
of truth and invites exactly the drift the task exists to remove.

The write path is two hub call sites, for WT-0: project onto the node in
player_manager.py:202 for new characters, and in serialization.py:552 for the
additive migration on load, so an old save does not lose its definition.

Two arguments recorded rather than decided. vitals, emotion and conditions are
runtime state rather than definition, and the task's goal paragraph lists them
in scope, so the list follows the task with a note saying so. And memories is
canonical but memory_index is not: the index is a lookup into the memories, so
storing both is two records that can disagree.

commit ac342aa873b2d677c27be1e3c7d581a2d25dd42e
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:05:54+02:00
Refs:   

tasks 505, 507, 490: emotion bridge, structured appearance, undead defences

task-505 - the server half of the embedding bridge. The browser half was
already there: emotion-mapper.js resolves keyword, then substring, then
embedding against DIM_ANCHORS, and both callers post {mapped:{dim:delta}}. The
gap was anything reaching the engine without passing through the browser - a
trigger effect, a background agenda, a saved decision - which had no semantic
path at all, because map_label was keyword-only and felt_from_llm rejected
anything that was not a BASELINES key.

Gated on emotion.semantic_labels, default false, because the keyword map is the
fast path, a semantic miss costs an embedding call, and on a world with no
embedding model it costs a model-load attempt. An injected embed_fn bypasses the
gate deliberately; the gate governs automatic provider use.

felt_from_llm uses resolve_label_semantic only, never map_label. An existing
test caught this: map_label substring-matches, and "hangry" contains "angry",
so a declared feeling was being resolved by coincidence into an emotion the
model never named - exactly what the old exact check protected. A declared
feeling is deliberate, so it gets the semantic bridge or nothing. The recall
path keeps its pre-existing substring behaviour, which is right there: it is a
recall heuristic over authored vocabulary.

embeddings.embed returns a zero vector when the model fails to load. Accepting
it would cosine to 0 against everything and silently resolve every unknown
label to whichever dimension happens to be first, so zero vectors are rejected.

task-507 - first slice, per the task's own instruction to grow per feature
rather than dump the editor catalog. Curated appearance and personality fields
only; every field names the mechanic that reads it, and a test asserts
media/mbti/alignment stay absent.

Stored on the character graph node rather than the Player. Adding a Player
attribute is player.py, a hub, and the node is where task-457 wants the
character definition to end up, so the two point the same way. The consequence
is stated in the task file: routes/library_ops.py is also a hub, so the import
path will not carry this until WT-0 wires it and the feature is API-only for
now.

References are ids, never display names - a display name is a presentation
choice, and a rename would silently orphan every reference. An id that will not
resolve stays visible rather than disappearing, because a kink that quietly
vanishes is a bug report with no cause. A bad entry fails the whole field with
a 400 rather than being silently dropped. Prose-only remains the normal case,
and the PUT will not clobber a hand-written base_description.

A sign bug worth recording: one affect table with turn-offs applied via a
negated sign turns "disgust up, arousal down" into "disgust down, arousal up",
the exact opposite of a turn-off, because the sign of an axis is semantic here
rather than arithmetic. Two tables now, no sign flipping.

task-490 - the other side of task-309's identities. 309 gave undead the undead
tag and ghost the ghost tag and wired them into visibility and vitals, but never
did what those identities do to an attacker: a ghost could be cut, chilled,
poisoned and grabbed.

A separate defence profile, because aggregate_bonuses already produces a
resistances dict that is a flat subtraction from equipped items. 5e resistance
halves and immunity negates, and a halved point of damage is nothing, which is
not a flat number. Folding undead into that aggregate would also make a sword
do less damage to a ghost, which is not what either rule means.

The rule is resistance to damage from NONMAGICAL WEAPONS - about the weapon, not
the injury. The first cut keyed it on damage types, so a mundane sword did full
damage to a ghost while lightning and force halved no matter what struck with
them, which is exactly backwards. The profile now carries a NONMAGICAL_WEAPON
sentinel and the caller says where the hit came from, because a bare fist is not
a nonmagical weapon: a ghost is hard to hurt with a sword, not with a fist going
straight through it.

Ghost and zombie are genuinely different. A zombie has a body, so it can be
grappled and it bleeds. 5e says a ghost is immune to all conditions except
exhaustion, so exhausted is deliberately absent from the ghost list and a test
asserts the two lists differ by exactly that, so a well-meaning edit fails
instead of silently rewriting the rule.

apply_condition returns False on a refusal where it returned None, because a
refusal has to be distinguishable from a missing player - otherwise a caller
cannot tell a grapple bounced.

One testing note: task-309 made an unmanifested ghost invisible and combat
refuses to strike what it cannot see, so the damage hooks are unreachable in
that state. The first combat test passed for the wrong reason, seeing "passes
straight through" from the visibility gate and concluding the resistance
worked. The test now manifests the ghost, and TestVisibilityGateStillWins pins
both gates and their order.

commit 1da02461dde6448c5e4d45c77d1a9541419f03b2
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T15:05:37+02:00
Refs:   

tasks 547, 552, 484, 354: who saw whom, what a character fears, pack logic

The load-bearing fix here is in engine/fear.py, and it is the one to revert
first if anything in this commit needs unwinding.

Fear was inert twice over. A 3-day Kraktooth soak recorded zero threat
actions with five humans living inside a goblin camp, and the conclusion drawn
was that nothing in the simulation could represent "that is not mine". That
half was right: engine/fear.py had a working fear_sources and
apply_frightening, and nothing in the background tier ever asked it.

The other half was wrong, and it was the part that mattered. fear_sources
matched a co-present character on the GRAPH NODE's tags property. A character
node is created bare - Node(id=..., type="character", name=pname) at
engine/serialization.py:552 and again in add_player - so it never carries tags
at all. Every shipped character puts its species in player.tags instead
("goblin", "teen", "female"), and nothing read that. So fear_tags: ["goblin"]
on a human would have done nothing at all, and the field round-trips through
saves correctly, so it looked entirely functional.

character_tags() now reads player.tags, the trait keys and the node, and both
fear_sources and the new fear_sources_for_character go through it. Fixed in
engine/fear.py rather than serialization.py, which is a hub file.

task-552 - run_fear_pass() in engine/background_social.py, called from the
background simulation before the social pass so a fear can pre-empt it. Fear
now produces a visible threat: action with a line, a trace, a memory, a Social
cost, an afraid spike on the affect map and a closeness cost. Four reactions
from a draw seeded by (character, source, tick), so five characters do not
flee in lockstep. Fear pre-empts sociability, checked before the action draw.
It uses spike_emotion rather than the module's _apply_emotion, which writes
the legacy single-emotion string and would have left afraid at its baseline -
a fear in no observable place.

task-484 - only the ends_on half, which was never a tuning question.
frightened gates behaviour toward one named source, so a flag kept after that
source has left is a stuck flag, and it sat for its full 30-minute timer.
release_absent_fears() lifts it, called from both entry points so detection and
cleanup cannot disagree. The numbers - defense_mod, the check modifiers,
FEAR_DURATION_MINUTES - are deliberately untouched: they are tuning, tuning
needs a distribution, and no shipped scenario authors a single fear, so there
is nothing to calibrate against yet. attack_mod: -2 predates any fear being
applied and is not evidence either.

task-547 - engine/observation_signal.py records who observed whom and whether
it was public. Not serialized, because it is derived per-turn state and
persisting it would only store what the next pass recomputes; that also keeps
the task off the serialization and player hubs. record_observations() covers
every tier that can look, not just simple NPCs, and
process_bystander_reactions() now builds its lines from that pass, so the
reaction cap bounds lines instead of silently truncating the observer set.
Noticing and commenting are separate facts: an observer whose reaction resolves
to "ignore" is recorded even though it emits no line.

Public versus covert reads conditions and the activity rather than Player.state,
because state is derived from a precedence hierarchy in which "awake" outranks a
lesser condition - a character who is asleep still reads as "awake". A test
asserts state genuinely fails to show the sleep, so the fallback cannot rot
unnoticed.

task-354 - the MVP the task file itself specifies. Pack identity is a
pack:<name> entry in the character's existing tags rather than a new
Player.pack attribute (player.py is a hub), packmate awareness walks the area
graph rather than using a flat distance, the coordinated target is the nearest
packmate's rather than a majority vote, and a call reaches one room further
than sight and stamps pack_target on every listener - without which a "pack" is
a crowd standing near each other. No formation, flanking or role assignment;
the task puts those out of scope and a test asserts the names are absent.

None of this does anything until a scenario authors it. fear_tags is [] on all
28 Kraktooth characters and no character carries a pack tag, which is the
correct answer and the lesson of task-552.

bug-28, bug-29: investigate speech corruption and WITNESSED attribution

Neither bug has a live repro, so this commit changes no behaviour. It is the
investigation plus regression guards, and the two task files record the outcome.

bug-28 - the corruption was real, the transform is gone. The filed evidence
pinned the shape but not the rule. Across all three Taco Bell exports the
transform is exactly apostrophe-to-space plus a full lowercase; em dashes,
interrobangs, ellipses and *burp* all survived. One rule explains every mangled
sample: that's -> that s, I'm -> i m, nobody's -> nobody s, my name's -> my
name s.

The filed "letter scrambling" was a second, separate thing and not ours. The
event log at data/exports/taco_bell_event_log_2026-08-23T16-19-57.txt:288
already reads "Pleas edont try to be cnormal ... wnat me to lick it away" -
stored mangled before any prompt was built, so it is the NPC agent's own
generated line for that tick, upstream of the engine entirely.

The transform did exist and is gone. Same file, same utterance, two renderings:
line 640 keeps "that's- that's advanced DLC content ... MYSELF" while line 719
has "that s- that s advanced dlc content ... myself". The WITNESSED block was
mangled the same way while the event log kept its capital P, so the pass sat
between the stored event and the prompt.

tests/test_speech_verbatim.py pins verbatim preservation across recent_hearing
for speaker and listener, the directed-whisper copy, speech_log, the speak turn
event and NarrationSystem.listen(). Four new tests in
tools/unit/test_conversation_context.js pin ownRecentSpeech,
buildConversationInstinct and markSpeechLine.

bug-29 - premise disproven on the artifact. Lines 423 and 425 of that same
export are different utterances by the same speaker, not one utterance twice.
The predicted alternative, speaker-label instability, is also not reachable
today: room-context.js computes anon once and substitutes that same value into
the body, so bracket and body cannot disagree. Recorded rather than patched,
since patching unreachable code on a disproven bug would be inventing a fix.

commit 21c08256070b2ebe828d8c1ce9e7ca8aab7c32ae
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T14:23:37+02:00

feat(items): item ownership and personal-item permission (task-515)

A treasured object is not a tool. Gribba's Good Knife is not communal kitchen
equipment, and "nobody else may touch it" should be true in the world rather
than only in the fiction.

The marker is a node property, not an edge: `owner` (a name or a node id - the
same person either way) plus the informational `personal` tag. An item with no
`owner` is nobody's in particular and behaves exactly as before. `take`, `give`,
`use` and `use-on` all refuse a non-owner, naming the owner and pointing at the
way through; the owner is always allowed, including to give it away, because
ownership is not a life sentence.

Two decisions the task left open, made explicit in engine/items/ownership.py and
pinned by tests:

- **A missing or incapacitated owner lifts the refusal.** A dead goblin's knife
  is not a sacred object, and a rule that keeps protecting a corpse's belongings
  is a worse failure mode than a permissive one. An owner who is merely
  *elsewhere* still owns the knife - only a claim nobody can enforce fades. An
  owner handle we have never heard of is assumed alive: silence is not consent.
- **A Social or Intimidation check does NOT lift it.** `steal` is already a
  roll; a second one hidden inside `take` would make an ordinary verb
  unpredictable. So the normal verbs say no plainly and `steal` is the way
  through - never blocked, just harder: a personal item adds a flat +3 to the
  target's Perception, because someone watches their own property more closely
  than loose change. `owner_bonus_to_perception()` is the one place to tune it.

`owner` joins the `_hydrate_item` passthrough - a spawned keepsake that lost the
property would come up unowned and anyone could pocket it, which is the one
thing the property exists to prevent.

Worked example: `gribbas_good_knife.json` (1d4+2 Finesse, `owner: "Gribba"`,
tagged `personal`).

Gate: `pytest -q` 61 failed / 4293 passed, the same 61 as before this change.
`node tools/unit/run.cjs` 397 passed / 13 failed (the same pre-existing ones).
lint, typecheck, js_module_index, tasks validate clean.

No hub file touched.

commit 02f4ddfafebad3bc3f1f6eea5c18920929952bc9
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>

feat(items): the part/component model for devices (task-493)

A device is a parent item whose capabilities are child part items. No new edge
type, no `is_part` flag, no device-type field: containment is an ordinary `in`
edge, and the only thing that says "this is a component" is that the part does
not declare `take` in its own `actions`. That is the model task-299 agreed.

- `engine/items/action_contract.py` is where every verb that *moves* an item
  now asks. Before this only `take` read the action list, so a part was
  untakeable but would still have been droppable, stealable, giveable and
  puttable - untested because nothing happened to point those verbs at one.
  `take`, `drop`, `steal`, `give`, `put` and `place` are all gated now, and the
  refusal names the device ("the battery is part of the phone - it doesn't
  come loose to steal it") rather than shrugging.
- **Charge is the generic `uses`.** No `power` or `charge` property, and a test
  asserts no library item invents one. The phone body is `uses: -1`; the cell
  carries the charge.
- **Depletion is an explicit semantic and no longer detaches silently.** The
  `use` path used to cut a used-up item's `in` edge, which fished a battery out
  of its phone and left the player holding a loose cell they never asked for,
  with no message and no hook. A part now goes `unlit`, fires `on_depleted`, and
  keeps its place - the same rule toggleable_items already used for a lit item
  burning out. An ordinary used-up item keeps the old behaviour. A part may
  author a different ending from its own `on_depleted`; that runs first.
- **Non-portable is not inert.** A part stays reachable and usable via
  `item_reach`, which walks any depth. Portable and reachable are not opposites,
  and `tests/test_item_parts.py` says so in a test name.
- **Prompt visibility agrees with the engine.** `static/js/shared/
  item-containment.js` is the client half of the rule `engine/item_reach.py` is
  the server half of; `world-state.js` and `room-context.js` both call it. The
  client walked one level and checked almost nothing, so it listed the inside of
  a locked cabinet and missed a part two levels down. It also gates the *parent*
  inside `walkContents` rather than trusting each caller to, which is the only
  reason the "forgot to check" version cannot come back.
- Worked example: `phone.json` holds `contents: ["phone_battery"]`;
  `phone_battery.json` is `examine,use` with `uses: 24`.

Found and filed bug-509 while testing: `drop_item` scans only EDGE_CARRYING
while `take` puts everything in a hand (EDGE_EQUIPPED), so you cannot drop
anything you just picked up. Not fixed here - out of scope, and test_item_parts
avoids depending on the broken path.

Gate: `pytest -q` 61 failed / 4267 passed, the same 61 as before this change.
`node tools/unit/run.cjs` 397 passed / 13 failed (the same pre-existing
test_involuntary + test_plan_tracker failures). `npm run lint`,
`npm run typecheck`, `tools/js_module_index.py --check` clean.

No hub file touched.

commit 728dbd7ba96f2826ec59e8703bf92de17a389c99
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-28T13:51:34+02:00
Refs:   

feat(items): item quantity property and pooled resource nodes (task-504)

One item node can now stand for many of the same kind. Mostly a presentation
win - "1 giant tree" and "40 berries" read correctly, and one node is far
cheaper than hundreds - but underneath it is the pooled resource node: a bush,
thicket or tree that yields real item copies when taken from and depletes as
it goes.

Kept distinct from `uses`, which is charges left on *this* copy. A stack of
40 berries is not one berry with 40 charges, and a tree standing in a forest
is not something you can carry at all.

- `room_perception`: `item_quantity` / `pluralise` / `describe_item_quantity` /
  `describe_item`. Absent means 1, so every existing item and save is unchanged;
  an authored count is shown even at 1 so the prose stays uniform.
- Both perception paths read the count through the one shared helper, so the
  agent and panel renderers cannot drift.
- Harvest: `take 3 berries` reaches the thicket that grows them, spawns k real
  copies, decrements the pool, and at zero hands the node to the task-424
  `on_depleted` teardown (`_finish_depleted`, split out of `_deplete_if_spent`
  so the hook is reused rather than reimplemented). Bounded by the ask, the
  authored size, and what is left; skill-gated, where the check scales the
  yield rather than gating the attempt. A yieldless pool is refused, so a whole
  apple tree is no longer carryable.
- A pool is never picked up whole and never stacks: `stackable_twins` refuses
  any node with quantity > 1, since stacking runs the other way and merging
  would fold a pool's count into a copy's uses.
- `find_or_spawn` aims at a pool already standing before spawning a loose copy.
- `lint_library` gains `resource_pools`, so a quantity without a yield, a yield
  naming a missing library id, or a pool yielding itself fails at lint time.
- Data: `apple_tree` becomes a pool of 3 apples; new `berry_thicket` pool.
  `bush_of_berries` is left as the task-410 renewable plant - it is a live,
  separately tested food-renewal system, and the task's own notes call the two
  related but not the same.

No hub file touched. engine/serialization.py needed nothing: item properties
round-trip verbatim, which the new save/load test pins.

commit 33f59c13c5352b6f73c84da60b751dc6997ca83b

fix(items): author consumption on every library food and drink

Foreground eat/drink had no fallback, so a player could eat a tag-only
library item and restore nothing, while background characters silently got
a hardcoded 45 Hunger / 50 Thirst. The two paths disagreed and neither was
authored in data.

42 items now author their own adjusting trigger. Food relieves Hunger by 45
and drink relieves Thirst by 50, matching the old constant so retiring it
does not move the balance; apple was authored at -2 and is corrected to -45.
The constants survive as UNAUTHORED_MEAL_RESTORE/UNAUTHORED_DRINK_RESTORE for
pre-authoring saves only, and the water-area case splits out as
WATER_AREA_DRINK_RESTORE because natural water is an area tag, not an item.

The new unauthored_consumables lint check is what stops this creeping back.
It accepts both trigger shapes, handles trigger_type being a list, and
requires the stat to match the drive being relieved - Hunger and Thirst are
drives, so relief is a negative amount. 26 food-adjacent fixtures keep their
tags for now and are declared in FOOD_ADJACENT_FIXTURES rather than demanding
a nonsense on_eat; untagging them changes what foraging finds and is a
content decision, recorded as not done.

Camp soak: 22/23 -> 22/23 at 1 min/tick, 21/23 -> 22/23 at 15. The 15-minute
gain is the foreground fix showing through.

Full suite was not run for this task at the user's request; the task file
records the last measured branch total and the outstanding run.

task-506, task-508 (already done)

commit 9e298c5d59829de4ed8ca1fed8c2e08b0a4d32b4
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T17:44:37+02:00
Refs:   

fix(examine): credit the character who actually looks inside a container

`examine <container>` already un-hid its contents, but that write is on the
node, so the first character to open a chest revealed the loot to everyone
permanently - and the character who did the looking was never credited with
meeting any of it. Contents therefore never rendered as known, and a second
character who opened the same chest later got nothing at all, because the reveal
had already happened and there was no per-player record to add.

Every item the examine reports is now passed through _register_item_discovery,
which writes the observation memory and the discovered_items entry. All three
readers of "known" are client-side and all read discovered_items
(contextual-actions.js isDiscovered, room-context.js, memory-context.js), so
the stamp is the whole contract - no engine renderer change needed.

Scope is everything the examine reports, not just `in`: on, under, behind,
beside and at count too. The credit is idempotent, and a count of genuinely new
items is narrated so a re-look at a known chest does not read as a discovery.

The chained-follow-up half of task-494 is not in here. The precedent the task
file names does not exist: engine/movement.py dash_to_area performs exactly one
hop and engine/tick_manager.py has no move/dash multi-step logic at all. The
real precedent is client-side (CHAIN_RULES, the human burst phase), and task-352
schedules both for deletion. POST /api/action never advances the clock, so the
server cannot see a turn boundary to enforce a follow-up on. Measurement and a
staged plan are in the task file.

commit 256c49fdddcf2f21c98029eb7088db8be5619db5
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T17:30:15+02:00
Refs:   

feat(relief): relief is permitted anywhere, privacy is a preference

The background tier could only relieve itself in an area tagged latrine/
toilet/privy/restroom/bathroom, and a camp with no such area left characters
who simply never went - the bladder branch ended in a bare `return None`. The
foreground had no such gate, so the two tiers disagreed about the same room.

engine/relief.py is now the single place both tiers read. It keeps the tags as
a *comfort* vocabulary: an improvised relief costs Sanity -2, and Social -3 when
somebody saw, which is the dignity rule that previously existed only inline in
the foreground handler.

Background characters rank candidates instead. Every area within
PRIVACY_SEARCH_HOPS is scored (witnesses, a private tag, a real fixture), and a
character heads for the best if it beats standing still. The occupancy term is
the load-bearing one: it is what stops a single latrine becoming a hub, since a
latrine holding three others now scores worse than an empty yard.

Worth knowing: build_exits_for_area hands back display names, not node ids, so
ranking has to resolve through _resolve_area_id first or every neighbour looks
empty.

The two tiers also disagreed on vocabulary - the foreground tested only
toilet/bathroom/restroom, so a room tagged `latrine` or `privy` was a restroom
to the engine and a corner to the player. Fixed by sharing RELIEF_TAGS.

spatial_item_nodes() is promoted into world_scopes as the one definition of
spatial reachability; the relation tuple had been spelled out three times.

commit 6b8598ccd0ee8e950db907376602b513f43d14bc
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T17:44:07+02:00
Refs:   

feat(environment): narrate the weather, and fix the moon that was never narrated

`env["weather"]` had exactly two readers in the whole backend: the light
multiplier and the DC of `guess time`. Nothing ever said it was raining, so a
character standing in a storm got a temperature sentence, an air sentence, maybe
a smell, and not one word about the rain. This is text only: no new state, no new
engine module, because everything the words describe already exists.

- `engine/area_description.py` -- `WEATHER_PROSE` (one sentence per state in
  `WEATHER_STATES`, so a forecast can never write a state with no sentence),
  `WIND_PROSE`, `TIME_OF_DAY_BANDS` + `time_of_day_prose()` so the sun has a
  presence, and `_is_open_sky()`, which accepts `outdoor` *or* `exterior`. All
  gated on an open sky: a storm is not something you hear through a stone wall,
  the same reason the moon text is outdoor-night-only.
- **The moon has never been narrated, in any world, ever.**
  `get_area_description` read a local `node` on the first line of the moon block,
  but Python resolved `node` as a local because it is assigned *later* in the
  same method (the item and people loops) -- so the line raised
  `UnboundLocalError` and the bare `except Exception: pass` underneath swallowed
  it. The except is now narrowed to the provider errors it was written for, so
  the next real failure is not invisible too.
- Wind was one phenomenon under two keys: `noise_prose` read `noise`, the
  forecast wrote `wind`, so an *authored* `noise: "windy"` narrated wind and a
  forecast gale did not. `WIND_PROSE` reads `wind` directly.
- The two divergent weather vocabularies are now one. `WEATHER_ALIASES` +
  `normalize_weather()` live in `weather_forecast.py`, and the DC table moved
  next to the vocabulary; the private list in the action handler -- which knew
  `sunny` and `overcast`, neither of which the forecast can write -- is deleted,
  and the moon-obscured check normalises so a world spelling it `overcast`
  behaves like `cloudy`.
- `tests/test_weather_narration.py` -- 26 tests, three of which pin the
  vocabularies together (every weather state has prose, every wind state has a
  sentence, every weather state has a DC) and one of which is the moon
  regression. `guess time` behaviour and the moon description are unchanged.

commit 362bb2422bb9b9ad37ff95a4acd6ddfbf11f1f23
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T17:41:21+02:00
Refs:   

feat(world): a painted town with named, typed, structured, enterable cells

Five WorldPainter tasks that together make a town grid mean something, all in
review. The compiler stays the only sanctioned way to mint a scope.

- task-526 map scale: the pitch is derived from the painted extent (a 20x9 zone
  gets 80px/cell, a 200x133 world 30px) with the stepper as a visible override,
  and below 140px/cell an area draws as a cell-sized dot with no name. A card's
  width is its *name*, which never shrank, so one global pitch could not serve a
  camp and a world.
- task-560 names: a `names` map beside the paint layers, because a name is
  metadata and not paint; the compiler prefers it, with a duplicate-inside-a-scope
  fallback so no area ever offers two exits with one name.
- task-561 buildings: 31 types across 9 categories as *biomes* (they used to be
  features, which made a house compile as a road), tagged with a category and
  their purposes.
- task-562 edge semantics: a cell can be something that is not a place. `wall` and
  `void` block, `window` sees through without passing, `door` is a threshold,
  declared in the vocabulary so the compiler asks rather than hardcoding. Merging
  is storey-aware now, since a classroom above a classroom of the same kind was
  becoming one place with a staircase in it.
- task-563 entering a building: a building is a place you go *into*. One `in`
  way per cardinal side that has a way, so you type `in` from the street instead
  of stepping onto the doorstep. With an interior it leads there (one-way, so
  `out` inside keeps meaning the doorstep and the next building is one turn); with
  no interior it is a closed door carrying a themed `refusal_message`, which the
  movement system honours until the way is open, so a knock or a key opens it
  later. `refusal_message` is a general way property, not a building hack, and a
  plain closed door still opens on approach as before.

Verified by walking a compiled town: `in` from the street lands inside, `out`
lands on the doorstep, and a smithy with no interior says "The smithy's door is
shut and the bench is cold." until it is knocked open.

commit 67ec7274e49686a5f310346db15cf04ff593a12b
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T14:32:26+02:00
Refs:   

wip: checkpoint the in-flight world, health, spell and telemetry work

A consolidated checkpoint, not a set of feature commits. Six independent
themes are interleaved at *file* level - `engine/tick_manager.py` alone carries
the lived_log rename, the Max_HP ceiling fix and the companion tick, and
`templates/index.html` carries both the storey wording and the round control -
so splitting this per feature needs hunk surgery, and a commit that mixes
someone's half-finished work under someone else's message is worse than one
honest checkpoint. Theme by theme, with the state each is actually in.

WorldPainter: a paint layer is a storey index, not a height
- The `elevation` layer (a 0..1 height the author never agreed to) is renamed
  `floor` and re-based on storeys: 0 is the ground plane, 1 one up, -1 one
  down, unbounded, so a save can say "eighty floors up" without a legend.
- What you *stand on* is a different fact and now lands on `surface`, read
  from the biome/road record. The old revision put the material on `floor` and
  the height on `elevation`; both were wrong and are gone.
- `RECIPE_ID` bumps grid.v1 -> grid.v2 so provenance records which compiler
  produced a node, and `LEGACY_LAYER_KEYS` keeps a record painted under the old
  name yielding its cells rather than silently losing the map.
- A storey step is a cliff only *outdoors*: inside a town or interior scope it
  is a staircase or the next deck, so `cliff_dirs` returns empty there. A
  way's storey is the lower of the two it joins, so the value does not depend
  on which side of the adjacency emitted first.
- Still open: task-525 (gating traversal on a storey step) is untouched by
  design - the floor feeds prose only for now.

Health and vitals: Max_HP becomes data (task-538)
- The engine had no health model, it had a `100`, hardcoded in at least ten
  places and load-bearing for death, regeneration, healing and hydration.
- HP regen was gated on the literal `p.vitals["HP"] < 100`. That only ever
  worked because Max_HP was 100 everywhere; against a real stat block (a 7-HP
  goblin) it is permanently true, so the character regenerates every turn and
  can never be finished off. Now reads the character's own ceiling.
- task-538 stays in `todo` on purpose: the single resolver that replaces all
  ten sites, `handle_heal` respecting Max_HP, and armour class are NOT done.
  The unchecked acceptance criteria are the record of that.

Spells and companions (task-391, task-537)
- Lyrie's 15 life-bound spell items land in the library with five new effect
  handlers, and five new effect types are registered: `polymorph_target`,
  `create_illusory_companion`, `broadcast_emotion`, `bind_companion`,
  `reveal_hidden`. Sixteen new library items in total, plus Fluffy as a
  character.
- Companions are inert at cast time and live out their duration in the tick
  loop, outside the `skip_npcs` guard on purpose: a conjured thing is not an
  NPC, so a fast-forward must still let it expire.
- task-537 is a review list, not a build list, and records the author's
  verdicts per entry (including "dont add" and "not sure I understand this
  one") so the decisions survive.

Simultaneous mode advances the world per round (task-533)
- Simultaneous mode forced `turnBased` off, which meant the world turn
  pipeline never ran: the clock stood still, vitals never decayed, and
  background simulation and soak orders were never reached. A round now
  advances the world once, when every character including the human has
  resolved, and the human is a participant in that count - never auto-acted.
- Also in the same path: `world._clock_advanced_by_task` was set True by any
  `rest` and never reset, so after the first rest anywhere in the world's life
  `tools/game_tools.py` short-circuited its per-action tick permanently.
- The increment is deliberately unchanged (`time_per_tick_minutes` per round).
  A turn *is* a timeframe, so actions within a turn being free in time is
  correct; how many you may do is task-352's question, not this one's.

Lived log and Soak telemetry become two separate systems (task-542/543/544)
- `engine/trace.py` -> `engine/lived_log.py`, the entry key `t` -> `tick`, with
  legacy load shims so an old save still reads. Renames rather than copies:
  there is no second module and no alias to keep in sync.
- A pre-existing save bug is fixed on the way: `trace` was read on load but
  never written, so a save silently lost the log.
- The `t` -> `tick` rename had a silent-failure mode worth naming: every reader
  that did `e.get("t", 0)` now reads tick 0 for every entry, so the span comes
  back empty and a promotion writes no memory at all rather than raising. The
  readers go through the module's own accessor instead.
- Scenario serialization drops the runtime lived log; save serialization keeps
  it. Two stores answering two questions: a lived log is one player's bounded,
  salience-filtered history; soak telemetry is the run's own record of where
  everyone was, which a capped per-player log cannot answer.
- Telemetry records presence intervals plus the action/condition/death stream
  with a validated `why` tag, and the vocabulary was measured against real runs
  with `tools/why_vocabulary.py` rather than guessed - which is how the missing
  `traversal:` / `forage:` / `schedule:` prefixes were found.
- The Soak Lab space-time view (task-544) draws swimlanes by area with
  condition ribbons, an occupancy heatmap, a `why`-over-time stack, death
  markers and clickable collision pairs. Isolated characters show only the
  areas they visited, but collision data comes from the unfiltered intervals so
  their company stays visible.

Docs, tasks and data
- Sneeze (task-534) gains a runtime condition, catalog entry, speech/emote
  triggers and tests; it documents that a conditioned emote does not roll, that
  the first matching table entry wins, and that a startle short-circuits.
- task moves: 213 (mature traits) and 436 (task durations / action-cost
  removal) to done, 391 to review.
- `tools/reposition_ways.py`: a manual, dry-run-by-default repair for
  hand-authored ways left stale by a scope move. It repositions and never
  removes, and skips generated ways, because a placed way is the author's
  decision to make.
- `.gitignore` collapses 21 individually-listed chroma test artefacts plus the
  generated soak telemetry and the kilo/tmp screenshots into three directory
  rules, so generated data stops accumulating one line per run.

Known state: task-534, 533, 537 and 538 remain open with their acceptance
criteria unchecked, and the folder is authoritative over the frontmatter. The
graph painted-coordinate fix is in the two commits before this one.

Tests: pytest 59 failed / 4044 passed - the same 59 pre-existing failures
(test_mcp_* FastMCP wrapper mismatch, test_scenario_name, test_templates), with

commit 4467da84e0ffd8ddaf1cb57950a045c0f79359fb
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T14:30:36+02:00
Refs:   

fix(world): floor is a storey index, not a floor material

The grid.v1 compiler recipe put the ground material on properties.floor and
painted a 0..1 height fraction on a separate elevation layer, which made
loor mean two unrelated things and left the storey unrepresentable.

loor is now the cell's **storey index**: 0 is the ground plane, 1 one up,
-1 one down, and the scale is unbounded - three stacked rooms, a lake bottom
at -2, an 80-storey tower, -900 in a hole to hell. The ground material moved to
properties.surface (and to surface in the biome/road taxonomy records).
properties.elevation is gone.

- engine/world_grid.py: paint layer elevation -> loor, with loor_at /
  loor_paint_at as the whole-storey reads (unpainted = ground) and a

ormalise_grid migration so a save painted under the old name keeps its
  numbers instead of losing the layer.
- engine/world_compile.py: areas get loor (storey) + surface (material);
  ways get the lower of the two storeys they join, so the value cannot depend on
  which side emitted first; entry_elevation -> entry_floor; RECIPE_ID ->
  grid.v2.
- Cliff prose is restricted to world scopes: a storey step in a town or
  interior is a staircase, not a rockface, so stacked rooms read as rooms.
- graph.py:
ormalize_area_floors repairs a grid.v1 save on load (material
  string -> surface, storey -> 0), the same pattern as the door -> way
  migration. Verified against the goblin camp save: 130 wrong values repaired.
- Front end: the painter paints a loor layer (+1 / -1 default), the storey
  filter and the inspector's Floor field are numeric and no longer clamped to
  +-10 (that cap truncated a skyscraper at ten floors), and every consumer
  coerces a legacy non-numeric value to ground.

Tests: 171 world/grid/biomes/migration (storey bounds, unpainted-as-ground, the
legacy layer migration, the indoor mode gate, the save repair); 361 JS with the
13 pre-existing plan-tracker failures; lint, typecheck and module index clean.

docs(tasks): file the session's measured tasks, with the measurements attached

The session produced more findings than it produced code. These are the tasks
that came out of it, written so the next person starts from a measurement
instead of re-running the same investigation. Three were verified against the
running engine before being filed, and each says so.

Calibrated against the Kraktooth goblin camp (3-day soak, 4,320 ticks, 23
goblins + 5 humans, all background, seed 1234, run through the Soak Lab UI):

- task-552 (fear response) - the `threat` share of every decided action is
  **0**. Five humans lived inside the camp for three days - the chief's pit,
  the cooking area, the workshop - and not one goblin produced a `threat:`
  action. Nothing in the simulation can represent "that is not mine". This is
  the producer side of task-484.
- task-550 (faction/ownership as tags) - no character has a `species` field and
  all service matching is tag-only, so a camp's water is nobody's water and a
  goblin and a human are the same kind of thing to the rules.
- task-551 (privacy-aware relief) - foreground `relieve` already works anywhere;
  only background NPCs are restricted to `RELIEF_TAGS`. The real defect is the
  opposite of the one the title suggests: a background character in a wilderness
  cell with no latrine has 84 eligible areas to choose from and still ends up in
  the camp's waste disposal, because that is the only relief-adjacent thing in
  the candidate set.
- task-549 (species) - the animal/goblin collapse above, stated as its own task
  because it is a data-model change, not a tag change.

Blocked-on re-measurements, filed rather than implemented:

- task-484 (tune `frightened`) - now carries the full `why` breakdown
  (needs 82.2%, social 16.3%, traversal 1.0%, forage 0.5%, threat 0) and stays
  **deliberately blocked** on task-552. Tuning a condition nothing applies is
  work nobody can verify, so the task says to close it rather than leave it
  dangling if 552 slips.
- task-487 (arousal from being seen) - `engine/background_social.py` reads
  `attention_seeker` in two places and both are *seeking* attention, not
  *receiving* it. The nearest real signal, `process_bystander_reactions()`,
  returns reaction strings only, skips every non-`simple_npc`, and is capped at
  one reaction - so the observer set is never actually available. Blocked on the
  new task-547.
- task-488 (gate release to one path) - `apply_stimulation()` already receives
  `verb`, `region_id` and `intensity` and folds all of it into one integer on
  `vitals["Stimulation"]`, discarding the inputs, while the release check reads
  a bare scalar. There is also no frustration state anywhere, so "other
  stimulation builds frustration" has nowhere to go. Blocked on 545 + 546.

Session work in flight, tracked here so the task tree reflects the state:

- task-542 (review/docs) - `trace` is now `lived_log` and the Soak telemetry is
  a *separate* run-owned system. Two stores answering two questions: a lived log
  is one player's bounded, salience-filtered history; telemetry is the soak
  runner's own record of where everyone was, which a capped per-player log
  cannot answer.
- task-543 (review/testing) - presence intervals + the validated `why` stream.
  The `why` vocabulary was measured against real runs with
  `tools/why_vocabulary.py` rather than guessed, which is how the missing
  `traversal:` / `forage:` / `schedule:` prefixes were found.
- task-544 (inprogress/ui) - the space-time swimlanes.
- task-534 (inprogress/characters) - the missing body reaction (sneeze).

`docs/design/soak-lab-telemetry.md` documents the telemetry schema, the
lived-log/telemetry boundary, the run findings above, and what the space-time
view can and cannot answer.

tasks.py validate: 635 files, 0 errors, 16 pre-existing filename warnings.

commit 4a3fe971b65e44c7b144a4c93882e9f725137010
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-27T14:16:37+02:00

fix(graph): stop re-scaling hand-placed node positions, and let ways move with physics

Three symptoms, one root cause: `properties.x`/`y` carries two different
coordinate spaces and the map layout treated every value in it as the first.

- ENGINE UNITS - the WorldPainter compiler writes `cell * 40`, which the map
  layout scales by the map pitch (task-496/bug-52) and translates by the
  scope's `map_offset` (task-523). The position is the cell.
- CANVAS PIXELS - a node dragged in the graph stores where it was dropped.
  Characters and items are always in this space.

`properties.cell` was meant to tell them apart, but the compiler stamped it on
AREAS ONLY (`world_compile.py`). Generated ways carried bare `x`/`y`, so they
were indistinguishable from a dragged node - and items/characters were never
painted at all, yet still went through the scaling path. Consequences:

1. Ways stopped moving with physics. `_gridUpdates` wrote `physics: false` on
   every node while the comment directly above it said the opposite ("ways,
   items, characters are left free so the solver can pull the loose nodes in").
   vis-network simulates only nodes whose `physics` is strictly true, so the
   global Physics toggle ran the solver over an empty node list and appeared to
   do nothing. task-530 had deliberately reversed the old "ways frozen" model
   and this one line was never updated. The non-painted cardinal path *does*
   set `physics: true` for ways, which is what proved it a regression.

2. Nodes walked further away every time they were moved. Two writers put
   canvas pixels over engine units: `GraphRelativeLayout.frozenDropOps` (no
   painted-coords guard at all, and it runs on every dragEnd) and
   `graph-background.persistPositionsToWorld` (guarded on a bare `props.cell`,
   which misses ways and characters). The next layout then rescaled the pixels
   AND re-added the scope offset, so the inflated value was saved back and the
   cycle repeated. The shipped scenario shows the damage: every character stores
   a negative off-map canvas pixel (`player_Arix` at -477,-405) that no painter
   cell can produce.

3. Once the grid pass hijacked a child's position, `_separate` folded that
   grid position into the leash offset and the Parent Pull target was rebuilt
   from the already-drifted offset, so the creep could never be undone.

The fix removes the double conversion rather than adding a third guard:

- `_gridUpdates` scales and offsets ONLY painted nodes. A non-painted node with
  coords is used verbatim; a node with no coords still falls to the `beside`
  fallback (which this also revives - stale coords had made it unreachable).
- Way physics restored (`physics: isWay && !frozen`); areas stay pinned to their
  cells, because the cells *are* the map and the art is drawn to them. Items and
  characters stay OUT of the solver on purpose: GraphRelativeLayout leashes them
  to their parent and keeps them out of the global gravity field, since a node
  left in it is dragged off its parent however stiff the edge, and holding it
  back would need a per-frame sweep that does not scale.
- Both save sites now use the one shared predicate
  `GraphLayoutEngine.hasPaintedCoords`, so the layout and the save paths cannot
  disagree about which space a node is in.
- New `GraphLayoutEngine.isFrozen` delegates to `GraphRelativeLayout.isStatic`
  rather than duplicating the author-freeze rule a third time.

Deliberately NOT changed: the `_separate` offset ratchet. It looks like the
cause of the creep but is task-511 intent ("fold the result into the offsets so
the next tick reproduces it"); it only misbehaved because the grid pass was
feeding it non-leash positions, which the above removes. The zone-drag maths
were checked and are already correct (fresh snapshot per move, screen->cells
divided by scale and pitch) - not cumulative. `buildNodeConfig` still ignores
`map_offset` in plain Graph view, which is a real inconsistency but a wider
change; left for its own task.

Also corrects Graph System.md, which still documented the retired task-112 model
("areas and ways use physics: false + fixed") and described items/characters as
physics-on - the opposite of what the code has done since task-530.

The compiler half (stamping `cell` on generated ways) is NOT in this commit: it
sits inside the in-flight `elevation` -> `floor` storey rewrite of
`world_compile.py`, and committing the file would sweep that unfinished work in.
Until it lands, generated ways are treated as hand-placed: they stop drifting
but also no longer follow a zone drag. Hand-authored ways keep their own stored
canvas position, which matches the standing rule that a placed way is never
moved for the author.

Tests: node tools/unit/run.cjs 361 passed (13 pre-existing test_plan_tracker
failures, untouched); 4 new tests cover the hand-placed passthrough, way
physics vs pinned areas vs leashed characters, the isFrozen rule, and the
frozenDropOps painted guard. eslint, tsc and the JS module index check clean.

feat(world): every painted cell is a place - roads, prose, scope membership, and map drawing

Completes the task-496 block and the WorldPainter/graph session work that hangs
off it. The observer-view model, locked with the author, now holds end to end.

Compiler (task-496)
- A road cell replaces its biome as the cell's identity: it compiles like any
  other, a run of road merges into one road area, and a road cell beside forest
  stays its own place. The biome survives as `properties.biome` plus context.
- Descriptions compose the place's character from its neighbours and the
  elevation step beside it (woods / forest line / rockface / carried over water),
  deterministically, with no LLM. A neighbour is named for what its place *is*.
- Feature entries carry a narrative phrase from the placement, plus
  `aliases: ["in", "out"]`, so `go in` and `go out` keep resolving.
- The painter's area estimate mirrors the compiler, so the header matches what
  Generate mints.

Authoring (task-528, task-539, task-540, task-541)
- A hand-written area can be placed on a cell; Generate will not mint a second
  place on top of it.
- Scope membership is edited on the node: a Scope section with a dropdown, and
  POST /api/world/scopes/<id>/areas moves a whole selection in one undo step.
  Membership is not placement, so an interior moves without touching the grid.
- Every cell can be read: a hover readout, an Inspect tool, right-click on any
  tool, and a panel with the actions that apply to that cell.
- The place tool's picker is grouped by scope, so a child scope's rooms no longer
  look like the world map's.

Map drawing (bug-51, bug-52, bug-53, task-548)
- Art is re-derived from its scope's grid on load and on a scope switch, so it
  cannot drift onto a different grid than the areas.
- A painted cell always wins over the physics-off flag the scenario builders set,
  so raising Map grid moves the areas with the art.
- Node boxes, item offsets, item rings and the solver spring follow the pitch -
  in the Map layout only.
- Dragging a zone moves *its* art, identified by reference image, instead of
  whichever layer happens to be selected in the panel.
- The match button sets the grid to the image's aspect and says what a shrink
  prunes.

Graph chrome (task-530, 531, 532)
- A three-zone toolbar with progressive disclosure, a contextual scope bar with
  breadcrumb and counts, and an overflow menu on narrow windows.

Tests: 49 compiler, 134 world cluster, 342 JS (13 pre-existing failures).

commit 2e7cfc611f0df7626f13e37db94ac41f920fdef9

test(timeskip): fixtures that measure decay scaling, not the focus rule (task-436)

The minute-scale decay tests placed a *focused* character in a stocked
clearing. A focused character is assumed to have spent the turn's first minute
on their own decision, so at a 1-minute turn it has no deterministic minutes
left (by design) - the fixture was measuring that rule, and the need ladder
would drink away a maxed Thirst before the assertion.

The fixture now sets simulation_mode = "background" and runs the starvation
tests in a food/drink-free "Dry Store Room", so the tests measure decay
scaling as intended.

feat(items): one depletion rule for consume - the engine spends the charge (task-508)

`use` auto-decremented `uses` and removed the item at 0; `eat`/`drink` did
not. So consume depletion had to be hand-authored - and the trigger suggester
told the model NOT to author it, which silently minted infinite food.

- ConsumeActionsMixin._spend_uses() spends one charge exactly as `use` does,
  after the item's own triggers so they can still read the pre-spend count
  (the suggester's `uses_above 0` guard, and `on_depleted` on the way to 0).
  `uses: -1`/absent is "no charge model" and is never spent.
- carcass.json and mansion.json drop their hand-written adjust_uses steps;
  the engine owns the decrement now.

tests/test_consume_contract.py pins the rule; test_consume_depletion.py and
test_camp_consumables.py move to it. tools/author_camp_consumables.py no
longer emits adjust_uses.

feat(engine): keep a character's appearance in step with visible state (task-486)

A character's stored description was only rebuilt on equip/unequip, so a
blush that faded, a condition applied by a trigger, or a body_state edit left
the prose stale until the next equipment change.

  what the description renders (_get_state_hash: equipped stacks and their
  live states, mature_content, visible conditions, region sensitivities), and
  _update_state_description(), which rebuilds only when the hash moved.
- ConditionsSystem refreshes on apply and remove (best-effort - the per-turn
  pass is the safety net for the many paths that apply conditions).
- TickManager reconciles every living character once per turn, so a change
  made by a direct player.conditions write is caught as well.

feat(world): WorldPainter grids, world scopes and per-scope graph loading

A painted world is thousands of cells and, once compiled, thousands of areas -
more than the graph can hold at once and more than a DOM grid could render.

WorldPainter (task-495/496/520):
- engine/world_grid.py + routes/world_grid*.py: scoped grid payloads (world /
  town / interior), one-request batch paint, and a route tool that reports its
  length in cells · turns · hours (1 cell = 1 turn, 60 turns/hour).
- static/js/worldpainter/{grid-model,editor}.js: a Konva canvas surface, so a
  16k-cell grid renders without one DOM node per cell - the old 60x60 DOM cap
  is gone.
- engine/world_compile.py + engine/generation.py: per-scope compile, so the
  root stays coarse and each zone holds its detail rather than one generate
  minting thousands of rooms.
- app.py: the grid handlers snapshot their own PRE-state, so Undo is not
  shadowed by the generic graph push.

World scopes (task-397/400/439):
- engine/world_scopes.py + routes/world_scopes*.py: the scope hierarchy, a
  flat depth-first listing, and a vis-loadable subgraph per scope.
- The graph view gains a scope picker (#graph-scope-filter) and loads only the
  selected scope's slice; loading a save drops a stale pick and rebuilds it.

Content/tooling: generation tags, a scenario catalogue, and the sweep helpers
(tools/file_tree.py, tools/scenario_stats.py).

Verified: tests/test_world_{grid,grid_routes,compile,scopes}.py 96 passed;
tools/unit 226 passed (13 pre-existing test_plan_tracker.js failures);
tools/js_module_index.py --check clean; npm run lint clean.

commit 2b608a83952f61fadaef424cb4d6fe1f60f8f028
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-24T18:44:17+02:00
