fix(timeskip): world advance soaks everyone (task-436 root cause)

advance_world just called tick_turn(), leaving every character 'active'. A
focused character gets minutes_in_turn - 1 minutes of action time in
process_due, which is 1 - 1 = 0 at a 1-minute tick - so a fine-grained world
advance decayed every drive without ever serving it. After one camp day at
T=1: Energy 0, Hunger 56, Thirst 78, Bladder 100, while T=15 sat near Energy
50 / Hunger 31 / Thirst 34 / Bladder 81. Energy was a symptom, not the cause.

advance_world now marks everyone 'background' for the span (restoring the
modes after, so the persisted attribute is unchanged). Two-day camp soak at
T=1 vs T=15: Energy 73.9/73.1, Hunger 17.0/17.0, Thirst 23.8/25.4, Bladder
33.9/37.7, Hygiene 60.6/73.1, worst gap 12.5.

Tests: a 2-day T=1-vs-T=15 comparison (tolerance 15) and a mode-restoration
check. The three pre-existing failures in test_tick_time_scaling (direct
tick_turn cases and the grace accumulator) are unchanged.

feat(timeskip): run spans beyond one day in chunked advances (task-482)

The blocking request cap was 1,440 minutes even though the engine supports a
week. A blocking span now runs up to timeskip.MAX_MINUTES (10,080) in day-sized
chunks, merged into one envelope; an interrupt in a chunk ends the skip early.
The shared-world branch declares a soak order for the full span and
_declared_span uses the same ceiling.

Tests: a two-day world advance reports elapsed_minutes == 2880; 20,000 min is
rejected with max_minutes == 10080. Still open on task-482: vendor leisure
buying and explore-prefers-frontier (blocked on task-398). The task-436 Energy
residual was not folded in - cause still unidentified, next step is a
measurement harness, not another guess. Suite 61 failed / 3798 passed.

feat(graph): implement short-range node separation

Add a new separation module to prevent item and character nodes from
overlapping in crowded areas. The system uses a smoothed, eased
approach to push nearby nodes apart unless they are connected by an
edge, preventing visual clutter while maintaining hierarchical
relationships.

- Implement `GraphSeparation` for repulsion logic
- Integrate separation pass into `GraphRelativeLayout`
- Add configurable repulsion parameters (min/max distance, strength,
  pull) to the engine config and UI settings
- Add unit tests for separation easing and settling behavior
- Update item library and engine configuration to reflect new
  structural standards

commit 52515fe368e1b03221f70a771226ee8cd219a9f8
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-24T17:30:18+02:00
Refs:   

fix(graph): reverse inverted container edges on load (bug-44)

Legacy contains edges (container -> contained) were relabelled to in
without swapping endpoints, but canonical in is contained -> container.
The migrated edges froze backwards in saves, so authored container contents
(Backpack -> Book, grandfather_clock -> Brass Key, ...) were invisible to
take/examine/search/lighting/reach.

- graph.py normalize_edges: the contains branch now swaps source/target.
- graph.py normalize_in_edge_directions (called by load_from_dict): reverses
  already-relabelled item -> item in edges whose source is placed (in an
  area, or carrying/equipped on a character) and whose target is not.
- Nested containers and canonical edges are untouched.

Tests: tests/test_spatial_edges.py (5 direction cases). Full suite unchanged
at 61 failed / 3758 passed (pre-existing failures only).

commit 432d3179ebde1f10753fa13600df48a5eef24a23
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-24T17:21:15+02:00
Refs:   

fix(saveload): unique save filenames and unicode-aware sanitizer (bug-43)

Named saves used a second-resolution timestamp with no uniqueness guard, so
two saves in the same second silently clobbered each other. The filename
sanitizer also kept only ASCII alphanumerics, collapsing non-ASCII names to
underscores (Draghal, AEro kysten).

- routes/helpers.py: shared sanitize_filename() (NFKC, keeps unicode letters,
  Windows-safe, reserved-stem guard) and unique_filename() (_2/_3 suffixes).
- routes/saveload.py: all four duplicate sanitizer sites use the shared helper;
  rename refuses to clobber an existing save.
- Collisions disambiguate the filename only; _save_metadata.name is untouched.
- Slot/autosave overwrites still write in place.

Tests: tests/test_saveload.py TestSanitizeFilename, TestSaveFilenameCollisions,
TestSafeSavePath (32 passing).

fix(graph): node images never rendered (vis group shape overrode per-node shape)

vis-network's group options override a node's own shape, so every image node
(shape circularImage) was drawn as its group shape (ellipse/diamond/triangle)

clips to the circle with a state-colored border. Fixes character and item art.

commit 840b8ec362d535e60fdbc1685d52c6eacb86ce34

Date:   2026-09-24T11:32:30+02:00
Refs:   

fix(triggers): trigger graph compiles NO branches and OR/NOT groups honestly (task-501)

triggerToGraph no longer keeps only conditions[0]: every leaf is drawn, and an imported condition tree the linear chain cannot draw (OR/NOT, or an AND nesting one) is stored on the trigger node and re-emitted verbatim. _traceGraph now collects NO-branch effects and builds a problems list: a lone NO message becomes fail_message, anything else is a refusal. compileToEngine reports refusal via compile_error instead of silently flattening to AND or truncating effects; fail_message is only set from a lone NO message. The four save paths surface the refusal (TG.reportCompileError), so a dishonest compile cannot be saved.

feat(world): atomic fidelity transitions and scope activation boundary (task-399)

Tier changes are queued (promotion.request) and applied as one batch by promotion.flush at the top of tick_turn, before any character is processed, so activate/offload is atomic and a character cannot act twice in a turn under two modes. activate_scope queues promotion for a scope's background residents, skipping soak-ordered and attended characters; POST /api/world/scopes/<id>/observe exposes it. Promotion memory is written at the flush boundary.

feat(world): promotion/demotion memory bridge for background characters (task-399)

Add engine/promotion.py: offload() demotes a character and stamps the span boundary; promote() consolidates the background trace into one bounded source:background memory and hands control back. The span is t > max(last_offload_tick, background_consolidated_through), so foreground actions are never summarized and repeated activation cannot duplicate a memory. Deterministic templated summary, no LLM.

Player gains last_offload_tick / background_consolidated_through (serialized and restored); structure residents now demote through the seam. task-412 is folded into task-399.

commit 4ff1bc778d145341958d1f7f871cb53b1e7af369
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>

fix(ui): keep the event log per world so exports stop mixing scenarios

Reported: "something went pretty wrong? im in world_template, but it seems
to have soaked on goblin camp?"

The world was fine: data/autosave.json is world_template (Kaelen Voss,
Lyrie, rat; 19 areas; _scenario_name world_template) and its game_log has
zero Krikka entries. What mixed was the client-side event stream, which is
what the Export button writes: main.js restores it from IndexedDB at startup
(line 806) and re-saves it every 5s (line 839), and it was only cleared when
a scenario was opened through the scenario manager. Any other world change
(server restart auto-loading a different world, a save load) left the
previous world's bubbles in place, so an export of world_template could
contain kraktooth_goblin_camp's turns.

StreamPersistence now stamps the persisted stream with the world key
(_scenario_name, falling back to scenario_source / body dataset) and drops
the stored log when the world differs - on both persist and restore, so a
reload in the same world still keeps its log.

Verified live: persist in world_template kept 86 entries; switching the
world to kraktooth_goblin_camp dropped the stored log to 0; a stale log from
another world was not restored; the same world kept its entries.

bug-46

commit 2634211a0dfe29c7704d521b9db1ae01bcc4c43c
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T15:43:45+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): per-node physics and distance control

"no way to calculate per parent node? ... to be able to say these nodes are
static and placed at xy, but these use physics and should want to be so and
so far from each other or so and so close to their connected nodes".

The layout now reads per-node intent out of node properties:

- layout_static: true (or central_gravity_enabled: false) - placed, not
  simulated; GraphRelativeLayout.isStatic accepts either spelling.
- layout_distance (child) - how far this node sits from what holds it.
- layout_child_distance (parent) - how far this parent's contents sit.
- layout_child_spacing (parent) - the gap between this parent's contents.
- layout_min_radius / layout_max_radius (parent) - clamp this parent's ring.

Precedence: child's own layout_distance > parent's layout_child_distance >
the graph-wide Item Edge Length. An explicitly set distance is exact (80
means 80, labels may overlap - that is what hugging means); the comfort
floor/cap and crowd-spacing growth apply only to inherited distances, so the
default arrangement stays readable.

Inspector: the Graph Physics section gains the three numbers, each clearing
back to inherited when blanked, and changing one calls
GraphRelativeLayout.reseed() so the arrangement re-derives at once.

Verified live on area_living_room (10 children): inherited 146-147px, room
asks 80/40 -> 79-81px, room asks 320 -> 319-320px, one item asks 90 while
the rest inherit -> that item 90 and the others 320.

tools/unit 162 passed; lint, typecheck, module-index clean.

task-485

commit 33e58dd7cc121221d5411c5df99e3255b0ffe6ce
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T15:37:46+02:00
Refs:   

fix(graph): make the graph physics settings actually reach the contents

Reported: "does these take the hug parent and other such settings into
account, or honestly you forgot about them didn't you?" - correct, the
contents ignored them. Keeping children out of the solver is what stops them
drifting to the middle, but it also meant the edges' rest length
("Item Edge Length", labelled Hug Parent <-> Stretched) had no physical
effect, because their positions came from fixed orbit constants.

The setting now drives the orbit:

- the ring radius is the item-edge-length value (Hug Parent <-> Stretched),
  floored and capped so it is never a pile on the label nor stretched across
  the map;
- spacing scales with it too, so a short rest length genuinely pulls a
  crowded room tight (labels may overlap - that is what hugging means) and a
  long one spreads it out;
- nested contents orbit their container proportionally smaller.

Also: changing a graph setting now calls GraphRelativeLayout.reseed() before
the rebuild, because remembered offsets used to win, so pulling the slider
did nothing visible.

Measured live on the living room's 10 items: length 25 -> 61px, 60 -> 146px,
200 -> 488px, and back to 60 -> 147px.

Still true and worth knowing: the other physics sliders (Spring Length,
Repulsion, Damping, Spring Stiffness, Solver) govern areas and ways, which
stay in the solver - not contents, by design.

tools/unit 159 passed; lint, typecheck, module-index clean.

bug-45

commit 407051c7171237b2ce2b7b61e576726f6024dcb8
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T15:25:15+02:00
Refs:   

fix(graph): cardinal layout only auto-moves nodes in map mode (bug-45)

applyCardinalLayout ran on every graph rebuild whenever the cardinal flag
was set, so in the manual graph view it re-anchored areas, ways and loose
nodes over the top of hand-placed positions - which is what moved a placed
way even with physics on.

It is now gated on map mode: the Map button (_cardinalLayout) or the map
view overlay (_viewMode === 'cardinal'). Those are two separate signals -
the Map button does not set _viewMode, so gating on _viewMode alone stopped
map mode laying out at all; caught by testing the toggle path.

Verified by instrumenting the call: graph-mode rebuild 0 calls, Map button
on 1, map-mode rebuild 1, back to graph mode 0. A displaced area also stayed
exactly where it was put through a graph-mode rebuild.

commit b1309872d20479862a45f06f02564742d01675dc
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T15:21:19+02:00
Refs:   

fix(graph): honour a frozen node in the layouts (bug-45)

"Disable physics" on a way did not work: the way still got pushed. The
inspector's Physics-enabled toggle stores central_gravity_enabled=false,
and the node loader correctly builds it with physics:false - but
applyCardinalLayout then rebuilt wayUpdates/looseUpdates with an anchor
position and `physics: true, fixed: {x: false, y: false}`, undoing the
freeze on every graph rebuild (and re-anchoring the placed way).

- applyCardinalLayout now filters out any node with
  central_gravity_enabled === false, leaving its position and physics
  exactly as they are.
- GraphRelativeLayout.apply skips frozen nodes too, and records no offset
  for them, so the follow pass ignores them as well.
- dragging a frozen node now writes its position to properties.x/y
  (frozenDropOps/persistFrozenDrop on dragEnd): nothing else restores it,
  because the layout deliberately leaves frozen nodes alone, so without
  this a reload would lose the placement.

Verified live through the inspector's own API: after the freeze the node
is physics:false, drifts 0px over 2.5s, and stays at exactly the placed
position through a full loadGraphData() rebuild. An unfrozen way is still
re-anchored (intended). tools/unit 157 passed; lint, typecheck,
module-index clean.

commit 187967feab2d02d4671be13bd89ea1a4f44bf540
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T15:15:47+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): orbit a room's contents instead of stacking them on its label

Free mode arranged a room's contents in a compact block below the node,
which piled items on top of the room's own label and on each other. They now
orbit it:

- evenly spaced on a ring, starting at the top and going clockwise;
- the radius grows with how many things are in the room so labels stay
  apart (`spacing`-derived), and is capped at ORBIT.maxRadius so a crowded
  room never stretches contents across the map;
- contents nested inside a container orbit that container on a smaller ring
  (depth >= 2);
- the parent-relative offset mechanism is unchanged, so orbits still follow
  a dragged room and a hand-dropped item keeps its place.

Measured live: area_living_room with 10 items puts every item at radius
146px (the spacing-derived ideal) with a 90px minimum gap between
neighbours; 60 items stay capped at 320px.

tools/unit 155 passed (three orbit cases: lone child on the minimum ring,
ring widening with the crowd and staying capped, nested ring tighter);
lint, typecheck, module-index clean.

task-485

commit 0767e3f2d3def3816e96d54c380698810eed906e
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T15:10:54+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): hierarchical (level) layout mode, switchable with free physics

Adds a `graph_layout_mode` setting with two modes, toggled by a new toolbar
`Levels` button and persisted:

- free (default): the force layout owns the areas and contents hold a
  parent-relative offset (unchanged behaviour).
- levels: vis hierarchical layout with physics forced off, so every node is
  placed by relation level - rooms on top, their contents below, nested
  contents below that, triggers below their host.

Hierarchical levels come from edge direction and the stored direction is not
trustworthy, so layout edges are oriented from the resolved relations
instead: attachment edges parent -> child, and room-to-door edges with the
door as the child (those are stored both ways round and the 2-cycle was what
scrambled levels: 153/197 -> 180/197 -> 100/100 visible children below their
parent).

Fixed four paths that silently re-enabled physics and would have dragged
nodes off their levels: GraphLayoutEngine.applyCardinalLayout (not called in
levels mode), _applyLockState "unfreezing" for a new world, the persisted
graph.physics_enabled preference applied on load and on view-mode switches,
and switching back to free leaving layout.hierarchical.enabled true because
vis merges options.

Verified live on the autosave: levels -> physics false, hierarchical true,
100/100 visible children below their parent, 0 nodes drifted in 2.5s; free
-> physics true, hierarchical false, contents back to 58/110px median/max.
Switching both ways is clean. tools/unit 154 passed; lint, typecheck,
module-index clean.

task-485

commit ffc4c13b6345f64230b0c9420285b75080aded6f
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T14:53:30+02:00
Refs:   

perf(graph): incremental child follow instead of a per-frame graph sweep

The follow pass walked every child every frame, copied every node's
position to do it, and moved nodes on an afterDrawing hook - which could
re-trigger a redraw off its own moves. At a large graph that is a
per-frame tax over the whole node set, and idle graphs could spin.

Now:

- children are kept out of the *global* solver (fixed: false, physics:
  false) rather than being held in it and corrected every frame. Central
  gravity is a field applied to every node every iteration, so a child
  left in it is dragged off its parent however stiff its edge is, and
  holding it back needs a per-child sweep - the thing that does not
  scale. The parent is still physics-driven and the child follows it; a
  dragged child re-records its offset from where it was dropped, so
  contents stay dynamic with their room and hand-arrangeable.
- the pass is incremental: only parents that moved since the last tick
  are visited, no positions are copied, and work is capped at
  FOLLOW_BUDGET per tick with the remainder queued for the next tick.
- the timer sleeps entirely when physics is off and nothing is pending,
  and wakes on drag, dragEnd and stabilization.

Measured on the autosave (235 nodes): children sit 19-110px from their
parent, identical after 2.5s (no drift) and through moving the room by
(380,-160); an idle tick is 0.03ms.

Recorded negative result: rebalancing the global physics cannot fix
clusters - springConstant 0.04->0.14 and centralGravity 0.3->0.06
(applied and verified in network.physics.options) changed the settled
layout by 2px out of ~5900. vis-network exposes no per-cluster force, so
the parent-relative offset is the only per-cluster control.

Also repairs the BOM/mojibake my earlier PowerShell writes introduced into
relative-layout.js, test_relative_layout.js and the task file.

tools/unit 151 passed; lint, typecheck, module-index clean.

task-485

commit e543cda4125731accb1027c89df6f227a17b6f31
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T14:37:11+02:00
Refs:   

fix(graph): keep room contents dynamic, leashed by parent-relative offset

Pinning the children (fixed, physics off) was the wrong shape: they need to
keep moving. Now a child holds a relative offset from its parent and that
offset is re-applied to the parent's live position on every drawn frame
(30ms timer as the floor for a settled network, which stops redrawing), so:

- children stay dynamic (no `fixed`, physics on, draggable) while global
  central gravity can neither drag them to the middle nor stretch them
  across the map;
- dragging a room carries its contents with it exactly;
- dragging a child keeps the place it was dropped - the offset is
  recomputed from the drop, so a hand-arranged room still follows.

Nested children resolve shallowest-first against the map updated in the
same pass, so an item inside a container inside a room is not placed
against a stale parent position.

Also straightens attachment edges (`in`, `equipped`, `carrying`, `at`,
`triggers`, `grappled`) and shortens their springs (35): vis's default
"dynamic" smoothing was bowing every containment edge outwards, which is
where the U-turns came from. Room-to-room connection edges keep their curve.

Measured live on the autosave: 0 pinned / 197 dynamic children, child-parent
distance median 58px (p90 78, max 110) and unchanged after moving a room,
and moving area_living_room by (350,-150) moved its item by exactly
(350,-150). tools/unit 149 passed; lint, typecheck, module-index clean.

task-485

commit 8f6c9ca4269b79de6fd4602dc08f12787504e6c4
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T14:25:11+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): derive node positions from relations, not saved snapshots

Items, characters and triggers were collected in the middle of the canvas:
nothing anchored them to a parent. layout-engine seeded item/character
positions once near the parent anchor with `physics:true, fixed:false`, so
global central gravity pulled every leaf node to the graph's centre of
mass while the many-linked areas spread out. A saved `properties.x/y` is
only a starting position (network-manager.js:533), so locking was the only
thing that made them hold - and a snapshot is wrong the moment someone
picks an item up and walks off with it.

graph/relative-layout.js derives the position instead:

- parent resolution by relation priority carrying > equipped > at > in >
  triggers, so a carried bag rides its carrier even when a stale `in` edge
  still points at the room it was left in;
- `in` has no reliable direction in the data (Backpack -> Ink but
  fireplace -> living_room), so for `in` the end nearer the area roots is
  the parent, and only a strictly shallower node may be a parent, which
  leaves a two-node container cycle parentless rather than each holding
  the other;
- ways sit at their rooms' midpoint and are placed first, so a trigger
  hosted by a way has somewhere to sit;
- children sit on a deterministic ring (130/88/62 by depth, wider when
  crowded) and are held (`fixed`, physics off); areas keep physics and stay
  the only world coordinates;
- re-derived on load, on stabilizationIterationsDone and on dragEnd, and
  positions are read from the node bodies so a child of a hidden node is
  still placed.

Verified live on the real autosave (235 nodes): 0 orphans (items 97/97,
characters 3/3, triggers 97/97, ways 19/19), children drift 0px over 3s
with area physics on, moving area_living_room by (+400,+200) carried all 10
of its items by exactly that, and median item distance from the centroid
went 411px -> 1486px.

Also files bug-44: the save's authored container contents
(item_Backpack -> item_Ink, 21 edges) are stored opposite to canonical
EDGE_IN, so the engine's get_edges_for_target(container, EDGE_IN) readers
cannot see them.

tools/unit 146 passed; lint, typecheck and the module-index contract clean.

commit e7784c5f5ee2b6b614b6a4fc1df62d97619c2177
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T14:12:10+02:00
Refs:   

feat(soak): risky ground rolls, routine ground takes 10 (task-475)

New engine/traversal.py maps soak actions to skills and decides one hop.
Routine ground takes 10 and never rolls, so the tier stays dice-free like
routine maintenance. Risky ground does roll:

- HAZARD_SKILLS: an area tag picks the skill that carries you through
  (Survival for wild country, Athletics for height/water, Acrobatics for
  balance/squeeze, Stealth for watched ground) at HAZARD_DC; a failure
  leaves exactly one minor condition (wet / injured).
- way_kind: a way needing crawl/climb/jump is crossed with that verb
  instead of stalling on "you need to climb through the north".
- traversal.hop never raises: a blocked or failed crossing costs the turn,
  is remembered (note_refusal/avoid, 30 game minutes) and one alternative
  hop is tried at once as a detour, so travel routes around it rather than
  walking into the same wall every turn.

Wired through BackgroundSimulation._travel_toward/_travel_to_area (new
_hop) and timeskip._move, so explore and heading travel are covered too;
_target_step gained avoid.

While validating: previously a blocked way made a soak order burn its
whole span standing still with no trace.

tests/test_traversal.py 12 passed; traversal/soak/timeskip/background/
forage/loot/checks/skills slice 331 passed.

commit b2933115e8cf35c150aecedd2eac2a5610d3042c
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T13:28:10+02:00
Refs:   

feat(ui): cancel a soak order from the roster row

The initiative-list soak badge gains a ✕ that drops the order:
- DELETE /api/world/soak?character=<name> cancels that character's order
  (defaults to the active character; 404 for an unknown name, a clean no-op
  when there was nothing to cancel).

task-481

commit 5b331178bb39ad1e6bce7888799fc1353803dd61
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T13:25:53+02:00
Refs:   

feat(ui): re-queue a promoted character by the normal order rule

When a soak order ends (promotion or expiry) the character re-enters the turn
queue by whatever order mode is active, rather than needing bespoke placement:

- sequential -> already alphabetic, nothing to do
- random -> already shuffled, nothing to do
- initiative -> the promoted character rolls a fresh d20 + DEX bonus and the
  queue re-sorts with the current character kept current
- simultaneous -> the queue is unused; the character resumes its own cadence

`TurnQueue.syncSoakPromotions()` detects the soaking->not-soaking transition
from the state payload and is driven from `reconcile()` (which runs on every
state fetch); a dead character leaving soak is not treated as a promotion.

tools/unit: turn-queue.js is now loaded in the sandbox; 3 soak-queue cases
(initiative re-roll keeps current, sequential untouched, dead ignored) ->
133 passed. lint / typecheck / module-index clean.

commit 3d2c1be262c8cb51f29c6f29a9dbcaa2ba8fbd8d
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T13:16:39+02:00
Refs:   

feat(sim): per-character soak orders (multi-human timeskip)

A timeskip in a shared world is an order on one character, declared on that
player's turn, not a table consensus and not a blocking server jump.

- engine/soak.py: declare/cancel/remaining/apply_orders. An order carries intent,
  span, target/heading/watch tags and a discovery baseline; apply_orders runs
  from tick_turn after the background pass, one policy step per turn (reusing
  timeskip.run_policy_step), decrementing by the frame length.
- While an order runs the character is GENUINELY simulation_mode: background
  (soak tier drives it, the LLM loop does not ask it for decisions); the previous
  mode is restored when the order ends or is cancelled. process_due skips ordered
  characters so the generic need policy never competes with the declared intent.
- Promotion on fear, hostile condition, critical vital, or a watched discovery:
  order cleared, frightened applied for fear, one resume memory, a `soak_end`
  turn event - and NO table pause. The character is simply re-queued and acts on
  its next turn by normal turn order.
- API: POST/DELETE /api/world/soak. POST /api/world/timeskip now declares an
  order when another attended human exists (instead of freezing them and 409ing
  them), and still fast-forwards when nothing attended remains - the blocking
  jump is demoted to that degenerate fast path.

commit b1b7981c55771e476e86666465e685634ac66b5f
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T09:40:12+02:00
Refs:   

docs(sim): timeskip follows the frame dial (clock consistency)

commit 0e7949333363e6324ede741d69c92c18759514b5
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-23T09:40:03+02:00
Refs:   

fix(sim): timeskip honours the frame dial so the clock stays consistent

The skip forced `time_per_tick_minutes = 1` for its span, but the world clock is
derived from ticks x dial, so restoring the dial retroactively rescaled the
skip: a 70-minute skip in a 5-minute world read as 350 minutes afterwards, and
the whole history shifted with it. Observed live (world_template dial=5: 08:00
+ 70 ticks showed 13:50).

A skip now advances whole turns of the scenario's length and never touches the
dial: elapsed_minutes = ticks x dial, so 120 minutes is 24 turns at dial 5 and
120 at dial 1. Interrupt resolution follows the dial; atomic actions keep their
own 1-minute durations inside the flow either way. Also passes the frame length
to the policy as `remaining` so a 2-minute drink is not forced into an activity
at a 5-minute frame.

Verified live: 120-min idle skip -> tick 70->72, 13:50->14:00 (10 min), the rat
moved and started eating, NPCs travelled, and the player was interrupted on
involuntary:bladder.

tests/test_timeskip.py -> 41 passed. Full suite 60 failed / 3379 passed (same
60 pre-existing).

Engine slice of the timeskip actions: declare an intent plus a span and the

  timeskip, the future fidelity tiers and soak deferral.
- engine/timeskip.py (task-464 engine core): advance(gs, minutes, intent=...)
  with idle / leisure / search / explore / travel. Forces 1-minute ticks for
  the skip and restores the scenario's time_per_tick_minutes afterwards; one
  active skip at a time; summary lines from the trace. No stasis: vitals decay
  exactly as in normal play.
- engine/background_simulation.py: public policy hooks (take_action,
  step_toward_area, step_toward_tags, find_matching) so the skip rides the
  same deterministic decisions, and the leash fix below.
- Fix a latent bug: the soak runner's "never puppet the human" guard compared
  identity against gs.active_player, which is a registry *key* (a string), so
  it never matched and a human-driven character was simulated behind the
  player's back. It now uses the documented marker `autonomy is False`

- tests/test_timeskip.py: 19 tests for the evaluator and the runner.

commit 53420e657c9bc89940d6e24434e18f6599a98a2c
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-22T21:18:43+02:00
Refs:   

feat(engine): implement character identity collapse and simultaneous turn modes

This commit introduces a major refactor to character identity management and
expands the turn-based engine with simultaneous execution modes.

Key changes:
- **Character Identity Refactor**: Implements a canonical identity system
  where authored `character_<slug>` nodes and runtime `player_<Name>`
  anchors are collapsed into a single identity. Includes alias resolution
  in the graph to prevent dangling references and a migration tool.
- **Simultaneous Turn Modes**: Adds support for "Simultaneous" (global
  countdown-based) and "Simultaneous per room" (independent room
  resolution) turn modes via a new UI turn-mode dial and `VWSimultaneous`
  module.
- **NL Editor Enhancements**:
  - Adds bulk mutation support (`update_matching_nodes`) using selectors.
  - Implements property-level diff previews for staged operations.
  - Adds a validation gate that prevents applying invalid batches and
    surfaces errors in the UI.
  - Improves character node authoring by mirroring graph property
    patches directly onto live Player objects.
- **Soak Lab**: Introduces a new "Soak Lab" standalone analytics UI and
  engine-level runner for headless simulation testing.
- **Library Ops**: Refactors library management to support batch
  operations and improved tag validation.

commit 34758364e4c84b5ce490ece4815961429dcc69a7
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-22T15:04:45+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): export the graph as a high-resolution PNG

Add a 📷 PNG toolbar action (and a ⋯ More item) that renders the graph
area to a PNG including the map background layers.

vis-network derives its canvas resolution from devicePixelRatio alone, so
the on-screen canvas cannot be captured above screen resolution. Instead
graph-export.js rebuilds the currently rendered nodes/edges into a hidden
N-times-larger vis.Network, renders at 1x/2x/3x, then composites the map
layers (Canvas2D) under the offscreen vis canvas. A small dialog chooses
whole-graph (fit all visible) or current-view scope; output is capped at
an 8192px backing store.

- new static/js/graph/graph-export.js (GraphExport)
- graph-background: getExportLayers() accessor (edited in .ts, rebuilt)
- world-export: saveFileWithDialog now maps .png to image/png
- tools/unit: crop/clamp/view-scale helper tests

commit 3ac07ba03c5118f0e004f83bc3a5d1cb7b60479a
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-22T14:47:39+02:00
Refs:   

fix(graph): physics toggle showed the previously inspected node's state (bug-37)

The checkbox used a boolean-attribute binding (?checked), which lit diffs against
the value it last bound rather than the DOM, and setCentralGravity never
re-rendered the inspector. Unchecking node A left the bound value true, so
selecting node B - also true - was not a change and lit skipped the write,
leaving A's unchecked box on screen until the panel was rebuilt.

Bind through live(), which compares against the element's current property on
every render, so the control cannot inherit another node's state.

Also relabels it 'Physics enabled': the stored key is vis-network's per-node
physics flag, not a gravity strength, which is what the old label implied.

commit b74c73cdc7e6586d4f940c0facda118f19edc282
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-22T14:47:34+02:00
Refs:   

fix(ui): stop the inspector header crushing the title and node ID (bug-38)

.inspector-header is a nowrap flex row and the title/ID column is flex:1, i.e.
1 1 0%, so it took only the leftover space: at a 319px panel the column was 44px,
the name input 44px (its content is 196px) and the node-ID input 9px - exactly
one character, which is why a node ID read as 'a' out of
area_abandoned_hunter's_cabin.

The header now wraps, the column keeps a 140px floor so the action buttons drop
to their own line, and the node-ID input fills its row instead of fighting the
sync button. Measured after: name 220px, node ID 186px.

commit 83225542781fb19f63b921daf76a967170cb6b93
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-22T14:47:29+02:00
Refs:   

feat(graph): multiple map layers per scenario with easy placement (task-451)

A scenario can carry several reference images instead of one. The world block
graph_background becomes {layers, positions, layoutLocked}: array order is
z-order, each layer has its own rect/rotation/crop/opacity/visibility/label and
lock, and a legacy single-image block migrates on read (its locked was always
the node-layout freeze, so it becomes layoutLocked).

The images paint beneath the vis canvas, which owns pointer events - so the old
drag target and handles were unreachable and a map could not be dragged at all.
The paint layer is now purely visual; an above-canvas interact layer carries one
hit box per visible layer plus the active layer's handles.

- select topmost, drag, alt-click to cycle overlaps, corner scale, rotate, crop
- arrow-key nudge, snapping to layers and node bounds, 10px handles at any zoom
- layer panel: visibility, lock, rename, reorder on drop, remove, add
- world-first persistence, per-scenario cache fallback, empty list clears

Also fixes bug-39: a scenario with no map serializes {}, which is truthy, so the
old code skipped both the cache lookup and the clear.

graph-background.ts is now the source and graph-background.js is generated by
npm run build:ts; ApiClient and appEvents were typed into globals.d.ts.

Restart the app to pick up the new save contract in routes/graph_ops.py.

feat(time): tasks longer than their timeframe span turns (task-436)

A task whose duration exceeds what is left of the timeframe now starts an

is mandatory, since a type missing from it never expires and strands the character
busy -- and in ACTIVITY_SKIP_TURNS.

commit 6efb651af100f5ebfa27ca787c3077ec183f0088
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T20:43:10+02:00
Refs:   

docs(tasks): root-cause the residual resolution dependence (task-436)

Two fixes for it were attempted after the flow model landed and both measured
worse, so both were reverted:

- gating on the action's own duration rather than the per-turn served set: Energy
  64.0/75.6 and Thirst 17.6/12.1 for T=1/T=15, against 67.1/77.3 for served. Too
  permissive -- a character above the thirst threshold drinks every two minutes.
- a TASK_COOLDOWN_MINUTES routine table: Energy 60.7/80.3, Hygiene 55.8/69.3.
  More realistic intervals, worse convergence.

The real cause is an overdraft: at T=1 the timeframe is a minute and eat reports
ten, and the loop spends it anyway, so a character does fifteen whole tasks in
fifteen minutes at T=1 while T=15 admits only what fits. The binding constraint
at T=15 is the timeframe, which is why no cooldown value reconciles them.

The fix is the one the model implies: a task longer than the remaining timeframe
spans turns as an activity, as eight hours of sleep already does. served stays as
the shipping rule, being best-measured and simplest.

commit fe3e1c31d9faab10917fa311e4e2203c693f4b9d
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T20:33:01+02:00
Refs:   

feat(time): a turn is a timeframe filled by an action flow (task-436)

Replaces the per-turn action budget (actions_per_turn) with minutes_in_turn plus
TASK_MINUTES, each action's duration. process_due fills the timeframe: actions
consume their duration until it is full or nothing is due. Travel is one minute
and repeats, so a walk fills a timeframe; every other task is recorded in served
and done once per timeframe. The _action_credit banking machinery is gone.

Measured over a game week at 15 min/turn: same survival and Hunger, but Energy
69.8 to 77.3, Sanity 80.4 to 85.9, Social 75.8 to 81.0 -- a long timeframe chains
different tasks instead of re-running one need ladder. T=1 and T=15 agree on
survival and Hunger; Energy still differs ~10 points, recorded as an open
follow-up since served is per turn rather than per timeframe.

Coarser turns are also much cheaper for equal game time: a week in 17s at 15
min/turn against 2m43s at 1 min/turn.

This task overstated its own bug: it claimed a 30-minute turn meant thirty meals
and 1440 actions/day. _act returns early when nothing is past threshold, so the
old budget burned T passes through the ladder but produced similar actions. The
old model was incoherent, not frantic. Corrected in the task file.

commit ebba09bf936f1b7b3a15d93f384fd0b082ddcac9
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T20:19:34+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(time): one owner for the clock; rest keeps its guard (task-436)

Step 4 was premised on _action_time_consumed becoming uniformly true and
therefore meaningless. That was wrong: the flag had two sources. apply_action
set it from the cost table's time field -- the part that made which actions moved
the clock an accident of the table -- and rest() sets it because rest loops
tick_turn once per minute itself.

So the flag was renamed _clock_advanced_by_task rather than deleted, and only the
cost-table source was removed. Making _ensure_tick unconditional, the obvious
reading of delete the flag, would have made a 60-minute rest cost 61 minutes on
every sleep.

consumes_time is gone too: it existed only to feed the old flag. ACTION_COSTS
entries are now bare energy values.

test_rest_advances_the_clock_itself_and_is_marked_as_having_done_so pins the
invariant. Full suite 3186 passed; the 6 failures in test_social_company and
test_tick_time_scaling are pre-existing and order-dependent.

refactor(time): absolute vital costs and an explicit consumes_time field (task-436)

ACTION_COSTS carried a time field doing double duty: it multiplied every vital
cost and served as the this-action-advanced-the-clock flag. So move cost 1 while
fumble cost 6 (3 x time:2) -- the table read as totals while behaving as
per-minute rates.

Split it: energy costs are absolute, and whether an action takes a minute is now
an explicit consumes_time field, defaulting to false when absent so the old
absent-or-zero-time behaviour is preserved. The three call sites using the
time-zero idiom now say consumes_time False.

Verified behaviour-preserving against HEAD with an end-to-end probe over move,
fumble, open, take and look: identical energy deltas and identical flags. The
scenario and library data audit came back negative, so no migration is needed.

tests/test_action_costs.py pins the split and fails if a time key returns.

commit c94ea5c0fc84bc42043fc4125b3d808a78d7da93
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T20:08:17+02:00
Refs:   

docs(model): write the simulation model; mark the action budget as interim (task-436)

One entity at four processing levels, sharing one clock and one action flow.
Cognition refines but never creates capability, so scale is bounded by attention
rather than world size. A turn is a timeframe filled by an action flow, survival
is slack-and-emptiness rather than a drain rate, and routine satisfies survival
by default so that deprivation - not maintenance - is the crisis.

Marks the per-turn action budget as superseded debt: budgeting one-minute actions
with no durations gives a 30-minute turn thirty actions, and ~1,440 actions per
character per game day. The test that asserted this now says so, so implementing
the flow model fails loudly instead of drifting.

Focused and unfocused characters sharing one executor stays: it is what makes the
four levels one scale rather than four entity types.

task-436 scopes the replacement: absolute vital costs (now that `time` no longer
scales them), delete `_action_time_consumed`, and put duration on tasks.

commit 376d9354c06ecf02c50084dfc45403418985114d
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T19:20:45+02:00
Refs:   

fix(background): one action per game minute, not per turn (task-409)

Your correction: a decision is not an action. My previous commit gave the
background tier one action per TURN; the rule is one action per game MINUTE,
because a turn of T minutes holds T ticks and a tick is an action. At the camp's
1 min/turn the two are identical, which is why the measurement looked right and
the error stayed hidden until a 5 or 15 min/turn turn was involved.

  actions_per_turn(gs) = max(1, round(tick_minutes(gs)))

Kept deliberately separate from the LLM **decision** cadence, which is one per turn
per focused character and is what costs money: a deterministic character spends
its whole turn, an LLM decides once and the engine resolves the ticks. Conflating
those two is what produced both this mistake and the earlier one.

Consequence: 30 game minutes is 30 actions at 1, 5 or 15 min/turn - the invariant
that makes fast-forward and live play the same world. A fixed one-per-turn budget
would give 30 actions at 1 min/turn and 2 at 15.

Also: travel is NOT a stateful action, and I withdraw the proposal. task-131's
owner-approved deviation removed the old synchronous multi-tick rest() loop
precisely because blocking multi-tick processes were the wrong shape, and
player.activity is documented as purely descriptive; task-352 lists go as a
plain action. Travel is a sequence of moves, one room per tick, up to the turn's
ticks.

Tests rewritten to the real invariant: 30 game minutes -> 30 actions at 1, 5 and
15 min/turn, driven through process_due with the character kept available. The
span has to divide evenly by the turn length (20 minutes is 1.33 turns at 15),
which the first version of the test got wrong. 3186 pass, 0 fail.

commit bdbfdad0cf7e44d851653b7f758877836f36198f
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T19:17:29+02:00
Refs:   

fix(background): one action per turn, matching the live tier (task-409)

The background tier was on a different clock from everything else. It acted once
per DECISION_MINUTES (10 game minutes) of game time, so against a live character's
1-2 actions per turn:

  turn length   background   live
  1 minute      0.1          1-2
  5 minutes     0.5          1-2
  15 minutes    1.5          1-2

At the camp's 1 minute/turn the background tier acted TEN TIMES less often than
the player; at 15 minutes slightly more. The two agreed only around T=10, which is
why the 15-minute soaks looked consistent for the whole of this session - they
were the coincidence, not the rule. A turn is one round in which every character
acts, so the background tier now spends that turn the same way:

  ACTIONS_PER_TURN = 1, MAX_ACTIONS_PER_TICK = 1 (a guard, not a rate)

No banking: a character mid-activity or asleep neither decides nor accumulates, so
a long sleep cannot be dumped as a burst on waking. The
ext_due_tick deferral
now skips the character outright - adding the turn's action to a deferred credit
of 0 silently cancelled the deferral, which my first attempt did.

Measured, one game day at 1 min/turn, new vs old cadence:

  vital          new    old
  Hunger        43.3   45.3
  Thirst        33.5   35.3
  Energy        45.2   61.7   <- the extra agency buys more travel, which costs Energy
  Hygiene       56.7   56.7
  Social        51.3   45.9
  Sanity        82.4   77.3
  Entertainment 39.0   36.3
  23/23 alive; 7.9s vs 7.2s per game day (the extra actions are cheap and
  sleeping turns are skipped).

Tests rewritten to the new contract: the old ones asserted the credit model
(equal decisions per game MINUTE at any tick length, and credit banking), which is
now deliberately false. The new ones pin N turns -> N actions at 1, 5 and 15
min/turn, driven through process_due with the character kept available, because
over real turns activity durations legitimately differ in turns (eight hours of
sleep is 480 turns at 1 min/turn and 32 at 15).

Also corrects tools/add_workplace_facilities.py, whose docstring rested on the
wrong diagnosis and which records that the facilities pass is kept, unused and
measured-worse (it degraded even the schedule-less baseline: area tags feed
_areas_with for every need search, so tagging work areas redirected the whole
camp's travel). 3186 pass, 0 fail.

commit 361c5e3ef252847451060bf5b178584ea300ff90
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T18:09:55+02:00
Refs:   

docs(schedule): record two failed fixes and the real blocker - the action budget (task-409)

Neither attempted fix worked, and both were reverted rather than kept as
unproven complexity. The measurements matter more than the changes did.

1. Committed errands. Hypothesis: journeys are walked one hop per decision and
every routine need crossing restarts them, so nobody arrives. Added CRITICAL_*
levels and a guard letting a scheduled errand run to arrival unless a need is
critical. Result: 388 -> 381 travels, 53 -> 45 work blocks. No effect, so the
hypothesis was wrong and the change is gone.

2. Co-located facilities with work. Hypothesis: the work sites have no food,
water, latrine or wash, so every need is a cross-camp round trip. Tagged 11 work
areas latrine/water/recreation and placed 4 wash basins
(tools/add_workplace_facilities.py). It made things WORSE, including the
no-schedule baseline: Hygiene 69.2 -> 24.4 with schedules disabled and 48.8 ->
7.9 with them on, and two characters died of exhaustion. Reverted.

The lesson from (2) is worth more than the change: area tags are NOT neutral.
They are what _areas_with consults for every need search, so tagging work areas
`water` and `latrine` redirected where the whole camp travels for every need, not
just where workers drink. A co-location pass cannot be evaluated as a local data
tweak, and the tool is kept with that caveat documented rather than deleted.

THE BLOCKER IS ARITHMETIC. A background character takes one action per
DECISION_MINUTES (10 game minutes) - about 96 actions a day. Survival service is
not cheap in actions: each need is a journey of one hop per action, and the camp's
food, water, latrine and wash sit in different corners. Twelve 30-minute work
blocks cannot fit beside those errands, and once schedule travel competes for the
same budget the errands slow down too - which is exactly what the numbers show
(Hygiene and Entertainment collapse, schedule:work fires about once per character
per day).

So this is a design decision, and the task now records three options with (b)
recommended: (a) more actions per day, which multiplies tick CPU and makes the
character finer-grained when the goal is "supercharged simple NPCs"; (b) coarser
need service - one "chores" trip to a service area servicing several needs in one
action, which is what a coarse simulation should already do and collapses ~5
errands into 1; (c) drop the working day and keep schedules for placement only,
which is already measured as Energy-positive.

Engine and scenario are unchanged from slice 1 (verified byte-identical). 76
targeted tests pass.

commit abec06e8f0ee0109da1708578d78667ab9cb2eda
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T18:02:25+02:00
Refs:   

feat(schedule): authored daily schedules and the planner (task-409 slice 1)

Turns the survival loop into a *day*. Before this every background character did
the same thing: eat, drink, sleep, wash and talk wherever it happened to be
standing, and moved only when a need pushed it somewhere. A schedule says Mikka
goes to the Workshop, Gribba cooks, Vekka scouts, and the camp gathers at the
Chief's Pit in the evening and sleeps in the halls.

Engine (engine/schedule.py, tests/test_schedule.py - 39 tests):

- Steps are {start: "HH:MM", activity, area, fallback}, sorted, wrapping at
  midnight; before the first step the LAST step stays in force, so a day starting
  at 06:00 does not leave anyone idle or wandering from midnight to dawn.
- Malformed steps are dropped, not guessed: a step with no usable start cannot be
  placed in a day, and inventing one would make a character behave in a way nobody
  authored. An unknown activity degrades to `wait` rather than freeing the
  character to wander. An empty schedule is a valid state - exactly how every
  character behaved before this task.
- minutes_of_day reads the engine's own clock (total_game_minutes), so a step at
  07:00 is 07:00 at any tick length. normalize also runs on load, so a
  hand-edited or legacy save cannot put a bad step into somebody's day.
- _pursue_schedule walks to the step's area - _target_step gains an explicit
  `areas` override for a named destination - then starts a short `working` block.
  It runs after every survival need and before boredom, so a character works with
  time no need claims and still breaks off to eat.
- `working` is registered in all five activity registries INCLUDING
  ACTIVITY_INTERRUPTIBLE, the omission that once made `conversing` never expire.
  The block is short (WORK_MINUTES = 30) on purpose: _act skips anyone
  mid-activity, so the duration is the longest a character can go without eating
  or relieving itself.
- tools/add_schedules.py authors schedules idempotently (dry-run default) from a
  per-role table. 16 of 23 characters have a role, derived from the area each
  already starts in; the 6 animals and the player get none deliberately.

THE DATA IS NOT SHIPPED, because measurement said not to. Authored, then measured
over 3 days at 15 min/tick, schedules on vs off, same seed:

  vitality        avg on   avg off    min on  min off
  Hygiene           48.8      69.2       0.0     45.0
  Social            40.3      61.6       0.0      4.0
  Entertainment     19.8      42.2       0.0      8.0
  Energy            72.3      55.4      40.0     31.0
  Hunger/Thirst/Sanity unchanged

Schedules work - traces show Thrazz at the Chief's Den working at 09:30, Mikka at
the Workshop, schedule:work firing - and Energy is BETTER with them, because the
night step puts characters in the Sleeping Halls so they sleep properly. But
Hygiene, Social and Entertainment collapse: 388 schedule-travels against 53 work
blocks. Schedule travel competes for the same action budget as the need ladder and
drags characters to work sites that hold no facilities, so the lower-priority
needs never get their turn - priority starvation via travel, not a broken
schedule.

So the schedule was reverted out of the scenario (verified byte-identical to HEAD
apart from the added key, and every destination a real area) rather than shipping
a regression to a world that was healthy this morning. task-409's Progress section
records the numbers and the order the evidence suggests: co-locate facilities with
work (tools/add_renewable_sources.py already places fixtures by tag, so it is a
data pass), then stop the ladder interrupting for non-pressing needs, then
re-measure and author the data with one idempotent command.

3184 pass, 0 fail.

commit 66c472d3ddd01b58e360a33243308b2689e65b32
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T17:49:44+02:00
Refs:   

fix(identity): identify entities by id, never by name; triage the in-progress tasks

Two things in one commit because the second is what surfaced the first.

IDENTITY BY ID (per the id-not-name convention)

Names are not identity: three characters can each know a "Fireball", and a camp
can hold two items called "Bag". Three pickers in static/js/inspector/agent-view.js
were keying on display names, which silently merged distinct entities and could
target the wrong one:

- _showAddKnownAbilityPicker compared worldState.getInventory() (which returns
  NAMES) against node IDS (`allItems.includes(id)`), so the graph branch never
  filtered and "Know" would add a duplicate `known` edge. The library branch
  matched by name. Both now key on id, and library entries use
  `properties.library_id` provenance (the key world-sync matches on first) rather
  than name matching.
- _showAddItemPicker had the same bug; fixed the same way.
- _showContainerPicker deduped containers by NAME, so two containers called "Bag"
  collapsed into one and `getNodeByIdentifier(name)` then resolved to whichever
  came first - "put in Bag" could target the wrong one. It now builds id/name
  pairs from getInventoryIds + equipped slots and dedups by id.

New `worldState.getInventoryIds(charName, edgeTypes)` is the id sibling of
getInventory, which stays name-based for its display-only callers. The remaining
name-keyed surfaces are recorded rather than changed: player.relationships[<name>]
(task-316 is the id-backed re-key; Player.id already exists for it),
getNodeByIdentifier (first-match-wins over id OR name), and the natural-language
command layer, which is name-based because it is text input.

DEFECTS FOUND WHILE TRIAGING

- static/js/inspector/agent-view.js: the picker bug above (real, user-visible).
- engine/item_actions.py: the item split left two dead facade wrappers that would
  have raised NameError - `_get_effective_weight` and `_sum_container_contents`
  called `_node_effective_weight` / `_sum_container_contents` from
  engine/items/carry_weight.py without importing them, and the second shadowed the
  module function's own name inside the method. No callers existed, which is why
  the suite stayed green. Both removed.

commit b3a46944464b25995dfb5d0b3cac357854c16d0b
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T17:11:27+02:00
Refs:   

fix(sanity): give Sanity sources and remove the self-locking condition drains (task-432)

Sanity had five drains and no source. A week emptied an authored 65-90 (the
baseline alone is 7.2/day), so every one of the 23 characters ended a soak
hallucinating - and it was not the Social coupling task-431 fixed: with Social and
Entertainment pinned at 100 for a whole week Sanity was still 0/0/0.

The self-lock was the worse half. hallucinating drained Sanity at -2/min and
social_breakdown at -1/min against a 0.005/min baseline, so -2/min was 400x the
baseline and -2880/day: a character who reached hallucinating could never climb
back out however well they lived, and low Sanity is what causes the condition.
Both drains are gone. The behavioural penalty is the cost - the same reasoning as
"low Sanity makes a character dangerous, not dead".

Sources, all reusing what the world already models (the chosen option) rather than
a new subsystem:

  sleep      0.025/min  ~12 over 8h   (the primary source)
  rest       0.05/min   ~3 over 60m
  meditate   0.05/min   (already existed; nothing ever started it)
  company    0.004/min while Social >= 70, the mirror of the isolation penalty

Sized against what Sanity actually loses in this camp: 7.2/day passive plus
ENV_DARK_SANITY (the goblin camp is a cave, up to 28.8/day). A first attempt at
0.10/min overshot and pegged every character at 100 within a week, which merely
makes the meter inert, so the rates stay under the drain.

The background tier gains a rest step, because sleep alone could not help
everybody: _tick_sleeping wakes a character the moment Energy is full BEFORE it
checks any duration, so sleep cannot serve anyone who is not exhausted, and a
character whose day costs little Energy never slept and never recovered its mind
- which is exactly who the five chronically-low characters were. _act now rests
for SANITY_REST_MINUTES = 60 when Sanity is low, a bounded block on purpose since
_act skips anyone mid-activity and a sprawling rest would stop them eating. An
earlier attempt that simply sent low-Sanity characters to sleep KILLED four of
them for that reason: full Energy woke them immediately, they looped, and starved.

Measured, one week, 23 background characters:

                       before   15 min/tick   1 min/tick
  Sanity avg              0        80.3          76.0
  Sanity min              0          13            23
  hallucinating       23 of 23    4 of 23       4 of 23
  alive                23/23       23/23         23/23

The same four at both tick lengths (Croak-Mother at Social 30, Silver-Talon 39,
Tusker 36, Old Iron-Back), so it is a property of the world rather than noise: the
sedentary, poorly-connected characters sit on the edge while nineteen campmates
are fine. Not darkness, which was the first hypothesis - they are in the LIT Camp
Entrance.

16 tests in tests/test_sanity_sources.py, including that the conditions no longer
drain Sanity, that a night beats the passive drain without pegging the camp, that
the company gain stays below the baseline (a steadying influence, not a source),
and that the rest block is bounded and in game minutes. 3139 pass, 0 fail.

commit f99e556c0e64036e1f0d4c5e2a35c124248d3564
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T16:48:39+02:00
Refs:   

fix(npc): authored behaviour intervals are game minutes, not ticks (task-428)

behaviors[].interval and npc_action_interval were raw tick counts, so their
meaning depended on world.time_per_tick_minutes: at 15 min/tick an authored
'every 5' fired every 75 game minutes, and a legacy wanderer moved once every 45
minutes instead of every 3. Every other time-measuring thing in the engine
(vital decay, condition durations, temperature drift, background action credit,
plant growth, the social cooldown) is in game units and scales with the tick;
this was the last one that did not.

_interval_ticks(gs, minutes) converts at evaluation, quantised to the nearest
tick with a floor of one - a tick is the smallest step the scheduler has, so at
15 min/tick an authored '20 minutes' becomes one tick. At the default 1 min/tick
an authored value's meaning is unchanged, so nothing in existing content shifts,
and nothing in the shipped data uses either field anyway (rg finds no authored
'interval' or 'npc_action_interval' in data/ or world_template.json) - which is
exactly why the divergence went unnoticed.

11 tests pin the unit, the floor (a short interval must not become 'never'), and
the junk fallback. 3123 pass, 0 fail.

commit 4cb646f5901ca215f0206678c21881ca285df898
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T16:44:03+02:00
Refs:   

fix(novelty): perception novelty never paid; bound it by a daily budget (task-434)

Fixing task-434 (meeting Entertainment double-paying with perception) uncovered
three further bugs, all in my own task-425 work, all fixed here. Entertainment
was 0, then pinned at 100; it now settles mid-range at both tick lengths.

1. Perception novelty never paid anything. _grant_arrival_entertainment called
   grant(), which RECOMPUTES freshness from the tick observe_area had just
   refreshed - so it measured 0 for every subject on every arrival. Task-425's
   tests checked grant and observe_area separately and never the composition,
   which is exactly how it hid. New grant_freshness pays the value observe_area
   reported, and its docstring says why.

2. The recovery window was measured in the wrong unit. freshness compared a tick
   delta against a window authored in GAME MINUTES, so the "2 hour" window was 2
   hours only at 1 min/tick; at 15 min/tick it was 30 hours. It showed up as
   Entertainment 59 at 15 min/tick vs 89 at 1 min/tick. Player.minutes_per_tick is
   now kept in step by the tick loop and the window is derived in ticks.

3. The window guards bouncing but not roaming, so it was never the farm guard.
   With 31 areas a character revisits a given one only after ~5 hours, so every
   arrival is a fresh subject and pays - a fresh subject per action against
   43/day of decay is unbounded. Measured with only the area subject paying and
   social switched off, one day still pinned Entertainment at 81 average.
   NOVELTY_DAILY_BUDGET = 30 now bounds it, deliberately below the daily decay so
   novelty is a top-up and the authored recreational fixtures hold the meter up.
   This is task-425's own "diminishing returns within a day" option, which the
   measurement shows is needed ALONGSIDE the window, not instead of it.

Also: people came off the arrival path. Paying for characters on sight saturated
the meter (a crowded camp re-observes 5-10 people per arrival, all going stale
within the window): 87 avg / 100 max with the fixtures never firing. observe_area
now records the area and its items only; a person is claimed by
register_first_meeting, which is what pays for meeting them. And a conversation is
now Social rather than Entertainment in the action tables - only joke/tease/flirt
pay amusement, and only a little.

task-434 proper: Player.node_id_for is the single definition of the character
node-id convention, Player.node_id derives from it (so a reload cannot go stale -
the deserializer builds Players without going through add_player), and
PlayerManager.get_player_node_id delegates. _grant_meeting_entertainment goes
through the novelty curve keyed on that id and both READS and RECORDS the
observation, which is what makes it idempotent in either order; the flat +10 and
its duplicate trait logic are gone. My first attempt only read, and the test
caught it: meeting first then perceiving would still have paid twice.

Result, one week, 23 background characters, 23/23 alive:

  Entertainment   49.1 (40-68) @1min   50.1 (29-70) @15min
  Social          81.4                  81.4
  Hygiene         72.4                  74.3

Perf also improved (86-105 ticks/s vs 34-58) because perception observes fewer
subjects. 3112 pass, 0 fail. Records the corrections in task-425's outcome (which
is in done/ and needed amending rather than leaving), task-403's progress note,
and closes task-434.

docs(tasks): close task-417 and task-423, file task-434

Records the outcome of the background social pass, including a design
correction the measurements forced.

task-423 is done with task-417 absorbed into it: the co-presence gate (per area,
never globally; both parties background; deterministic greedy pairing by mutual
affinity) is a component of the action pass, not a separate deliverable - the
"symmetric meeting" task-417 specified was the placeholder task-423 replaces.
Task-416 was not needed: grouping ~23 characters by current_area in one pass is
cheaper than an index and has no cache to invalidate.

The blocking `conversing` activity specified in task-423 §4 is GONE. Its stated
purpose ("without this a character takes ~1,000 interactions a week") is already
served by the daily cap - measured at 6.2 social memories/character/day at
15 min/tick and 6.7 at 1 min/tick - while it broke the survival ladder at short
ticks:

  1 day, 1 min/tick   with activity   without
  Hygiene                  28.0        70.3
  Entertainment            11.3        38.1

A 10-game-minute conversation is 10 ticks at 1 min/tick, and a blocked character
cannot eat, sleep or wash. Worse, a conversation pays enough Entertainment that
the "seek amusement" need stops firing (needs:entertainment 68 -> 6 in a day), so
the camp slowly stopped looking after itself. At 15 min/tick the duration rounded
to ONE tick and never blocked a decision, which is why the damage was invisible
there and first appeared as an unexplained Hygiene 13.7 in a short-tick soak.

The bound is now the cap, and a SOCIAL_COOLDOWN_MINUTES = 90 game-minute cooldown
spreads the day's allowance instead of letting it burst. Both are tick-length
independent by construction and tested.

Also recorded: ACTIVITY_INTERRUPTIBLE is the set _tick calls
_maybe_end_by_duration for, so a type missing from it never expires and the
character is stuck `busy`. Adding `conversing` without it made every character
permanently busy and they died of exhaustion within two in-game days.
activities.py now documents that at the set (the dead `conversing` registrations
are removed with it).

Result, one week, 23 background characters, 23/23 alive at both tick lengths:
Social 78.9 / 78.6, Hygiene 79.3 / 75.4, Entertainment 44.7 / 46.1 at
1 and 15 min/tick. Social was 0 before task-431 and 37 with company-maintenance
alone; interactions are now what fills it.

Files task-434: _grant_meeting_entertainment still double-pays with task-425's
perception novelty, because it is called with a bare name while novelty keys by
subject node id and Player cannot resolve one. Invisible in a soak (the callers
are rendering paths that only fire for the attended player) but wrong in a human
session.

Two decisions the user made after the recon, both applied.

1. The bake was stale; re-bake from the engine defaults. Three of eight vitals
had drifted upward while five matched exactly, and the baked Social value
silently disabled a shipped mechanic: SOCIAL_COMPANY_GAIN 0.030 could never beat
a 0.050 baseline, so Social fell even in constant company.

tools/migrate_decay_rates.py --all rewrote 93 player records across 15 files.
That turned up a second, larger class of drift the camp probe had hidden:
thirteen scenarios baked the legacy `1` for every vital (the pre-2026-09
per-tick scale), which the per-minute engine now applies as -1/min - vitals
empty in about a hundred minutes. Fixed too.

value equals the canonical default (17 JSON files, all clean), because this is a
1200-line diff across scenarios that are not the one under test.

combat_pit.json and corsair.json had an empty or absent decay_rates, which

savegame AND freeze the values against future recalibration, which is strictly
worse than inheriting.

maintenance: it stops the rot. Interaction is the source (task-423). It was

Entertainment pinned at 100 for a whole week it is still 0/0/0 with all 23
hallucinating. Sanity is a meter with five drains and no source - the baseline
alone is 7.2/day, which empties an authored 65-90 in ~11 days. Filed as task-432
rather than fixed here.

commit 699d5c8074d5fc2b4bfc49dcc66dac2dc7b9587d
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T15:26:25+02:00

refactor(relationships): one mutation path for closeness (task-420)

Relationship state is a blunt per-pair closeness scalar, and every gameplay
writer touched the dict itself: combat, the foreground loop, first-meeting
registration, the `label` command. So no *cause* was recorded (a delta from a
beating looked identical to one from a shared meal, so nothing downstream could
explain a relationship), and the two fidelity tiers wrote the same field through
different code, which is how the value could jump at a promotion/demotion.

engine/relationships.py is now the only writer:

- apply_relationship_delta(player, other, delta, cause, tick, area_id) clamps in
  one place, stamps last_interaction_tick/interaction_count, and records the
  cause on the trace as why="social:<cause>" with delta={closeness, cause, with}.
- apply_symmetric_delta(a, b, ...) makes symmetry a property of the call, so a
  meeting cannot be applied to one side by accident; each side still gets its own
  trace entry, because each side's history is its own.
- closeness_band / band_at_least / BAND_LABELS lift the band ladder out of
  Player.get_relationship_nl, which now calls it. Band-gated rules (task-423's
  flirt/confide gates) can no longer disagree with the prompt's wording about
  where the boundaries are. band_at_least is the min-quality gate.
- ensure_relationship(player, other, tick) -> (record, created): creating a
  record is not the same as feeling something - no closeness, no novelty.

Refactored callers: player.update_relationship (cause `dialogue`),
register_first_meeting, felt_toward, get_relationship_nl, engine/combat.py
(cause `combat`, was an inline -30 - combat.py had no logger, so one is added
rather than silently swallowing), the `label` command (writes label, not
closeness: a declaration is not a measurement), and the felt-toward route.
engine/derive.py turned out to be a reader only and needed no change.

The doctrine is pinned by a test: test_relationships.py scans engine/, routes/
and player.py for `relationships[...] = ` and allows exactly two elsewhere, both
bulk imports in routes/player_ops.py where a payload replaces the store
(deserialization, not a mutation). 32 tests.

_KNOWN ISSUE_ left deliberately: _grant_meeting_entertainment is a second
Entertainment path for meeting someone and double-pays with task-425's
perception novelty. Folding it in needs the name -> character-node-id mapping
that task-423 establishes when it populates entity_ids, so it is recorded there
instead of half-fixed here. 3044 pass, 0 fail.

Also files task-431, found while probing why Social is 0 for every character in
a week soak: the camp bakes decay_rates Social 0.050 / Hygiene 0.050 /
Entertainment 0.070 against engine defaults of 0.020 / 0.020 / 0.030. Baked
Social 0.050 exceeds SOCIAL_COMPANY_GAIN 0.030, so Social falls even in constant
company (the sampled character had company 94 of 96 ticks) - and Sanity is
coupled to Social, so all 23 characters end the week in social_breakdown +
hallucinating. With --engine-decay, Social is 99.2/84/100. The task records both
readings of which side is wrong, since it is a balance decision, and notes that
either way the passive company gain currently dominates task-423's Social deltas.
task-423 also now records that task-417 is absorbed into it (the co-presence gate
is a component, not a separate deliverable) and that it is blocked on 431 only
for the Social balance, not for its relationship/memory/trace work.

commit ddf78f0613e909cfef1b7de175a921446f966cec
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T14:52:27+02:00
Refs:   

feat(gameplay): novelty recovery and recreation - Entertainment is no longer pinned at 0 (task-425)

Entertainment had no recurring source: an area paid +15 once ever, item discovery
paid +8 once ever, and ACTIVITY_REGEN has nothing recreational in it - so a
settled goblin's Entertainment decayed to 0 within a day and stayed there for the
rest of the week. A background week now ends at 40.1 avg / 25 min / 50 max at
15 min/tick and 39.3 / 24 / 50 at 1 min/tick, 23/23 alive. The two tick lengths
agreeing is the property that matters: the source is measured in game minutes.

engine/novelty.py is one curve for three subjects (place, thing, person):

    freshness = clamp((now - last_experienced) / recovery, 0, 1) ** 2
    bonus     = round(NOVELTY_MAX * freshness)

NOVELTY_MAX 15, curious x1.5, homebody 0, wanderlust recovers on half the window
(how the old "+3 on re-entry" survives). Config
entertainment.novelty_recovery_minutes, default 120.

The curve is SQUARED, not linear, and that is a fix rather than a flourish. A
linear ramp pays a little for any gap: a two-room bounce every 10 minutes earned
+1 a hop, 6/hour against Entertainment's 1.8/hour decay - the treadmill the
window was supposed to prevent, and task-425's "a window comparable to the decay
time" reasoning was wrong about it. Squaring makes a short gap worth nothing
(10 minutes of 120 pays 0, 40 minutes pays 2) while a real absence still pays in
full, and matches "rising toward +15" better: slow at first, then real.

Freshness is measured BEFORE the refresh, which is why observe_area computes and
returns it: an arrival both refreshes the observation and earns novelty, so
re-deriving the tick afterwards reports every arrival as already stale.

Item novelty is now paid on PERCEPTION rather than only on take/use. task-425's
table said "taking or using it" and the non-goal deferred perceptual novelty
"until the perception path can carry it" - that path now exists, and paying on
take would pay zero because entering the area already stamped the item. The
take/examine path stays as a fallback for items perception could not see: hidden
until examined, or taken out of a container.

Recreation is authored, not trickled: three fixtures (camp_drum, knucklebones,
story_fire) placed by tools/add_renewable_sources.py in Chief's Pit, Camp
Entrance and Cooking Area, with a _recreate step in the background tier that
gates on need. The gate is also the anti-spam - after using one, Entertainment
sits above the threshold for the better part of a day. The fixtures carry
`recreation`, never `food`/`drink`, or _consume_here would eat them.

Also fixes a real leak the fixture save exposed: loading a scenario observes
every character's starting area, and saving it back baked that into the file -
23 characters' worth of "you have been in Blackmarsh", ~60KB on the first save.
to_scenario_dict now strips runtime perception and the derived memory_index
(authored `manual` memories stay); to_dict, the savegame path, keeps everything.
The scenario's own 501-line diff is 3 fixtures plus pre-existing load/save edge
reordering, already seen in d4424fc.

visited_areas and discovered_items are NOT deleted: they still feed spatial
memory's known-route filter, the `adventurous` micro-modifier and three frontend
readers, and both are still written so those keep working. Retiring them is
task-430, which records the ordering hazard in the `adventurous` swap.

Tests: 23 new (test_novelty.py, test_background_recreation.py) plus a rewritten
discovery class on a real Player and a scenario-strip case; 3012 pass, 0 fail.

commit 873e1f4c482f03ea5a01e025b8c5f21851ccde7c
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T14:13:02+02:00
Refs:   

feat(memory): observation memories - one live record per subject (task-403 slice 1)

The prerequisite task-425 was blocked on: `entity_ids` populated (was 0/17) plus
a per-subject index so "have I seen this?" is not a scan over uncapped memories.

engine/observation.py records one *live* observation memory per subject - the
area the character stands in, each item it can see there, each character standing
there - carrying who/what/where/when and the subject id in `entity_ids`.
Perception is not re-implemented: the subject list comes from
engine.room_perception, the shared prompt/panel source of truth, and
get_edges_for_target already expands spatial edges so an item on a bush counts.
The area itself is recorded unconditionally (you know which room you are in with
the lights out) while its contents need light - can_perceive excludes dead,
unconscious and asleep, and honours darkvision.

Player.record_observation refreshes that memory IN PLACE rather than appending.
That is what keeps the store bounded by subjects instead of by visits, and it is
measured: 908 memories after a week at 1 min/tick vs 912 after a week at
15 min/tick - fifteen times the game time, the same count.

Player.memory_index (subject -> memory id) takes "which memory is about this
subject?" out of the memory list. has_seen / observation_tick /
supersede_observation read it; observation_memory falls back to a scan and
repairs the index, so it can never silently disagree with the store. Sightings
refresh and do not chain; superseded_by retires a belief that was replaced (the
bread was eaten) so recall stops surfacing it, and _trim_memories drops an
evicted subject from the index - forgetting re-enchants the world (task-425).

Wired on area entry in engine/movement.py, which the background tier also passes
through (_travel_toward swaps gs.active_player), and once per character at load:
nothing is observed without a move, so without that pass the starting area would
be the one place a character could never remember. add_memory now accepts
entity_ids/location/salience and returns the entry; the index, superseded_by and
an evicted subject's entry all round-trip and are rebuilt on load.

Cost: ~5% of a background week (alternating A/B with warm-up; cProfile agrees -
a first measurement of 43% was a cold-start artifact). Soak unchanged at 1 and
15 min/tick: 23/23 alive, vitals identical to baseline (Hunger 34.2, Hygiene
53.7). Tests: 14 in test_observation_memory.py plus round-trip and load-time
cases in test_serialization.py; 2983 pass, 0 fail.

Also aligns task-425's spec with the shape actually built, and records the
progress in task-403. Still open in 403: the AgentMind facade, preconceived
knowledge, need-driven retrieval, memory traits, memory decay.

feat(background): goblins now relieve themselves and wash - Hygiene was pinned at 0

Two needs the background tier ignored entirely. Bladder filled to 100 every ~4

background set ever restored Hygiene - the `bathing` activity exists (+1.5/min)
