commit 916884458fe9cab424ef8a58ad3d8b781d8f1181
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T13:03:46+02:00
Refs:   

feat(world): renewable sources - plants feed the camp, and foraging sees containers

A week soak of the goblin camp now ends 23/23 alive at 1, 5 and 15 min/tick, and
the food supply stabilises instead of collapsing (4 -> 50 items, hungriest
survivor 49 instead of 100). Before this, five of the six coarse-tick sweeps lost
characters to starvation.

The source pattern (task-410, now widened to renewable world sources):
- `data/library/items/bush_of_berries.json` was already there and broken three
  ways: it tagged ITSELF `food` (so a hungry character ate the bush and
  `_consume_here` deleted it), its spawn effect referenced `item_id: "b"` (not a
  real item), and it fired `on_examine` only - a one-shot, not a source. Rebuilt
  as an untagged standing item with `parameters.growth`, a `parameter_reached`
  gate at 100, a `contains_count` cap of 10, and `per: "minute"` so growth
  measures game time rather than ticks (100 growth was 100 minutes at 1 min/tick
  and 25 hours at 15).
- `tools/add_renewable_sources.py` places sources through the real library
  placement path, with deterministic ids, `--replace` to pick up library
  changes, and a dry run by default. Five bushes authored into the camp.

Three bugs surfaced on the way, all broader than plants:
- **`_find_consumable` only saw items with an `in` edge to the area**, so food in
  ANY container was unreachable and the background tier could starve beside a
  full store. It now traverses every spatial relation (in/on/under/behind/
  beside/at) plus one level of nesting, and `_areas_with` does the same - that is
  what decides which area a forager travels to, so without it nobody walks to the
  forest where the bushes are.
- **`_is_consumable`'s action fallback accepted `eat` OR `drink`**, so a hungry
  character ate a water skin and Hunger was satisfied. Intent is threaded from
  the need/consume kind now: food is not drink.
- **`_spawn_library_item_node` dropped `parameters`**, so any gauge-carrying item
  lost its counter at placement. It also accepts an explicit `node_id` now, so
  authored placement gets stable, re-runnable ids instead of name+timestamp+random.

Tests: tests/test_forage_reachability.py (9) - spatial relations, nesting, the
tag trap, intent, and travel targeting. Note the graph renames a node whose
id/name already exists, so those tests assert identity rather than id strings.
2951 passing.

commit f7bb7f9a4b35b1e91866f7819f128103affa49c5
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T11:35:55+02:00
Refs:   

feat(triggers): parameter_reached and contains_count conditions for gauge gating

task-410's plant needs to gate on a growth counter and on how much produce it
already holds. Neither was expressible: the only numeric gate was
`uses_reached`/`uses_above`, which keys on `uses` - and `uses` is remaining
charges, not a counter. The consume and crafting paths delete a node at
`uses <= 0`, and examine/stacking/carry-weight present `uses` as durability, so a
gauge that starts at 0 reads as a broken item and invites removal.

- `parameter_reached` - compares a gauge in the node's `parameters` dict
  (`key`/`parameter`, `value`, `op` defaulting to `gte`). Missing reads as 0, a
  non-numeric stored value fails safe rather than raising.
- `contains_count` - counts the items a container holds, optionally filtered by a
  name/id substring. `op: "lt"` is the produce cap ("has room for more").

Both registered in `trigger_validator.CONDITION_TYPES` (an unregistered type
would be stripped on save/reload) and documented in the condition cheat sheet and
the Rules Engine doc.

task-410 updated: its task-406 dependency is satisfied (standing-item on_tick
landed at tick_manager.py:745-757 and does not double-fire), the mechanism is
now expressible, and the tag trap is written down - the plant itself must carry
no food tags or a hungry character will eat the bush and `_consume_here` will
delete it. Put the food tags on the produce, stacked as `count: N`.

Tests: tests/test_gauge_trigger_conditions.py (9). 2942 passing.

commit ac1154ee3b5fe95418ade5e887feab67e4bfeb23
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-21T11:21:05+02:00
Refs:   

feat(engine): make the simulation scale to any tick length, plus relationship decay

Everything measured in game time now honours `time_per_tick_minutes`, which a
scenario or Engine Config can set to anything. Before this, decay, conditions,
temperature drift and background decisions all silently assumed a one-minute
tick, so a 15-minute world starved everyone 15x too slowly while the clock ran
15x faster - and nothing errored.

Time scaling:
- `vital_rates.change(..., minutes=)` and `tick_minutes(world)`; every per-minute
  effect in `tick_manager` routes through `TickManager._decay`. The baseline loop
  also drops its second accumulator (`_decay_accum`) and shares `change()`'s.
- Starvation grace and damage are counted in minutes, not ticks: a 15-minute world
  no longer stretches the 1-hour thirst grace into 15 hours.
- Condition durations and `periodic` drains are game minutes
  (`minutes_remaining` replaces `ticks_remaining`; nothing read it).
- Body-temperature drift/convergence scale, or a 15-minute tick spent 15 minutes
  taking cold-band damage per 1 minute of leaving the band.
- Background decisions use an action credit measured in game minutes instead of
  `DECISION_INTERVAL` ticks, so decisions-per-game-hour is now tick-length
  independent (measured 78.2/hr at 1 min vs 78.0/hr at 15). Plans/sleeps are
  activities that consume credit; a busy character banks nothing.
- `soak_sim.py` gains `--minutes-per-tick` and `--mature`; its `TICKS_PER_DAY` is
  derived rather than hardcoded, so the day/week projections stop lying.
- Verified: a week soak is 23/23 at 1, 2 and 5 min/tick and 21-22/23 at 15/30/60,
  where the residual deaths are the known finite-food shortage, not the scaling.

Relationships and memory:
- `Player.decay_relationships()` drifts closeness toward 0 once per game day,
  damped by familiarity, never crossing zero, never touching an authored label.
  `last_interaction_tick` was written in five places and read in none.
- `Player.add_memory` no longer hardcodes a 200-entry FIFO cap;
  `memory.max_per_character` defaults to unlimited and eviction spares
  `source: "manual"` so generated chatter cannot eat the authored backstory.
- `world.set_scenario_source(path)` names a world from its filename; all seven
  path-bearing assignments route through it and `_scenario_name` is initialised
  in `__init__`.
- Fixed: `npc_action_interval: null` (what saved data contains) made
  `time_ticks % None` raise, and `process_simple_npcs()` is unguarded in
  `tick_turn`, so it took the whole tick down. Guarded on both interval paths.

Camp scenario ships `mature_content: true` (the base template stays false).
Filed task-423: deterministic NPC-to-NPC social interactions, with the drift
model rather than a tier ladder, built on the relationship and memory work here.

Tests: +3 files (tick-time scaling, relationship decay, memory retention) and
updated pleasure/social suites pinned to a one-minute tick, since they assert
per-minute calibration. 2933 passing.

feat(scenario): make the graph the only copy - 28% smaller files, working map restore

A saved scenario carried `areas`, `rooms` and `graph.nodes` for the same world,
with `rooms` a byte-identical duplicate of `areas`. Every field was audited and
nothing in `areas` was unique: name/description/environment/floor already live on
the node, ambient_light/light_description are recomputed each tick, and `items`
was always empty (placement is the graph's `in` edges). to_scenario_dict() is now
graph-only - the goblin camp drops 455,567 -> 322,407 bytes (-28%) with identical
reload behaviour. The live /api/state payload keeps the projection, because ~20
frontend call sites read worldState.areas.

Also in this pass:
- `_scenario_name` round-trips again. Every save dropped it, so a restored file
  was "unnamed" - and since the name keys the local background-map cache, that
  alone made a saved map unloadable. An empty name is now omitted rather than
  written as "", which `data.get(...) or ...` reads as "unnamed".
- The Changes panel reads areas from graph nodes, exactly as it already did for
  items and ways; areas had simply never been migrated. Its fingerprint drops
  `exits`, which every written payload strips - a constant on the live side and a
  false "changed" for any older file still carrying a copy.
- The graph-background cache key is derived from the identity, so the two cannot
  disagree (the key honoured body.dataset.scenarioName; the identity did not).

kept, context window 179 -> 29 messages, errors surfaced, ghost spotlight fixed),
locked/blocked doors no longer out-soundproofing closed ones, and the tasks filed
for the attention / relationship / spatial-model work.

commit c15102484098fda8e4f94764e5d7a7c56d057c16
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-20T12:02:59+02:00
Refs:   

perf(engine): optimize simulation performance and implement LLM inspector

This commit implements several significant performance optimizations and adds a new developer tool for inspecting LLM interactions.

Key changes include:
- **Performance Optimizations**:
    - Fixed an $O(n^2)$ bottleneck in `GameLogger.record_turn_event` by implementing turn-based pruning and a 2,000-event buffer cap.
    - Optimized lighting calculations by using a per-tick recomputed stamp and switching to a `max()` brightness model instead of summation.
    - Improved graph mutation efficiency by implementing `retarget_edge` for in-place moves (e.g., equipping/unequipping) and optimizing edge unindexing to avoid full index rebuilds.
    - Resulted in a performance increase from ~17 ticks/s to ~181 ticks/s in long-horizon soak tests.
- **LLM Inspector (task-405)**:
    - Added a new UI panel and browser-side storage (`llm_raw_exchanges`) to capture and inspect raw HTTP request/response exchanges.
    - Implemented automatic redaction of sensitive authorization headers.
    - Added usage tracking (tokens, cost, reasoning) and error shape capture.
- **Engine & Data Improvements**:
    - Added `grant_memory` effect to allow triggers to inject memories into players.
    - Implemented "fire-once" logic for discovery triggers to prevent duplicate effects.
    - Provided a tool (`tools/fix_scenario_authoring.py`) to migrate legacy scenario data to the modern trigger/effect format.
- **Testing**:
    - Added `tests/test_perf_guards.py` to assert the structural integrity of hot paths (buffer bounds, index usage) to prevent future regressions.
    - Added `tests/test_camp_trigger_wiring.py` to verify scenario trigger integrity.

commit 961b2865463dc9e24b1254fde105e7ded8969850
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>

Refs:   

perf(engine): optimize trigger dispatch and graph lookups

Implement event-driven trigger indexing and edge caching to reduce
per-tick computational overhead. This addresses performance bottlenecks
identified during long-horizon simulations.

Key improvements:
- Added `_trigger_index` to `WorldGraph` to allow O(1) lookup of nodes
  owning specific trigger types, replacing full node sweeps in
  `_fire_turn_triggers` and `_fire_time_triggers`.
- Implemented edge indexing (`_edges_by_source`, `_edges_by_target`)
  to replace O(N) edge list scans with O(1) lookups.
- Introduced an exits cache in `AreaDescription` that invalidates on
  graph revision, preventing redundant exit rebuilding.
- Optimized `ExecutionMixin._execute_triggers` to fetch matching
  trigger edges before building the expensive template context.
- Added support for "standing item" ticks in `TickManager`, enabling
  `on_tick` triggers on items that are neither carried nor lit.
- Refactored `LightingSystem` to use a single pass over area edges
  and optimized character item lookups.
- Cleaned up scenario data by consolidating the Kraktooth goblin camp
  files and removing redundant generator byproducts.
- Updated documentation with a comprehensive roadmap of upcoming
  simulation, character, and gameplay tasks.

Introduce an objective character trace system and the architectural groundwork
for long-horizon background simulations. This enables the engine to
maintain a mechanical history of character actions and survival needs
without requiring active LLM processing for every agent.

Key changes:
- Added `engine/trace.py` to provide an append-only, bounded history of
  salient character events (actions, need crossings, and deaths).
- Implemented `engine/background_simulation.py` to support deterministic,
  scheduled survival logic for non-active characters.
- Updated `Player` to support `simulation_mode` (active vs. background)
  and `next_due_tick` for event-driven background processing.
- Integrated trace recording into `TickManager` for actions, vital
  threshold crossings, and death events.
- Enhanced `WorldSerializer` to persist trace logs and simulation states.
- Added design documentation for the reversibility contract, trace
  formats, and long-horizon simulation progress.
- Expanded `tools/soak_sim.py` with background simulation support,
  progress reporting, and enhanced reporting capabilities.
- Recalibrated `vital_rates.py` to ensure survival stability over
  extended in-game timescales.

commit 8fd273fe4310124b3257fd0d4bd160674da25407
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-19T03:32:55+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(engine): centralize vital decay logic and migrate to per-minute scaling

Introduce `vital_rates.py` as the single source of truth for all vital
dynamics, replacing hardcoded values in `Player`, `VirtualWorld`, and
various JSON data files. This change transitions the engine from a
per-tick integer-based model to a per-minute fractional model,
improving precision and consistency across the simulation.

Key changes:
- Created `vital_rates.py` containing `BASELINE_DECAY` and various
  environmental/trait-based modifiers.
- Updated `TickManager` to use the new `change()` utility for all
  vital adjustments, ensuring fractional increments are correctly
  handled via the accumulator.
- Refactored `ActivitySystem` to use per-minute regeneration rates
  (e.g., `resting` and `meditating`) instead of per-tick integers.
- Migrated `kraktooth_goblin_camp.json` and `world_template.json` to
  use the new calibrated decay rates.
- Updated test suites (`test_activities.py`, `test_social_company.py`)
  to reflect the new temporal scaling and fractional accumulation.
- Added `tools/migrate_decay_rates.py` to assist in data migration.

commit 1da9e36fe84d81f904e7995bad2f036f4193a789
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-19T03:17:45+02:00
Refs:   

fix(engine): recalibrate vital decay rates and fix sub-1/tick truncation

Adjust the decay rates for Hunger, Thirst, and Energy to reflect real-world
scaling (e.g., ~3 weeks for hunger, ~3 days for thirst).

The previous implementation used integer truncation which caused sub-1
decay rates to vanish every tick. The `TickManager` has been updated to
use a fractional accumulator to ensure sub-1 rates accrue correctly over
time.

Additionally:
- Tuned Hunger and Thirst grace/drain constants in `TickManager` to align
  with new baseline rates.
- Added `high_metabolism` trait to support faster decay for specific
  entities.
- Added `soak_sim.py` for simulating decay rate accumulation.

commit ccc1c655918db11ccdb124b869674f51e4f6aab4
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-09-08T18:44:17+02:00
Refs:   

fix(survival): teenagers were starving in 45 minutes with granola bars in their pockets

The mansion run had all four teenagers dead of "starvation and
dehydration and madness" by turn 47 -- while three of them carried
water_bottles and granola bars. The Butcher, meanwhile, was the only
character still alive at tick 6834. Three independent bugs, not one.

  1. Decay was 1/tick for every drive. At 1 tick = 1 in-game minute
     that's needing food every single minute. Hunger/Thirst maxed out
     in 15-25 ticks, before any character had a chance to act.
  2. Vitals were invisible to the agent. The prompt showed "Your
     stomach growls loudly" but never the numbers, never which item
     to eat, never the consequence of not eating. Across 160k lines
     of log there were 67 actual eat/drink outcomes, belonging to
     exactly two characters (Sammy and the Butcher).
  3. Sanity was draining HP and being listed as a cause of death.
     Going insane doesn't kill you -- it makes you more dangerous.

  survival: Hunger 1 -> 0.06/min, Thirst 1 -> 0.18/min (~3 weeks
  without food, ~3 days without water). Fractional accumulator so
  sub-1/tick rates actually accrue instead of vanishing under int().
  Death slope softened: grace period at max (60m Hunger / 30m Thirst)
  then 0.5/1.0 HP/tick instead of 1/2.

  maslow: Hunger/Thirst moodlets are now imperatives naming the exact
  carried item -- "EAT your granola_bar or FIND SOMETHING TO EAT NOW",
  "DRINK your water_bottle", threat-qualified so they don't
  contradict ThreatDetector. Plan prompt cites Maslow's hierarchy and
  requires the first step to satisfy the most urgent physiological
  need. Generic: driven by item tags and carried-item graph edges.

  sanity: removed the Sanity -> HP drain and "madness" from death
  causes. Added two mutually-exclusive Sanity-triggered conditions --
  paranoid (attack +1, defense -2, Sanity < 50) and hallucinating
  (attack +2, defense -3, perception/concentration/willpower auto-
  fails, Sanity < 25) -- wired in the tick loop next to
  social_breakdown. buildInsanityContext tiers rewritten as
  behavioral directives ("Attack first, ask questions never").
  Involuntary flavor added for both.

  monsters: slasher/undead traits now suppress Hunger/Thirst/Sleep
  moodlets and Maslow needs -- the engine already skips their vital
  decay, so the prompt shouldn't contradict that by telling the
  Butcher to eat first.

  Also: normalized mansion.json decay_rates keys to the Player
  convention (lowercase hunger/thirst were silently ignored by the
  tick loop, which reads capitalized Hunger/Thirst).

  Files: virtual_world_engine.py, player.py, engine/tick_manager.py,
  engine/player_conditions.py, data/scenarios/mansion.json,
  static/js/agent/prompt-builder/character-state.js,
  static/js/agent/plan-manager.js, static/js/agent/involuntary.js,
  CHANGELOG.md

  Verify: npm run lint clean; 233 condition/vital/tick/sanity tests
  pass; 2672 tests pass overall. The 56 test_mcp_*.py failures are
  pre-existing harness breakage ('function' object has no attribute
  'fn') and touch none of these files.

commit 7f56075f9621a55833c26bd35781c432d5286648

Refs:   

feat: rewire planner to PlanTracker, add observer turn-feed, name masking, and react phase overhaul

The planner was disconnected from the decide prompt after the PlanTracker migration - `buildPlanContext`, `hasPlan`, and the inspector all read the old `window.VW.agent._plans` store. All three now read `PlanTracker`, so the plan renders in the decide prompt on the same turn it's generated. `trackStep` matching was fixed twice: stop-words are filtered with a real list (the old `length > 2` filter passed "the"), and targeted actions must match a non-verb step word. `shouldReplan` now returns a reason string consumed by the crisis log, and `setPlan` records the turn clock instead of `time_ticks`.

The turn-feed was rewritten around structured entries (speech/whisper/action/emote/result/system/NPC/recall) with observer-concise as the default - whispers collapse to "X whispered", inner monologues and meta noise are filtered, and stranger names are masked. The react phase gets its own minimal system prompt (~6.2–8.1k → ~2.5–3.5k tokens) with a fresh 2-message conversation, and movement-aware react context replaces the stale "surroundings are unchanged" lie.

Name masking (`mask_name_in_text`) scrubs real names and aliases out of stranger appearance handles in both `area_description.py` and `scene_snapshot.py`. The taco_bell_date scenario was rebuilt to open cold (Elm Street, two strangers, zero shared history), and 7 new library tags (coziness, cozy, familiarity, home, mess, sadness, wonder) were added. 6 new PlanTracker unit tests and name-masking tests round out the suite.

## 1.3.0 - "Weather Eye & Wild Words"

**2633 passing tests**

### 🌦 Weather & Sky (engine)
- **Forecast engine** - authored/state-machine/random/hybrid modes, zero entries = no-op, GM override with countdown auto-revert
- **Calendar** - `game_day/month/year`, `calendar_config`, `set_game_time`/`set_game_date` effects
- **Moon phases** - 30-day cycle, outdoor night light bonus, stormy/foggy gating, blood_moon, **moonlight in area descriptions**
- **Wind** - 6 levels, heat propagation multiplier, wind chill with `wind_resistance%`, Energy drain on exterior moves, extinguishes lit items in gale+
- **Humidity** - 4 levels, effective_temp mod (hot/cold), Social sap, flooding Energy cost
- **Sunlight triggers** - `on_dawn/dusk/day/night/full_moon/blood_moon` (one-shot per day), `on_turn_start/end`
- **Trigger effects** - `set_time/date/weather`, `forecast_override`, `adjust_forecast`; extended `set_environment` with weather/wind/humidity/transparent + cycling

### 🌠 Sky Widget
- **Top-bar**: `🕐 09:40 · Jan Day 1 · 🌑 new moon · ☀️ clear · rain in 2h`
- **World Sky panel**: animated stage driven by engine state, time skips, weather override controls, forecast next-change indicator

### ✨ NL Editor power-up
- **Ghost-node canvas** - dashed translucent previews on the graph; update/delete tint live nodes
- **Selective apply** - per-op checkboxes, Apply Selected (n)
- **`populate_area(area_id, theme)`** - 9 theme packs, NPC + items + ambience in one call
- **Smart mechanic inference** - glowing crystal → light_source; roast chicken → eat action
- **Inline property tweaker** - ✎ expands editable JSON payload on any staged op
- **Selection-aware prompting** - auto-fills missing ids from graph selection
- **Lore/style injection** - scenario name, theme, world_lore, and selection into system prompt
- **`>` palette route** - Ctrl+K → `> make the tavern darker` → NL Editor runs it

### 🛠 Fixes
- **Batch route** - Apply now pushes ONE undo snapshot, not N
- `update_node` flat patch, `link_to_library` template_id, `connect_areas` direction props, `search_library_areas` description search - all fixed

### 🧪 Behind the scenes
- New test file: `test_graph_batch.py` (6 tests)
- `engine/weather_forecast.py`, `static/js/sky-scape.js`, `engine/effect_handlers/weather.py` - 3 new modules
- Suite: **2633 → 2633** (all green, no regressions)

## 1.2.0 - "Craft & Carry" (2026-08-31)

Items stopped being cardboard props. Uses, durability, weight, freshness, stacking, crafting,
teaching, auto-dressing, gated shortcuts - and a pile of the item/gameplay todo queue landed in
one pass, tested with **2615 passing**.

### 🧰 Items grew up

- **`max_uses` + weight reconciliation**: a half-eaten loaf weighs what's left
  (`base_weight × uses/max_uses`); infinite-use items stay static; `combine` merges two
  identical stacks (uses summed, clamped at max, source destroyed), `split <item>` divides one
  into parts - capacity re-checked, "stackable twins" ignore the auto-renamed copy suffix.
- **Armor & equipment wear on hit**: outermost armor/clothing decrements uses; at 0 it breaks -
  back to carrying + `on_break` trigger. **No raw numbers anywhere**: prompts read
  `[pristine/worn/battered/about to break/broken]`, the HTC chips and paperdoll modal show
  plain words, the item inspector has a durability bar.
- **`on_use_progressive`** trigger type - fires on every use; gate thresholds with the existing
  `uses_reached`/`uses_above` conditions.
- **Freshness**: `perishable` food decays per tick → `spoiled` (fires `on_spoil`), cooking -
  use fresh food on an oven/stove/heat source → `cooked`, decay halts. Examine and prompts say
  it in plain words: `(fresh)`, `(cooked)`, `(spoiled)`.

### 🗺 Gated paths & movement

- **`requires_item` on ways** - a bike lane needs a bike, a fly path needs an item tagged `fly`:
  visible-but-blocked without the gear, open with it. Prompt exit lines show `(needs: bike)`.
- **Over-encumbrance = one size larger**: ≥50% load bumps your effective size tier - narrow
  passages stop fitting.
- **Chain follow-ups (task-104)**: the dash→go chain generalized - `lead` → go/approach/
  release, `grab` → approach/release. One same-turn follow-up, agent-decided.

### ⚒ Crafting & knowledge

- **Full recipe system**: recipe graph nodes + `engine/crafting.py` - inputs (consumed or not),
  conditions (`state_equals`/`has_item`/`random_chance`/`skill_check`), outputs hydrated from the
  library, learning via `global` / `skill:<name>` / `item:<name>` / discover-on-first-craft,
  persisted `crafting_known`. Commands: `craft`/`make`. 🧪 Recipes panel in the agent inspector.
- **`teach <recipe | skill:NAME> to <character>`** - teacher must know it, student must be
  present; recipes transfer, skills +1.
- **`use N items on target`** - structured `amount` on use_on (`use 2 eggs on pan`) validates
  against tracked uses and consumes N; prompt shows the syntax.

### 🧍 Characters dress themselves

- **🤖 Auto-Dress from Interests** (Inventory tab): scans the item library for wearable pieces
  matching `interest_tags`, equips through the normal stacking rules, weather-aware
  (hot → skips heavy insulation, cold → wants it), idempotent by construction.
- **✨ Generate from Personality** (Bio tab): the character's LLM gets the full system tag list
  and answers with JSON/CSV tags → sets `interest_tags` in one click.
- **🌊 Simultaneous Mode (experimental)** - every autonomous character acts on its own
  countdown derived from Social/traits/Energy (high-Social acts more, exhausted slower).
  Chaos by design; sequential mode untouched; off by default.

### 👁 Senses & health

- **`scry` effect**: a frozen distant-area view (rendered description + ambient light + exits) -
  the shared observer path is untouched. Editor entry with area picker + lead-in/fail text.
- **`proximity_effect` items** (EMF-style): BFS room-distance readings on examine - sharp when
  they're right here, needle-jumps adjacent, faint blip beyond, prose only.
- **Context-aware vitals**: isolated-wording only when you're actually alone and quiet; company,
  addressed-to-you, noisy rooms, and visible food/drink all change the lines. Sanity is now a
  neutral stress curve - no more "the shadows seem to watch you" in a bright Taco Bell.
- **Encumbrance in the prompt + HTC** - natural language ("at the edge of your capacity"),
  never numbers.

### 💬 Input & feedback

- **Invalid-action auto-retry** (setting, off by default): one same-turn retry with the error
  fed back for rejected and engine-failed actions.
- **Appearance grammar guardrail**: third-person directives + few-shots, validator catching
  `you is`/`body is who` leaks, one silent repair pass, safe fallback - broken text never
  persists (it re-sends into every prompt forever).
- **Carried/worn prompt lines**: full untruncated description + allowed actions +
  (known / not yet examined) + durability + freshness.
- **Gear totals strip** in the Inventory: armor / best weapon / insulation / resistances as
  pills with "from: …" contributor tooltips - one place, no per-item duplication, same
  aggregation rules as the engine.

### 🧪 Trigger-effect templates

- **42 library items** - `template_<effect>` (e.g. `Template: Scry`), each with a wired
  `on_use` trigger demonstrating that effect; generator at `tools/gen_effect_templates.py`
  (re-run after new effect types, a coverage test guards it) + index in
  `docs/virtualWorld/Templates/trigger-effect-template-items.md`. Scenario-ending/room-spawning
  ones are loudly flagged ⚠️.

### 🎓 Help, tips & guided tours

- **❓ Help Center** (top bar or **F1**): a coach-tip system that fires *when you touch the
  thing* - welcome tip on first load, inspector tips per view type (area/item/way/agent),
  button-triggered tips for Settings, the Game menu, Overlays ▾, More ▾, Triggers, ▶ run,
  auto-dress, crafting, and a ⚠️ warning when Simultaneous Mode flips on.
- **Spotlights**: "Show me" physically highlights the UI element the tip describes (the card
  stays put; ESC/✕ clears).
- **Guided tours**: *First five minutes*, *Triggers & effects*, *Scenario workflow* - ordered
  chains with Next steps.
- **State**: tips remember themselves (localStorage per-id) with session re-shows; the index
  lists every tip with one-click replay and Reset all. No second system - tips point at the
  real UI, and the registry is one easy-to-extend array.

### 🛠 Fixes

- **⋯ More ▾ and Overlays ▾ dropdowns** were opening *invisibly* - the toolbar's
  `overflow-y:hidden` clipped them; now visible.
- **`take` puts everything in a hand first** (generic held; hands full → one item auto-stowed
  to carrying, then pick up, same turn; `two_handed` needs both hands; intrinsic abilities skip
  to carrying). New **`stow`** verb.
- **Saved worlds are graph-only** - no more redundant per-room `exits`/`exits_authoring` in
  scenario files (runtime `/api/state` payload still computes exits live).

### 🧰 Gotchas in this release

- **Restart your server** (`start.bat`) - engine + route changes need the fresh process; static
  JS/UI is reload-only.
- The **mature-tags → inspection reveal** discussion stays open: newer item mechanics
  (freshness/proximity/max_uses) aren't yet wired into the tag-reveal pattern in the item
  inspector - say the word and they get the same treatment.

perf(ui): optimize state update frequency and reduce api overhead

Implement throttling and debouncing mechanisms across the inspector,

and re-renders during high-frequency state updates.

  rebuilds to protect the expensive embedding search vector store.

- Disable automatic polling in `WorldState` while the agent loop is
  actively running to prevent redundant data streams and render
  cascades.

commit d81c44311b4bd18200ad17bed1db5268d156568a
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-08-28T17:02:05+02:00
Refs:   

feat(library): support self-contained character imports with embedded item materialization

Characters can now carry embedded item definitions in their equipped slots. When
importing a character, if an equipped entry contains properties and the referenced
node doesn't exist in the world, the item is materialized from the embedded copy.
This makes characters portable without requiring separate item files.

The inspector agent view now resolves equipped item node IDs into rich objects
with name, node_id, library_id, and properties for better display.

Added standalone_test.json character and event log exports.

commit 261322b49cb851dc8c8d14d504cfcbae7c2910b7
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-08-27T19:06:33+02:00
Refs:   

feat: add searchable item placement picker and graph config persistence

Adds a search-based placement target picker for items and characters,
backed by a new `/api/search/placement-targets` route. The picker
supports area, container, and character tabs with optional tab
restriction, and is wired into the create-modal item form, the
inspector item-view move section, and the library-browser character
placement flow.

Graph visualization settings (physics, item visibility, inhabited-area
filtering) are now persisted to and loaded from engine_config, with
toggle buttons saving their state via the settings API.

Also fixes BOM handling in JSON file reads across the codebase by
switching to `utf-8-sig` encoding, adds `remove_edges_for_node` to
sever dangling connection edges on equip/unequip/drop, and introduces
new library tags (anticipation, craving, disorientation, hydration,
theft) and clothing items.

Adds dev task doc for task-348 and an event log export.

commit a9ef625b25a984594388f6e8104a8a930ce85f12
Author: Tommy Slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-08-27T14:07:37+02:00
Refs:   

fix(triggers): consolidate duplicate condition evaluators into single leaf registry

Two near-complete parallel implementations of trigger-condition evaluation
existed: `ConditionTreeMixin._evaluate_conditions` (condition_tree.py) and
`EvaluationMixin._evaluate_trigger_condition` (evaluation.py), used by
movement.requires_open. They duplicated ~20 leaf types and already diverged
(e.g. random_chance always divided by 100 in evaluation.py vs heuristic
handling in condition_tree.py).

evaluation.py's duplicated leaves are removed; `_evaluate_trigger_condition`
now delegates to the shared leaf registry in condition_tree.py so both entry
points share one implementation. Also removes dead copy-pasted branches
(sound_heard/speech_matches re-implemented verbatim later in the same elif
chain).

Adds `test_random_chance_dual_path_consistency` asserting both call paths
return identical results for a fixture matrix of random_chance values (seeded).

Also replaces 5+ bare `except Exception: pass` blocks in tick_manager.py with
`except Exception as e:` + `logger.warning` including context (character name
/ node id / event id) so failures in vitals decay, trigger firing, item
burn-down, and death resolution no longer vanish as silent no-ops.

Adds dev task docs (task-343 through task-347, bugs 27-31), library tag data
files, autosave/embeddings data, and a log export.

commit 2ab8f29f802dc2c58b2a9adb5a06c58be91b9dae
Author: Tommy slaatbraaten <tommy.slaatbraaten@hotmail.com>
Date:   2026-08-26T16:43:24+02:00
Refs:   

refactor(engine): modularize trigger system, effects, and routes

This commit implements "Wave 1" of the modularization task (Task 314) to reduce the complexity of large monolithic files.

The following major refactorings were performed:
- `engine/trigger_system.py`: Split into a package `engine/triggers/` containing `behaviors.py`, `condition_tree.py`, `constants.py`, `effect_resolution.py`, `evaluation.py`, `execution.py`, `testing.py`, and `ui.py` using mixins.
- `engine/effects.py`: Refactored to use a registry of category-specific handlers located in `engine/effect_handlers/` (e.g., `vitals.py`, `memory.py`, `spawn.py`, etc.).
- `routes/action.py`: Extracted logic to `routes/action_handlers.py`.
- `routes/graph.py`: Extracted logic to `routes/graph_ops.py`.
- `routes/library_routes.py`: Extracted logic to `routes/library_ops.py`.
- `routes/players.py`: Extracted logic to `routes/player_ops.py`.
- `player.py`: Extracted condition definitions to `engine/player_conditions.py`.
- `static/js/inspector/`: Modularized `way-view.js` by extracting `agent-header.js`, `way-view-connections.js`, and `way-view-triggers.js`.

These changes improve maintainability and follow the project's goal of reducing file line counts for better developer ergonomics.
